# FPF Library Architectural Reference

**DocID:** `Library` within **FPF Library**.

**Architecture and connected use of the Library's pattern languages**

- **Author:** Anatoly Levenchuk, with AI-assisted development
- **Date:** 23 November 2026
- **Use:** Understand the Library's architecture, select the appropriate level of explanation and preserve the relations between contributions from different fields.
- **Full publications:** [FPF Core](FPF-Spec.md), the [domain-framework catalogue](DPFs/) and the publications linked below.

FPF Library gives people and AI agents a connected language for reasoning about work, constructing useful results and developing better ways to obtain them. As a methodology of open-ended development, it can serve as a canon for a shared culture of thinking and acting. Its common distinctions and general Methods meet specialized knowledge in domain frameworks. A reader can use one sufficient contribution directly or follow a larger question across several publications.

This Reference explains how that language is organized and why its boundaries matter. It develops the shared account across individual frameworks: what the larger wholes are, how their contributions connect, which alternatives explain the arrangement and what changes when a relied-on premise changes. The full patterns supply the Methods, cases and source grounds. For practical search and reading, use [Using FPF Library](USING-FPF.md); the [FPF Library overview](./) gives the short introduction.

## Library.Reference:1 - One connected language, several kinds of whole

The Library becomes useful when a question crosses boundaries that an isolated answer leaves unresolved. A sound model may be unusable with the available observations. A feasible design may lack an authorized commitment. A helpful AI answer may leave the person unable to make the next judgement independently. In each case, the missing contribution has to be identified and connected to the work that consumes it.

The common language makes these differences expressible. Core distinguishes a System, a Method, its description, performed Work, capability, evidence and authority. The domain languages explain what those distinctions require in particular practices. A mathematical consequence, a corporate decision and an acquired bodily skill therefore retain different construction and qualification conditions while participating in one undertaking.

FPF Library is an organized collection of related framework publications with this connected public account. Its unity of description comes from shared meanings, general Methods, explicit dependencies and developed returns between contributions. That does not establish one discipline above every field in the collection. A Library-wide explanation can be necessary even when the explained publications describe several independently constituted disciplines.

Within the Library, a Suite makes a stronger subject claim: it describes a composite discipline through constituent DPFs and their connections. Engineering, Management, Foundational Thinking and Human Capability Engineering each have an account of that whole. A DPF outside those Suites keeps its own subject; its Methods can supply contributions to the same work. Neither arrangement determines how many publications a particular task needs.

This distinction follows [FPF E.4's ecosystem architecture](FPF-Spec.md#e4---fpf-ecosystem-architecture-framework-families-products-and-dpf-suites) and [C.20's construction of a discipline (FPF C.20:4)](FPF-Spec.md#c204---solution---recover-the-whole-from-direct-construction). The requirement for a shared explanation, including alternatives, whole conditions and worked connections, comes from [FPF E.11.PFP:4.7](FPF-Spec.md#e11pfp47---explain-the-methods-at-each-selected-scale).

### Library.Reference:1.1 - Long mantras and connected reasoning

A language of patterns needs more than useful separate Methods. Its reader also needs to understand why particular questions belong together, how their answers constrain one another, which alternatives remain possible and what a changed premise reopens. A **long mantra** puts those questions in words a practitioner can use to recall the reasoning. To find out whether it improves recall after an interruption, compare how readers resume the intended work with and without it. The full explanation supplies their reasons, subject knowledge and limits. A calculation can use a mathematical formula at one of these steps.

For teaching, unfold the reasoning in an order that makes its dependencies clear. To introduce a method, the speaker can choose the starting point and show what a disciplinary answer makes possible at the next step. For practice in applying a given method, supply the choices the task assumes and leave the relevant operations to the learner. When the learning aim includes choosing the distinctions or constructing the route, leave those choices open. Learners may use a live question in a familiar project. Earlier work can show which decisions they made, with the help available then. Use a fresh situation when the aim includes transfer to one. A spoken account, a text with blanks or a table of general types and local answers can express the same reasoning. When a term changes the type of the thing being discussed, correct it at the inference or action it changes. A checklist can reveal a missing consideration. Test the proposed answers together. Could they all hold under the stated conditions and still leave the required result out of reach? An unsupported answer that matters directs the next inquiry.

In practice, begin where evidence or an available way of working gives you a foothold. Follow the dependencies that matter to the result. The same questions can be used again on a related subject, or revisited later on the same subject as conditions change. Keep an actual prerequisite with the operation that needs it. When the reasoning is familiar, give the recipient the conclusion or proposed action in the language of their work. Keep the general terms visible when you need to teach the distinctions or resolve a disagreement about them. [Reason about the types used to model a domain](#libraryreference621---reason-about-the-types-used-to-model-a-domain)

The system-thinking long mantra is one instance. FPF A.1.STM connects the system’s intended use at the time of operation to its construction, the Methods for obtaining it, the capabilities of its creators and their cooperation. When the required contribution depends on the joint functioning of a larger whole, establish how the system and the other parts produce that result together. A bounded question about use may already have a sufficient answer in an observation or accepted operating requirement. When identifying a project’s system-of-interest, establish the extended enterprise and its commitments to that product. The system-of-interest (SoI) is the product System that an extended enterprise is organized to create, change, sustain in use or retire. Agents acting for the participating organizations agree their objectives and commitments in light of that product's use. Organizational changes retain that reference while treating the creator as an engineering subject with its own requirements and local acceptance. An independent supplier can have its own product SoI: a lathe is the machine builder's product and a means for its customer to make parts. The receiving uses and agreed obligations establish how those purposes connect. These questions connect large areas of engineering and organizational knowledge. The mantra shows where material laws, production techniques and cooperation arrangements are needed and why the supplied answers must fit together. [Engineering.Reference:0.5](DPFs/Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/ENGINEERING-DPF-SUITE-REFERENCE.md#engineeringreference05---the-system-thinking-long-mantra-across-engineering-contributions) explains the connection across that Suite.

[B.5.PI:4.2.1](https://fpf.tools/B.5.PI) explains how to use a suitable mantra to look for consequential problems that are not yet felt. Choose it by the work and its needed result, apply the supplying Method's actual connections to a current or possible case, and turn an unsupported connection into an inquiry. A noticed difficulty can reconstruct those same grounds through examination of what the work should enable. STM develops that transition for a systemic project question; other inquiries retain their own subjects. Neither a prompt nor a blank answer alone establishes an actual problem.

Other developed accounts retain different subjects and justification orders. The following examples show full reasoning already developed in the Library.

| Connected question | What holds the reasoning together | Full account |
| --- | --- | --- |
| What can be inferred about this subject, for which cases, and which method can do the required work? | Identify the field's kinds and their criteria. State the relation and the rule that supports the inference. When an action is needed, give the method, its conditions and the evidence that would establish its effect. | [C.39.WA:4.3, a mantra for reasoning with models and methods](FPF-Spec.md#c39wa43---identify-the-relation-used-in-each-consequential-transition) and [F.0.2:4.6, semantic and methodological results](FPF-Spec.md#f0246---semantic-and-methodological-results). |
| How does a way of working reach the job that needs it? | Discover or develop a missing way, make it available, identify who needs the capability, select among alternatives if needed, set the applicable parameters, and establish readiness. A performed job, a delivered product and an effect in use can each be checked separately. Start where the current need arises. | [C.32.MWA:4, a mantra for following a method from a need to its use](FPF-Spec.md#c32mwa4---solution). |
| How can observations, choices and actions form a usable continuation of control? | The available information, conditional future responses, consequences, timing and means constrain the same continuation; a changed dependency determines what to revise. | FPF C.43 supplies the common Method and control mantra. CAD, OPS and STR provide the specialized constructions described below. |
| Which direction is worth committing to, and under what conditions? | The worthwhile contribution, possible ways of obtaining it, capabilities, resources and bounded commitments constrain one another; changed evidence can reopen the direction. | [STR.18](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/STRATEGY-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#str18---connect-strategic-reasoning--the-strategizing-mantra) gives the strategizing mantra. STR.Reference:4, STR.Reference:11 and STR.Reference:14 explain its place in the field. |
| What can the continuing operation deliver? | Start with the result under consideration, commitments already in force and the choices each party may make. Relate the required work, readiness and feasible order to shared capacity, financial means and people's working conditions. For an account of delivery, establish what happened and which outcomes met the terms. | [OPS.23](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/OPERATIONS-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#ops23---connect-operational-reasoning--the-operational-mantra) gives the operational mantra; OPS.Reference:8–OPS.Reference:11 explain its place among the operating Methods. |
| How can a changing body of knowledge serve work? | The receiving work determines what must be found, preserved, interpreted and delivered; an answer must retain enough of its grounds to be used and revised. | KCAE.Reference:4 in the Knowledge-Corpus Access Engineering Principles Framework. |
| How far does the customer’s order reach: one delivery, a repeatable offer, or service to customers choosing independently? | Recover what the provider must produce, how the permitted configurations obtain it, and which work has to fit together when the choices are exercised. | CUST.1 and CUST.Reference:3 in Customization and Productization Management, and MKT.11 in Marketing. |

The internal FPF Architectural Reference, FPF.Reference:16.10, explains how the common control construction relates the independently usable Core Methods. Here the question is how their results connect to the subject languages in this Library. Development is also a receiving use of the common control account. FPF C.43:4.8 explains how to choose an intervention from the attained repertoire, trace its effect through related Methods and provision, and use the resulting capability to form a further attainable problem. C.40.OD supplies the whole development construction; C.40.DC constructs directions, C.40.JD connects the Method, developing agent and conditions of use, and C.40.NP develops the continuation after a result. They preserve development of knowledge and possibilities beyond correction of current performance.

A development recommender uses these contributions to form advice rather than merely rank learning materials. Apply PSDA.14 in the Decision Design Principles Framework to obtain a grounded recommendation, and PSDA.15 to connect it to feasible observation and reconsideration. The actual choice, work and capability in receiving use determine what changed. Technical control, economic comparison and developmental recommendation can therefore share a connected reasoning Method while retaining their particular means and conclusions.

These accounts can meet in one undertaking without collapsing into one Method. A strategic decision can change the proposed system's use; an engineering limit can reopen a strategy; changing an offer can alter the knowledge and cooperation needed to fulfil it. Follow the shared subject or premise and bring its changed conditions to the reasoning that uses them. Shared words alone do not establish a dependency.

The teaching order makes a connection understandable one part at a time. Actual reasoning can begin at a later question, develop answers together, discover an incompatibility or return to an earlier assumption. The analogy with simultaneous equations captures this mutual constraint; the answers can be incompatible or non-unique. FPF A.22.CGUS develops the alternatives and conditions when that structure needs to be made explicit; its requirements for a formal claim are additional to using an ordinary reminder.

The general explanation of a mantra stays with the pattern or framework that supplies it. A Framework Architectural Reference connects the language's Methods; a Suite Reference explains the additional connections across its constituent fields; this Library Reference explains connections and boundaries across publications. README pages make the important entry visible. These levels should lead to the full reasoning rather than repeat several competing definitions or hide a missing Method behind a navigation link. FPF E.11.PFP:4.7.2 explains this publication arrangement.

#### Library.Reference:1.1.1 - Rhythmic coordination across different practices

Rhythmic performance, technical control and organizational work can all require one action to keep its timing while another varies. Identify the required timing relation and its reference. A specification states what should happen. A proposed schedule and the limits on the available means can establish whether that timing is feasible. For an account of an actual event, use evidence of its timing relative to the reference. When the task calls for a change, [C.43:4.5.1](FPF-Spec.md#c43451---coordinate-recurring-work-and-separately-timed-actions) connects preparation, execution and recovery to the timing that must be preserved.

In Embodied Rhythmics, [RHY.11:4.4.1](DPFs/EMBODIED-RHYTHMICS-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#rhy11441---keep-a-common-time-while-allowing-different-actions) explains how to vary the position of an accent while keeping a supporting pulse. [RHY.11:5.4](DPFs/EMBODIED-RHYTHMICS-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#rhy1154---move-an-accent-without-moving-the-pulse) shows this in a passage. An accent after the beat can be exactly where the score places it. Compare the intended placement with the observed timing of both the accent and the next pulse. A recording can establish which relation held while leaving its cause open. If a correction is needed, distinguish an accent that missed its intended place with the pulse intact from an action that also delayed the next pulse. RHY.10 helps distinguish phase, tempo, grouping and cue delivery. If a person needs to learn the coordination, RHY.12 connects practice to the intended performance. Establish what the person can do from their own performance with the support available to them.

In Control Architecture Design, CAD.2 specifies event order, behavior between updates and reset conditions; CAD.5 relates sampling phase, information age and observation gaps to the model used for control. CAD.4 checks their shared implementation resources. A changed event time can change which information a command uses, so it returns to these constructions and the dependent feasibility or error calculation. The correspondence with rhythmics concerns the required temporal relations and their construction, not an imported model of human timing.

In Operations Management, OPS.13:4.5 selects periodic review, an event signal or their combination from the time and work needed for a useful response. OPS.10.2 supplies joint resource occupations, preparation and recovery, and the revision of the remaining schedule. An exception may need action before the next regular review; providing that response must also account for the work it interrupts and what must continue afterwards. The common choice is which temporal relations to preserve and which to change.

RHY can specify a rhythmic variation, assess what performers did or help them realize a passage. CAD explains how timing affects the information a controller uses and the response it can produce. OPS connects response times to workable commitments and the capacity to meet them. A timing relation found in one field can suggest what to examine in another. Each field supplies the means and evidence for its own conclusion.

### Library.Reference:1.2 - Holonic thinking across different kinds of whole

Holonic thinking connects a whole with the parts and relations that constitute it, and with its possible contribution to a larger whole. It helps answer a practical question: **what must be connected, retained or changed for the whole to do what the work requires?** System thinking is one important application. The same question also arises when constructing a method, understanding performed work, assembling knowledge or developing a discipline.

Begin with the recipient's question. It may concern the whole, a constituent or a relation between them. An observation or an established rule can sometimes answer it directly. When the question concerns construction, or its answer depends on composition, follow the relevant relations in the direction the conclusion requires. Try keeping a part fixed while changing its integration or use. Try keeping a reported result of the whole fixed while changing the organization of its parts. A change in the action or explanation the recipient needs tells you what the model must preserve. A requirement on the whole can guide the choice of parts, and a limit on one part can constrain the whole. Follow the directions on which this commission depends.

The construction rule depends on the kind of whole. [FPF A.1](FPF-Spec.md#a1---recognize-a-whole-with-parts-uholon-and-admitted-holon-kinds) supplies the common holon criterion; the subject patterns explain what constitutes each kind.

| Whole being considered | What the holonic reasoning connects | Full contribution |
| --- | --- | --- |
| System | Constituents and their organization with functioning and use; the whole's required contribution with choices about its construction. | A.1 and A.1.STM, with the relevant system-composition Methods. |
| Method | Which larger Methods include this operation, and who else uses its result? What prerequisites, joins or alternatives determine the contribution it can make in each, and how can it be provided? | [B.1.5:4.1, recover the enclosing Methods](FPF-Spec.md#b1541---recover-parts-before-composition). Use A.3.1 to identify the Methods. [B.1.5.CM](FPF-Spec.md#b15cm---construct-and-qualify-a-composite-method) develops a particular composite Method when its design is wanted. B.1.5.RS follows a proposed replacement into its affected uses. |
| Performed Work | What larger work is being or was performed through this action? Who did what, when, and how did those actions combine? What can the recipient now establish about the whole or do to continue it? | [B.1.5.EW:4, recover the encompassing performance](FPF-Spec.md#b15ew4---solution). Use A.15.1 for the grounds that establish what actually occurred. C.32.MWA.PP helps arrange a next performance when its participants or resources must be coordinated. |
| Episteme | Which larger account, if any, incorporates this interpreted claim? What conclusion follows with the other premises, on what grounds, and in what form can the recipient use the resulting account? | [C.2.1:4.0.4, follow the claim into an argument](FPF-Spec.md#c21404---connect-the-claims-with-their-subject-and-grounds). B.1.3 develops the synthesis when several accounts must support a conclusion together. |
| Discipline | Where does this field's knowledge guide practice, and how can results from practice test, sustain or revise that knowledge? What does a wider field or an external user need from this connection? What will keep it working if requirements change? | [C.20:4.2, investigate field membership](FPF-Spec.md#c2042---direct-disciplinepartofrelation) and [C.20:4.4, maintain or change the coupled practice and knowledge](FPF-Spec.md#c2044---identity-continuity-and-change). |

B.1:4.4 develops the inference when composition matters. To derive a conclusion from constituents, establish how their relations preserve or change the property at the scale of the whole. For a design constraint, determine which configurations it permits and which choices it leaves the designer. A direct measurement of a whole can decide a threshold while leaving its internal arrangement unknown. Keep the distinction between what a proposed construction would do under its assumptions and what an actual assembly did.

When someone asks what has changed, first establish what they will use the answer for. To decide whether a whole continues, apply the identity condition that governs this subject to the change or occasion in question. A replacement can preserve identity. A terminating event under the constituting rule can end the whole even with the same participants. When you must choose or justify an identity criterion, compare what the proposed criteria would count as continuation. The same outcome can come from two differently organized wholes. A new outcome can come from the same whole. [B.2](FPF-Spec.md#b2---meta-holon-transition---whole-reidentification) explains how to tell these possibilities apart. In designing a future arrangement, the relevant inference runs from the needed result to a construction that would deliver it. Evidence that the construction actually came into operation answers a further question.

Sometimes the constituent, the whole and what supports a decision about them change at different times. Identify whose work or decision each finding can support. [B.3](FPF-Spec.md#b3---is-this-claim-supported-for-this-use--trust-and-assurance-calculus) connects evidence to the claim and conditions that matter for that use. When evidence from several parts must support one joint decision, explain why the premises hold together. For repeated adaptation, [B.4](FPF-Spec.md#b4---coordinate-repeated-adaptation-canonical-evolution-loop) asks how findings from use reach someone able to retain or change the arrangement. A recipient who needs only a decision on the present use can stop as soon as its grounds suffice.

The table illustrates applications of holonic thinking to different kinds of whole. A system's components, a Method's constituents and a Work occurrence's parts have different identity and composition conditions. Reasoning across them preserves those differences.

The distinction is especially useful between **methodological thinking** and reasoning about **performed work**. Methodological thinking asks how a way of doing is selected, constructed, combined or changed. Its holonic application concerns a Method whole. An account of performed work asks what actually happened and how its parts belong to that occurrence. A reusable laboratory Method can permit a retry; a particular test can contain an interruption and two attempts. The permitted branch and the performed attempts answer different questions. A five-minute log window alone identifies neither a submethod nor an admitted Work part. Plans and descriptions of future work retain their prospective meaning.

A live performance can show a feature of a reusable method without a written description. To rely on the demonstration, identify what the observer can see and which feature that observation warrants. A.3.1.MR recovers candidate methods when the way of working is uncertain. A.2.2 tests a different claim about what the observer can now do independently.

The connections between kinds make the shared account useful. Knowledge supplies grounds and limits for constructing or selecting a Method. Capable Systems perform Work that enacts Methods. Results of that Work can support claims used to revise a Method or a system design. The relevant discipline can organize this continuing relation between knowledge and practice. Each connection needs its own explanation: enactment, production, evidential use and parthood are different relations.

For example, a laboratory Method compares two readings and repeats a reading when their difference exceeds a tolerance. If the receiving scientific claim requires independent measurement paths, two readings from the same dependent path cannot supply that independence. The required conclusion changes the Method and the provision of apparatus. A better account of the Work history helps establish what was performed, but does not make the method or apparatus adequate by itself. The whole's requirement thus changes the parts through a specific reason.

To develop an explanation at this scale, use [C.39.WA](FPF-Spec.md#c39wa---develop-an-account-of-the-whole-subject). Recover the question and its subject, construct the relevant grounds of unity, and show how they govern the choice and change of parts. Connect worked cases to that explicit construction and vary them where its premises or branches differ. To establish what work would answer the question in the first place, or when a local result must answer an encompassing request, [C.39.QR](FPF-Spec.md#c39qr---return-results-to-the-original-question) supplies the return: identify the relation, justify the inference, compare what is now available with the original use and obtain the missing contribution. These Methods organize the reasoning; the subject patterns above supply the relevant conditions of composition and identity.

#### Library.Reference:1.2.1 - Related forms of thinking retain their questions

The holonic aspect of system thinking concerns Systems, the holonic aspect of methodological thinking concerns Methods, and the holonic aspect of reasoning about performed work concerns Work. These distinctions help explain the common contribution. The familiar names also have wider uses. A.1.STM connects the proposed system with its intended use, creators, Methods and cooperation; OPS.23 connects work with capacity, commitments, service, money and people's conditions.

Management and strategic reasoning connect several of these subjects. C.43 relates observations, choices and actions under the conditions of the same continuation. STR.18 relates worthwhile contributions, their obtaining ways, feasible means and commitments. Their holonic questions can concern systems, methods, work and knowledge together. “Strategic” and “operational” name different questions here; the words alone place neither the thinker nor the object at a particular system level.

Mathematical thinking also has several contributions. A proof account or theory can be considered as a connected whole of claims. A reusable proof Method can compose submethods. Mathematical thinking additionally constructs objects, operations, representations, witnesses and countermodels, and develops conjectures. A mathematical object that a claim concerns is different from the claim-bearing Episteme. [MATH.Reference:1–3](DPFs/Foundational%20Thinking%20DPF%20Suite/MATHEMATICAL-PRACTICE-DPF.md#mathreference1---problem-frame---construct-mathematics-for-the-question) develops that broader repertoire.

This distinction changes the next action. An adequate proof of existence may settle one mathematical question while a later use requires a way to obtain a witness. MATH.12 examines what construction the proof supplies; another mathematical contribution may be needed. A constructive account of mathematical work preserves the distinction between existence, a witness-producing operation and its execution; it need not exclude classical reasoning.

Synthesis connects what one contribution supplies with what another needs. The work is interdisciplinary when the shared result depends on contributions from distinct disciplines. For a transdisciplinary principle, state the relation or operation and the conditions under which it can be interpreted in different disciplines. To show its use within one field, identify the distinction, inference or action it guided there. To establish a new discipline, [FPF C.20:4.1](FPF-Spec.md#c2041---run-the-complete-recognition-test) asks how its knowledge and reusable practices form a whole that can persist through change.

For a proposed use in another discipline, name the decision the rule would allow and the condition on which it depends. Use the receiving practice to establish that condition for the requested conclusion. For example, when reusing an earlier inspection for an acceptance decision, establish which item and state it covered and what may have changed. If additional evidence bears on the criterion, take it into account. Once at least one situation fits the evidence, the receiver can conclude that the criterion is met when all possibilities still open satisfy it, and that it fails when all violate it. An archivist can report the date recorded for a photograph once it is established that one of the possible entries belongs to it and that all such entries give the same date in the relevant field. If the register might omit the photograph, whether it records a date remains open. A complete search that establishes the omission settles that the register supplies no date for this photograph. To cite a particular entry, the archivist must identify it. Using that date as the date of exposure requires grounds for interpreting it that way. The archive example explains how an inference about the surviving possibilities can carry to a different practice. To use the principle elsewhere, identify the possibilities the new evidence leaves open and the answer each would give to the receiving question. The five classes of principles can help an author find a missing assumption. A further claim that reuse saves time depends on performance in the receiving work. [FPF E.5.4:4](FPF-Spec.md#e544---solution--principletaxonomyguided-bias-audit)

#### Library.Reference:1.2.2 - Use and translate the terms through their subject

In introductory writing, “holonic thinking about systems, methods, performed work, knowledge and disciplines” names the subjects directly. A shorter adjective works when the surrounding explanation makes the subject and operation clear.

Existing usages can differ. English “systematic” can mean methodical organization; it is not a substitute for FPF's “system thinking.” Richmond's [Operational Thinking](https://thesystemsthinker.com/operational-thinking/) concerns how actual processes work and their causal organization. The French historical expression *pensée opératoire* concerns logical operations in [Piaget's account](https://www.unige.ch/piaget/piaget1947PIN_03). Neither expression should be translated into the Work-holon application merely from its word form.

[E.10's lexical guidance](FPF-Spec.md#e1099---thinking-terms-and-the-whole-being-considered) applies the distinction in authoring. Preserve the source meaning, make the intended subject and relation clear, and follow the method for the decision the reader will make. The next paragraph gives three such decisions. The shared explanation gives an entry into that work; recognition of a holonic question and assurance that a particular whole satisfies its conditions remain separate results.

When you compare two accounts, first ask what you need to decide. An instruction may already declare two names for one value. Recover a disputed meaning in its source with F.0.1. Use F.9 when the decision needs a correspondence between meanings with different interpretation bases. If it is whether a team followed the method in a particular job, recover the method and the actions performed (F.11). To teach the method through that job, show which choices change with the next case and why the operation still works (C.39.RO and F.16). If a passage or a working reply answers for one case or subclass and loses the more general argument, use [C.39.GA, Restore a Missing General Argument](FPF-Spec.md#c39ga---restore-a-missing-general-argument). It takes the reader from finding the first unsupported step to repairing and checking the explanation. Several worked pages may be needed to establish a result about one whole. Show how their conclusions hold together for the receiver’s decision.

For a comparison, name the entity judged or the alternatives compared. Two methods can be tested at the same site. To compare the methods, identify both methods and the observations about their performance. To judge an item tested there, identify the item and the measurements relevant to that claim. A.19.CPM can return that two alternatives are incomparable under the stated criteria. Start with the choice left to the recipient. One suitable option can finish an order for a workable method (C.38). C.11 governs a local choice. To return every efficient option, apply the dominance rule to the whole eligible domain and preserve the identities the recipient needs (C.18:4.4). When asked where a search looked, distinguish the regions tested from the niches retained. [A.0:4.3](FPF-Spec.md#a043---putting-the-glossary-to-use) shows how to choose the result. Use G.5 for method-family dispatch or to declare selected members on an established basis. An agreement can settle which editions the cohort is to use. Promising they will work together and reporting their actual use each require their own grounds. Use G.0:4.5.1 to design the basis of comparison when the commission leaves it open. To compare alternatives reproducibly, G.9:4.2 shows how to decide what belongs inside each one and what needs to remain common across them. The same component results can warrant different judgements about a service if the way its parts work together changes.

### Library.Reference:1.3 - A canon for shared human–AI culture

The Library articulates a culture of thinking and acting oriented toward the open-ended development of people, AI agents and their joint practice. It can serve as that culture’s **canon**: a common, revisable body of explanations, methods, worked cases and grounds for judgement. Participants can use this repertoire to choose and perform work, examine its results and develop better ways of continuing. The canon makes the shared practice recoverable and revisable across different situations.

People and AI agents can both sustain and renew this culture through their work. An AI contribution may be assistance with one operation or participation as a teammate responsible for an agreed contribution. The working arrangement determines who can perform, decide, check and change what. Shared use therefore combines different capabilities and responsibilities; each participant needs access to the contributions relevant to its work. No individual has to internalize the whole Library. [C.32.MWA.PP](FPF-Spec.md#c32mwapp---construct-and-compare-method-performance-and-provision-arrangements) explains how to construct and compare such arrangements.

Acquiring and sustaining the practice requires different means for different participants. People may need explanation, repeated practice, feedback and support for participation. AI agents need suitable capabilities, access to the relevant material, execution support and continuity of attention and task context. Giving an agent a text can make the method available. To establish use of that source, trace an instruction or rule from it to an operation, interpretation or choice in the agent's work. An execution record can show that an agent loaded an edition and separately establish that it performed the step the edition prescribes. To attribute that performance to use of the source, establish how its content supplied a premise, an operation or the selected branch. The same step might already have been fixed by the program. The resulting product can meet its commission whether the agent learned the rule there, already knew it or obtained the answer from a teammate. [C.36.RP and C.36.MC](#libraryreference917---how-does-a-useful-episode-become-a-renewable-cultural-contribution) connect the shared repertoire to these different ways of obtaining, reconstructing and renewing practice.

For example, a person and an AI teammate can use the same engineering methods while dividing model construction, criticism and decisions according to capability and authority. A changed requirement calls for recovering the affected assumptions and revising their connected work. The person may need to learn a missing judgement; the agent may need better access to evidence or a changed execution procedure. A useful result can then expose a further attainable problem or a better method. [The connected development account](#libraryreference91---open-ended-development-through-connected-contributions) explains that continuation and the distinctions between assistance, learning, cultural renewal and development. A publication supplies the canon; actual use, retention and renewal establish the culture in practice.

For the connected practical account, section [9.2](#libraryreference92---help-enablement-and-the-organization-of-professional-support) explains how to choose and organize help, and section [9.3](#libraryreference93---acquiring-sustaining-and-changing-a-shared-humanai-professional-culture) explains how people and AI acquire, sustain and renew their shared practice. Its [forecast case](#libraryreference9321---derive-the-next-contribution-from-the-result-and-its-conditions) shows how available conditions change the useful continuation.

## Library.Reference:2 - What the names refer to

The same word can name a field, a framework about it or a file. Keep the subject of the claim recoverable before relying on its name.

**FPF Library** names this publication collection; **FPF Core** names its transdisciplinary framework. The Core document retains `FPF` as its DocID, so an address such as `FPF C.39` identifies a Core pattern. Cite **FPF Library** for the collection; name the particular framework and pattern when the claim relies on them.

For one inquiry, a Core passage may supply a transdisciplinary relation, a DPF the professional conditions for using it, and an LPF a locally adopted choice. Follow the contribution of the particular passage. Local adoption can preserve every admissible option. If a local rule is said to narrow the professional model, check that at least one choice still fits, that every locally allowed choice preserves the professional commitments, and that an earlier choice is excluded for the same inquiry. B.5.MR gives the full comparison. A record of actual work can then establish which choice was made.

| Object | What it contributes to this architecture | What a change affects |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Domain | A subject area used to frame a question or organize material. | A revised boundary changes what the designation covers; it does not by itself constitute a discipline. |
| Discipline | A whole of knowledge and reusable practice, with actual constituent relations and a way those contributions constrain and revise one another. | Changes to knowledge, Methods or institutions can preserve the field when its essential connections and continuity conditions remain. |
| Principle framework | An account of recurring situations, problems, forces, Methods, cases, consequences and their relations, expressed as a pattern language. | A revised account can improve the description or change its field promise. |
| Framework edition | An identified body of framework claims about its subject under its reference scheme. In FPF terms, an episteme. | A change in the claims or effective reference scheme identifies another episteme; its continuity and compatibility with earlier editions require their own grounds. |
| DPF Suite | A larger domain framework describing a composite discipline through constituent DPFs and any justified whole-level patterns. | A constituent or connection changes the described whole only under the relevant discipline conditions; editorial inclusion alone supplies no parthood. |
| Product series | A continuing collection of editions under declared admission and reidentification rules. | Admission, retirement and continuity concern the publishing series, separately from the described discipline. |
| Publication carrier and access route | A file, site, rendering or service through which a reader obtains the content. | Availability, layout or retrieval can change while the described Method remains the same. |

These distinctions are developed in [FPF C.20:4.6](FPF-Spec.md#c2046---keep-neighboring-objects-with-their-subject-patterns), [FPF E.4:4.1](FPF-Spec.md#e441---place-support-units-and-adjacent-products) and [FPF E.4:4.2](FPF-Spec.md#e442---describe-a-composite-discipline-with-a-dpf-suite). They prevent a practical error: moving a file, giving a publication a name or admitting an edition cannot supply a missing methodological contribution.

A pattern is also distinct from the Method it explains. The text may help a reader perform, criticize or adapt a way of working. The occurrence of that work and its actual result need their own basis. [FPF A.3.1](FPF-Spec.md#a31---umethod-reusable-way-of-doing-with-explicit-applicability) and [FPF A.3.2](FPF-Spec.md#a32---umethoddescription-description-episteme-for-a-way-of-doing) retain that distinction; [FPF A.15.1](FPF-Spec.md#a151---uwork-dated-performed-work-occurrence) supplies the account of performed Work.

## Library.Reference:3 - Why general Methods belong in Core

[FPF Core](FPF-Spec.md) contains both common conceptual distinctions and developed transdisciplinary Methods. Its Kernel is the subset that defines universal meta-concepts and the rules for their meanings and relations. Core also explains how to investigate a question, construct and combine Methods, compare arrangements, qualify claims, make decisions and continue development.

The placement question turns on what an operation requires. Constructing a Method from interacting contributions, comparing its performance and provision, or deciding which questions a checklist must answer can apply across fields without importing one field's substantive mechanism. These general constructions belong with their shared conditions in Core. Repeated domain copies add a duty to keep the same general construction consistent.

For example, [FPF C.32.MWA.PP](FPF-Spec.md#c32mwapp---construct-and-compare-method-performance-and-provision-arrangements) constructs and compares complete performance and provision arrangements. [FPF A.15.8.MS](FPF-Spec.md#a158ms---configure-methodological-support-for-work) configures the methodological support that actual work needs. [FPF C.41 and its connected patterns](FPF-Spec.md#c41---select-checklist-questions-for-a-work-result) distinguish selecting checklist questions, making their reusable expression, fitting an occasion, obtaining grounded answers, reconciling shared uses and qualifying the checking means. A filled checklist can support a decision about one delivery. To design questions for repeated use in a discipline, its practitioners identify which conditions should change their decision and explain how the questions disclose them. Core supplies a method for choosing questions. The discipline supplies the conditions and the grounds for the answers. The six Methods can be used separately whenever their inputs are already available. [FPF E.8.ML](FPF-Spec.md#e8ml---construct-a-pattern-language-for-connected-methods) explains the construction of a pattern language for connected Methods.

Wide applicability alone does not justify Core placement. Mathematical Modeling constructs correspondences, transformations and mathematical consequences under mathematical premises. Research Method Practice reconstructs practice and investigates competing accounts under inquiry-specific evidence conditions. Organization Change Engineering constructs contribution, information, assignment and authority arrangements in an organization whose work continues. The common idea of improving a method does not teach those specialized operations.

The architecture therefore keeps a general construction once and gives its specialized realization an explicit supplier. The test is whether the full operation and its conditions survive a change of subject, and what domain knowledge still has to be supplied. An abstract object, a broad title or use by many professions cannot settle that test. [FPF E.4.PFAD](FPF-Spec.md#e4pfad---principle-framework-architecture-decision) compares the actual contributions and their organization; [FPF E.4.CM](FPF-Spec.md#e4cm---develop-connected-methods-as-framework-contributions) distinguishes an already explained connection from a missing reusable Method.

## Library.Reference:4 - Why these Suites form useful wholes

A Suite describes a field whose knowledge and reusable practices form a larger whole. Its constituent languages remain usable independently, but the Suite also explains the consequential connections: what one contribution constrains, what result another consumes, how failure returns to an affected premise and which substitutions preserve the whole. The four Suites below cover selected portions of their fields, not every practice associated with their broad names. Their subjects and knowledge-practice connections, rather than the current number of files, establish the boundaries.

### Library.Reference:4.1 - Engineering connects knowledge to functioning systems

The [Engineering Suite](DPFs/Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/) is organized around designing, realizing, changing and sustaining functioning systems. This edition directly assembles Systems Engineering, Maintenance Engineering and Management, Knowledge-Corpus Access Engineering, Semantic Integration Engineering and Control Architecture Design. Management describes the organizational specialization, including OCE, and Human Capability Engineering describes the human specialization in §4.4. Their separate Suite editions retain their engineering subjects while making the corresponding specialized knowledge and practices available in full.

When several disciplines contribute to one engineering decision, establish how their results will be used together. To decide whether independently approved changes can proceed, identify what the allowed changes may vary, who can commit each change and which relations the common use depends on. Local evidence can cover the whole range if it establishes a feasible arrangement and shows that the needed relations hold under all admitted combinations. Where two arrangements fit the local accounts and require different decisions, include the missing connection in the model or obtain the condition that resolves the decision. [Engineering.Reference:0.5](https://fpf.tools/Engineering.Reference:0.5)

Direct constituents and specialized contributions can constrain the same realization. An attainable operator capability can change automation. A maintenance condition can change a design. A delayed observation or actuator limit can change the feasible control arrangement and reopen a system design choice; [CAD](DPFs/Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/CONTROL-ARCHITECTURE-DESIGN-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) connects those conditions. The meanings carried across an information interface must remain usable in the receiving operation. Organizational authority and protected practice time can determine whether a new way of working becomes possible while existing obligations continue. Qualification and later use can reopen the knowledge or design on which those arrangements depended.

Engineering includes discovering and reformulating the problem, not only executing a settled specification. A particular task can finish with an adequate analysis, design or justified no-change recommendation; a claim that a system actually changed requires that result. Similarly, HCD's engineering contribution concerns constructed and qualified capability development. The fact that all thinking and learning have physical bearers does not make every intellectual or learning activity engineering.

The [Engineering Reference](DPFs/Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/ENGINEERING-DPF-SUITE-REFERENCE.md) explains selected combinations and the external contributions they need. An asset valuation, a research finding or a management commitment can be indispensable without bringing its whole discipline into Engineering.

### Library.Reference:4.2 - Management connects direction to jointly supportable commitments

The [Management Suite](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/) concerns directing and sustaining joint activity through worthwhile direction, legitimate and feasible commitments, provision, performance and warranted reconsideration. Its fourteen constituent languages are Strategy, Goal Construction and Regulation, Operations Management, Organization Administration, Corporate Governance, Management Accounting, Corporate Finance, Marketing, Customization and Productization Management, Engineering Asset Management, Leadership, Community Management, Organization Change Engineering and Human Resource Management.

A customer promise constrains operating work. Capacity and existing obligations constrain the promise. [Management Accounting](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/MANAGEMENT-ACCOUNTING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#mareference11---form-the-economic-object-from-the-activity-and-the-question) connects the proposed work to its resource use and changing payments. To decide whether an order is affordable at its due date, [Corporate Finance](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/CORPORATE-FINANCE-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#fin2---assess-liquidity-and-funding-needs-by-date) follows the cash to the participant who must pay. A positive group balance can coexist with a shortfall at that participant if a transfer cannot be made in time. The applicable mandate and procedure determine who may act and what gives the act effect. Participation conditions determine which contributions can be relied on. A locally sound answer can therefore fail in the whole: the budget, schedule and promise may depend on the same unavailable resource or on an agreement that has not been made.

To construct a management arrangement, work back from what the recipient must be able to do to the decisions, rights and provision it requires. Then follow the proposed work forward under the choices its participants retain. A contribution can come from another enterprise, an authorized representative can act for a group, and one delivery can depend on several separate decisions. Choose which of these relations to model from the answer it must support. An agreed receipt can establish that delivery occurred. To explain how it was achieved, establish which relations operated on that occasion. [Management.Reference:3](https://fpf.tools/Management.Reference:3)

When an asset decision depends on several assets, establish which ones must function together to deliver the service and which compete for a budget, a crew or a period out of use. Those two groupings can differ. An assessment or recommendation about the resulting policy can reach its recipient before the governing body acts. To establish whether the corporation decided, identify the competent participants and the act that counts for this proposal. A certified outcome may establish the decision directly. Where a separate consent is required, establish whether that act covered it as well. A joint service and a joint decision each require an account of how the relevant contributions combine. [Asset and service decisions](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/ENGINEERING-ASSET-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#eamreference1---the-working-problem-and-practical-gain) · [Corporate governing acts](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/CORPORATE-GOVERNANCE-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#cgovreference1---problem-frame---governing-a-corporation-through-particular-acts)

Start with the result someone needs from the enterprise. Which rights and choices would a new undertaking grant, and which already govern work that is owed? Who can choose the time, method or terms? Which work must be done separately, and which result can serve several recipients? A feasible allocation may settle a known order. Where the agreement allows independent calls on one provider, the participants need a way to respond to all permitted combinations using the information and authority each has when they must act. A single performance may suffice if each recipient’s terms accept it and each can use its result on time. Apply the promised criterion over its eligible population and period. A single demand that no authorized response can meet defeats a guarantee for every such demand. For a minimum proportion guaranteed in each period, ruling out the undertaking requires showing that every permitted way of responding can fall short of that minimum or violate another mandatory condition. For a probability target, assess the risk under the permitted arrangements and compare it with the agreed threshold. A supported percentage remains usable even if an individual deadline prevents acceptance of the whole. If service is already owed, the same finding directs the manager to remedies. [Management.Reference:4.2](https://fpf.tools/Management.Reference:4.2) · [OPS.13:4.3](https://fpf.tools/OPS.13:4.3)

[Customization and Productization Management (CUST)](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/CUSTOMIZATION-AND-PRODUCTIZATION-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) connects decisions about client differences to the provision and continuing obligations they require. Practitioners may use a sufficient existing offering, retain a supported local answer, construct or change a repeatable offering, or decline the request. CUST.1 supplies the complete connected Method; CUST.2–CUST.8 can also be applied directly. Membership of a configuration in a family, composition of a delivery method and performance of a customer order answer different questions. To develop the offer, work back from what the customer may choose to the operations the provider must carry out and the inputs it can obtain from others. For a question about the outcome of one order, use the record or bound that settles it. To explain who produced the outcome or how, trace the work that made the difference in that order. Reuse a model of these relations across orders when its distinctions preserve the decision and its grounds. These management questions receive full engineering, implementation and accounting contributions without duplicating their supplying frameworks.


[Human Resource Management (HRM)](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/HUMAN-RESOURCE-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) connects actual work and participation terms through justified assessment and selection, reward, employee voice, protection and work transitions. Adequate output can coexist with conditions requiring preventive change. A substantiated recommendation can complete an assessment. To appoint the person or put the agreed work within reach, establish the authorized act or the actual provision respectively. HRM obtains qualified professional results for those decisions while Human Capability Development, Community Management, OCE, OPS and ADM retain their own contributions. The relation explains its Management membership without putting all people, culture or development work inside HR.

[Goal construction and regulation (GOALS)](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/GOAL-CONSTRUCTION-AND-REGULATION-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) connects the desired result, the reliance created by a goal, evidence, feasible means and reconsideration. Its professional practice includes acquiring and sustaining that connection. Use STR to develop direction and establish which commitments have been made, OPS to determine the actual operating conditions and available capacity, and LDR or HRM for participation and personnel decisions. Human capability development offers methods for learning, practice and assessment. Engineering offers methods for realizing and checking the systems and instruments on which execution depends. In choosing and pursuing the goal, distinguish what the participants can already rely on from what they still need to obtain.

The GOALS language develops these joins and the complete OKR application while keeping those professional contributions available at their owners. Its whole contribution belongs in Management because the undertaking must construct and reconsider those goals as part of its own direction, commitments, provision and performance. Changes in achievement evidence constrain the work; changed means or participation constrain the goals. [Management.Reference:3.4](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/MANAGEMENT-DPF-SUITE-REFERENCE.md#managementreference34---goals-are-part-of-the-management-arrangement) explains the common grounds, substitution condition and distinction from full external use. The complete GOALS language retains its direct human and AI applications outside collective management.

The composite contribution is a jointly supportable management arrangement, with a return from actual performance to the premise that needs reconsideration. The same construction accommodates a business, public service, temporary collaboration or voluntary community. Corporate Finance and Corporate Governance retain their corporate conditions; membership of the Suite does not impose those conditions on every undertaking.

Management specializes the construction and sustained functioning of organized joint work: authority, interests, commitments and resources have their own substantive conditions. The organization can be the immediate subject of a management change while the created or changed system and its use remain the grounds of the product undertaking. [Management.Reference:3.5](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/MANAGEMENT-DPF-SUITE-REFERENCE.md#managementreference35---keep-the-created-system-in-management-decisions) connects that project system-of-interest to the choice of local work, effective provision and reconsideration from use. A separate organization project retains its own result and closure while serving the product purpose.

[OCE.1](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/ORGANIZATION-CHANGE-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#oce1---identify-the-changed-organization-and-intended-contribution) identifies the organization and its contribution; OCE.3 develops the allocation of authority and means across organizational units, enterprises and their cooperation. A changed allocation or operating commitment can change the organization's state without redesigning its architecture. Management is therefore not separated from engineering by an alleged absence of system change. It also receives independently useful results from other disciplines. A voluntary population or network is not automatically one organization or capability holder; the relevant whole and authority must be established for that use.

Organization Change Engineering belongs directly to Management: organizational constructions constrain feasible commitments, and changed commitments can require another arrangement. Maintenance Engineering and Management belongs directly to Engineering: failure and condition knowledge, maintenance policy, provision and intervention connect to sustained functioning; later functioning can reopen them. Management practitioners can apply the full maintenance Methods themselves or obtain their results. This preserves the technical and management connections inside MNT without making its complete field an additional Management constituent. Each complete language has one home in its respective Suite.

### Library.Reference:4.3 - Foundational Thinking connects formal constructions and their interpretations

The [Foundational Thinking Suite](DPFs/Foundational%20Thinking%20DPF%20Suite/) connects Mathematical Thinking, Mathematical Modeling, Physical Thinking, Computational Thinking and Notational Engineering. These Methods can produce new objects, explanations, correspondences, algorithms and languages, as well as use existing ones. A subject difficulty can call for a new formal construction; a formal construction can open a new subject question.

A physical account constrains admissible model relations. Mathematical construction derives consequences. An algorithm must obtain the required answer within its resource and interaction conditions. Modelling connects that answer back to the subject question; notation keeps the distinctions operable and their changes recoverable. The whole makes a consequence jointly interpretable and revisable while retaining its separate mathematical, physical, computational and expressive grounds.

Start where the reader’s decision or explanation is still open. When a result has to hold across several levels of composition, establish how the relevant parts can be combined and which differences the models must preserve at each step. The same reader may need a conclusion for each part, a consequence of their interaction, or both. One narrower question is whether two fields in a record preserve a dependence. For example, a design file may allow a number and a release label in any combination, while the generating rule allows only some pairs. That comparison concerns allowed records. To draw a conclusion about the parts of a system working together, also establish how the parts produced or used those fields and how their connection affects that conclusion. An observation can settle a decision even when several histories fit it. If the record fits an admissible history and one available action meets the requirement on every history that fits, the recipient can establish that this action meets it. Whether to take, recommend or authorize the action depends on the entrusted decision. If a received pair is impossible under the reporting rule, examine its source, reading or governing model before acting on it. To explain which history occurred, connect the record to that process and retain any difference the explanation needs. [Choosing the relevant structures and model detail](https://fpf.tools/Foundational.Reference:3.9)

An observational mismatch can reopen an idealization; computing the same model more accurately does not repair that premise. A numerical error can require an algorithmic correction without invalidating the physical account. These different returns are part of the field's construction. The [Foundational Thinking Reference](DPFs/Foundational%20Thinking%20DPF%20Suite/FOUNDATIONAL-THINKING-DPF-SUITE-REFERENCE.md) explains such connections.

The name does not designate all thinking or a universal learning sequence. A pure mathematical result can be complete without a physical application. Explanation Design, Research Method Practice and Narrativization have other subjects and remain independent suppliers. Their use of mathematics or notation does not absorb their full fields into this Suite.

### Library.Reference:4.4 - Human Capability Engineering changes the human holder's capabilities

The [Human Capability Engineering Suite](DPFs/Human%20Capability%20Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/) concerns forming, changing, restoring and maintaining what a human holder can do. Human designates the target of the change, not necessarily its performer. A human teacher, an organization or suitable AI assistance may contribute; the resulting capability claim still concerns the person. This Suite connects the full Human Capability Development and Somatic Movement Modeling languages.

HCD connects later Work, the person's starting evidence, programme alternatives, practice, provision and qualification. SOM supplies the bodily distinctions, regulation and coordination needed for its movement-learning branch. Their results constrain one another: a programme cannot supply a missing coordination by listing it, and a local movement exercise does not establish performance of the whole task. A dancer may have adequate strength, a stationary axis and a remembered figure while lacking coordination during rotation. The two languages connect the missing bodily contribution to whole-task practice, variation, transfer and retention, rather than treating separate successful exercises as a complete result.

When preparing people for joint Work, establish what each role must do or decide and where another role’s contribution enables, constrains or changes that action. The same common result can sometimes be obtained by people acting independently. A claim that they learned to coordinate needs grounds for that interaction under the conditions of their later Work. See [HCD.22:4.2.1](https://fpf.tools/HCD.22:4.2.1).

This is a capability-engineering synthesis with limited present coverage. It does not claim all medicine, psychology, personality change or personal management, and it does not imply that these questions are reducible to teaching. When physiological, clinical, motivational or other mechanisms are necessary, their substantive Methods and qualified provision must be obtained. The current two DPFs are the available part of the field, not a reason to close its boundary around the files already written.

The qualification Capability distinguishes this target from the human–system interaction studied in ergonomics. A bounded capability change also differs from open-ended development. The [Human Capability Engineering Reference (HCE)](DPFs/Human%20Capability%20Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/HUMAN-CAPABILITY-ENGINEERING-DPF-SUITE-REFERENCE.md) explains the field, alternatives, available constructions and limits. An assisted answer can satisfy an immediate need without changing the person; acquiring a capability can finish while a wider open-ended development undertaking continues.

## Library.Reference:5 - Why some DPFs stand outside the Suites

A standalone DPF is an independently usable domain language whose own subject and useful results warrant its boundary, without a demonstrated constituent relation to one of the described assemblies in this Library edition.

The ten standalone languages have the following distinct contributions:

| DPF | Contribution that retains its own boundary |
| --- | --- |
| [Decision Design (PSDA)](DPFs/DECISION-DESIGN-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) | Constructing a decision situation, ends and possible means, alternatives, consequence and uncertainty accounts and warranted recommendations, together with the engagement, participation and facilitation that make them usable. |
| [Research Method Practice (RMP)](DPFs/RESEARCH-METHOD-PRACTICE-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) | Inquiry and criticized knowledge contributions, including source recovery, observation, research design and evidence limits. |
| [Explanation Design (EXD)](DPFs/EXPLANATION-DESIGN-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) | Designing explanations of warranted connections and assessing an existing explanation. Examine what grounds the connection and what the intended recipient could recover and use under stated conditions. Evidence of the recipient’s work can establish what they actually recovered. |
| [Narrativization and Narrative Studies (NSTD)](DPFs/Narrativization-and-Narrative-Studies-Principles-Framework.md) | Constructing and interpreting narratives: forming working accounts and fictional or hypothetical events, choosing purpose, viewpoint and presentation, and examining their use with recoverable source relations. |
| [Implementation Facilitation (IS)](DPFs/IMPLEMENTATION-FACILITATION-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) | Agreeing, constructing, adapting and continuing facilitation through which participants make an interpretable practice workable under actual conditions, including brokerage and their further facilitation capability. |
| [Financial Domain Modeling (FDM)](DPFs/FINANCIAL-DOMAIN-MODELING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) | Financial parties, positions, rights, obligations, events and flows with their domain meanings. |
| [Economic Reasoning and Coordination (ECO)](DPFs/ECONOMIC-REASONING-AND-COORDINATION-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) | Scarcity, incentives, exchange, commitments, institutions and coordination under economic conditions. |
| [Defense and Transmission Modeling (DTM)](DPFs/DEFENSE-AND-TRANSMISSION-MODELING-DPF.md) | How practices persist and spread, how they help or impair work, and how protective action changes those outcomes. |
| [Music and Dance Practice Engineering (MDPE)](DPFs/MUSIC-AND-DANCE-PRACTICE-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) | Musical and dance creation, performance, learning, transmission and cultural continuation, including engineered performing configurations and changes to their supporting environment. |
| [Embodied Rhythmics (RHY)](DPFs/EMBODIED-RHYTHMICS-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) | Performed rhythmic relations, their representation, transformation, coordination, practice and observation. |

Start with what the recipient must accomplish. Which part of that work can a discipline supply, and what would remain for the recipient to decide or construct? If the answer depends on how several contributions work together, establish their relation in the commissioned whole. Where composition matters, follow the part–whole relation for each kind of holon the question concerns. At each relevant scale, ask which possibilities the model must distinguish for the recipient’s conclusion.

For example, a company may commission tonight’s performance, an account of how the ensemble coordinated it, or a preparation method for groups in which the performers can make different choices. The same passage can be involved in all three. For performance, deliver what the participants agreed. To explain how they achieved it, trace which cues were available and how the players actually used them. To design the preparation, work back from the choices the performers keep during the passage. Give the rehearsal leader a way to find out what a new group can already do together, choose an exercise for a missing response and recognize when the group is ready. Design the exercise so that a partner can vary the cue, the performer can act on it and both can tell what happened. Show how to bring the response back into the whole passage under the changes of partner or phrasing that the brief allows. The designer can deliver that method before the next cast is named. When preparing an actual cast, its leader may find that a briefing or a check of the arrangement is enough. Within a performance, the question may concern the movement of one player or how the parts form a passage together. Within a preparation method, it may concern a cueing exercise or how several exercises and rehearsals prepare the same passage. At the same scale of the performance, a score can show the intended order while leaving the source of the cues or the choice of the next action open. Include the distinctions needed for the answer. [Frame the music or dance project](https://fpf.tools/MDPE.1:2.1) · [Choose the rhythmic unit and scale](https://fpf.tools/RHY.2:4.1) · [Design the practice](https://fpf.tools/RHY.12:4.1)

Use Economic Reasoning and Financial Domain Modeling together when a decision depends on both the use of a scarce resource and a financial right, obligation or position. Work back from the conclusion or action its recipient needs. The people who use a resource, the parties to a contract and the members of a reporting group can form different sets. To choose the boundaries of the model, hold the aggregate fixed and vary which party can decide, which claims can be exercised together or how the result is distributed. Keep the differences that change the required answer or its grounds. A direct rule or an available account can already settle the question.

For example, a group saving can coexist with a member who cannot make a payment by the due date. [ECO.1](https://fpf.tools/ECO.1:4.2) or [ECO.7](https://fpf.tools/ECO.7:4.3) can establish which use would be displaced and what supply can reach the proposed use. [FDM.2](https://fpf.tools/FDM.2:4.4) or [FDM.4](https://fpf.tools/FDM.4:4.4) can establish who can command funds in time or which obligation changed. A sufficient lower bound can settle the payment question. A decision about the effect on all participants may also need to account for who bears the displaced cost. Use the contributions on which the original decision depends.

Within Decision Design, construction of alternatives and professional engagement have different completion conditions. A sound comparison can exist while representation, consent or the conditions for useful advice remain unresolved. Conversely, a well-conducted engagement does not supply a missing mathematical or empirical argument. One language keeps these Methods independently selectable while explaining their necessary connections. A recommendation can complete an advisory engagement. Where the issuing body holds decision authority and its act makes the choice effective, that act can also settle the choice. Implementation of the decision and engineering of an operational decision service require their own work.

Narrativization helps an author decide whose experience and knowledge the reader can follow and in what order the reader can discover a connection. A scene may concern one participant while its sources concern several organizations and its conclusion concerns a practice shared across them. Choose what to show from what the reader must be able to understand or do. Establish the relation between those subjects before using the scene to explain the wider practice. This use of narrative in research, teaching or management leaves narrative work a discipline in its own right.

Several of these Methods can serve one commission. For an explanation of a past decision, distinguish what participants could know from what they actually used. A dated record may settle the question if it connects the relevant information to the decision. A request to design how decisions will be made across several services may also require a way of relating their decisions when each service can act on its own. Ask which differences between possible arrangements would change the recipient’s action or its grounds. [Research Method Practice](https://fpf.tools/RMP.1:4) can establish a missing premise. [Decision Design](https://fpf.tools/PSDA.3:4) can use that premise to compare the arrangements. To design a procedure, establish who is entitled to make each decision. Determine what they can learn before each choice and how those choices can combine. Return a rule they can use to meet the joint requirement, the conditions under which it works or grounds that rule out the required procedure. If feasibility still turns on an unknown condition, identify it. An existing rule can suffice. For a commission to advise on a choice, a warranted recommendation can complete the work. Where the recipient must put the practice to work, [Implementation Facilitation](https://fpf.tools/IS.1:4) helps identify what the recipient will do, what can be supplied by others and which contributions must work together. If a warranted answer already settles the commission, the recipient can use it directly.

Standalone placement is open to substantive revision. A newly established larger discipline could include a field under [FPF C.20](FPF-Spec.md#c20---composition-of-udiscipline-discipline-cal); a changed problem family could justify repartition.

## Library.Reference:6 - Several relations, not one hierarchy

The Library uses several structures at once. Their relations answer different questions.

| Relation | Architectural consequence | Example |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Discipline parthood | A constituent contributes to the continuing practice-and-knowledge assembly described by a Suite. | Maintenance knowledge and intervention practice participate in Engineering's account of sustained functioning. |
| Method composition | Constituent Methods together obtain the result of a larger Method under their joint conditions. | A way of answering a decision question can combine Methods for obtaining observations, interpreting them and comparing alternatives. |
| Specialization | An inherited construction is developed under additional subject conditions. | Somatic Movement Modeling gives bodily sensing and coordination constructions within human capability development. |
| Supplying use | One Method's result is consumed by another operation under stated conditions. | An engineering choice receives a financial constraint without absorbing Corporate Finance. |
| Framework dependency | A named use in one edition requires particular content supplied by another. | HCD uses Core's account of extractable structural information to judge what a reader can recover from instructional material. |
| Publication assembly | Selected complete descriptions are included with ways to find them and links back to their sources. | OCE has its full home in the Management Suite and MNT in the Engineering Suite; uses from elsewhere return to those same complete descriptions. |

Which publications need attention when a rule changes? Follow the claim to the work that needs it. In HCD the instructional-material assessment uses a particular edition of C.2.8 to judge which relations a reader can recover. A change to that definition calls for checking that assessment when adopting the change. To conclude that a person can perform the Method, HCD requires evidence about that person's work. Whether the change also bears on that conclusion depends on how the evidence was obtained. A guide can use Core directly, and a return link for a worked example can serve readers in the other direction. The permitted supply directions and the test for actual reliance are in [FPF E.5.3](FPF-Spec.md#e53---unidirectional-dependency-between-fpf-families) and [FPF E.4.PFR:3.4](FPF-Spec.md#e4pfr34---edition-and-package-discipline). See [HCD.Reference:13](DPFs/Human%20Capability%20Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/HUMAN-CAPABILITY-DEVELOPMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#hcdreference13---fpf-dependency-and-compatibility) for the supplying editions and uses.

Discipline parthood is established for the field, Method parthood for the way of working, and Work containment for performed activity. A project's participants and timetable do not establish any of these other relations.
