[FPF C.32.MWA](FPF-Spec.md#c32mwa---synthesize-an-architecture-account-of-methods-and-their-use) helps recover the Method, Work, capability and provision structures when they do not correspond one-for-one.

The directory tree supplies an unambiguous home for full descriptions. It does not turn all semantic relations into a tree. A claim of multiple disciplinary parthood needs grounds for each assembly; repeated usefulness is insufficient. Nor does a graph with several parents automatically form a mathematical lattice. A formal model requires its selected elements, relation and mathematical properties, as [FPF E.11.PFP:4.7](FPF-Spec.md#e11pfp47---explain-the-methods-at-each-selected-scale) explains.

OCE's organizational construction participates in the Management whole, a specialization of engineering. A management undertaking can also use MNT's full policy or programme Method while maintenance remains a constituent of the broader Engineering edition. The same agent can perform the maintenance Method in its management role. Corporate Finance can similarly supply a constraint to an engineering project without that use establishing its whole field as an Engineering constituent. The subject and the relation of the complete field to the whole decide composition; a useful connection alone does not.

The transformed object also differs from the performer and recipient. Replacing a teacher with an AI assistant can leave the human-learning target unchanged. Replacing the learner with a robot changes the target and can require different learning and control Methods. Methods with the same input and output kinds can require different operations for those targets. Replacing an instrument can change motor demands and attainable musical material; changing listeners or cultural selection can change what is retained for other reasons. The complete MDPE.20 account preserves these distinct mechanisms. The general constructions remain in Core; the bodies that depend on a particular target and mechanism remain with their substantive fields.

### Library.Reference:6.1 - Connect structures through the result they must obtain

A change can leave the organization chart intact while making its service unavailable. A controller can retain the same components while a faster command sequence defeats its limits. Use the connected account here when a proposed change crosses structures whose parts or relations do not correspond one-for-one. If one understood relation and its subject Method already settle the question, use that answer directly.

Begin with what must become possible, for whom and under which conditions. Work backward to the contributions that could obtain it, then forward through their connections to the receiving result. The backward inquiry identifies what the chosen arrangement requires. Another arrangement may supply the same result through different means. The forward inquiry asks whether the proposed contributions can work together. A list of available parts is insufficient when their inputs disagree, their results arrive too late or their combined demand exceeds a shared resource. A failed connection can require another constituent, another interface or provider, or a revised result agreed with its owner.

Several accounts can contribute to this reasoning. A **system composition** identifies constituents of a particular whole and the interactions through which it functions. A **functional account** groups contributions by what they do and relates the results they supply. A **Method composition** connects reusable ways of working under the whole Method's applicability and construction conditions. An account of **Work** concerns particular performances: what they constitute, what precedes or overlaps what, and what occupies the same means. The descriptions of those objects have their own structure. Their paragraphs, boxes and rows can be aligned without the described parts being identical.

Connect the accounts through the relation that carries the consequence. A tracking calculation can be allocated to a processor; the processor can be a constituent of a controller; an execution consumes processor time; the calculated command is used to act on a plant. Replacing the processor can preserve the calculation while invalidating its timing premise. Changing the meaning or permitted rate of commands can instead require a different calculation on the same processor. The needed change follows the premise and its dependents.

Composition makes a stronger claim than use or dependence. A constituent Method contributes to a reusable whole under its construction rule. Another Method can consume its result without becoming that whole's constituent. Likewise, a supplier can make delivery possible without being part of the receiving organization. Developing a Method changes a way of working; performing that development does not make it a higher constituent level of every Method it changes. Provision, development and delivery can nevertheless compete for the same person's time. Preserve that shared demand even when their Method boundaries remain separate.

Control adds another connection: information must support a choice that can influence the relevant action. In organizational work, identify who may change the commitment and how that choice reaches capable performers and available means. A reporting line, a feedback arrow and a relation of organizational parthood can coexist, but each supports a different inference. [FPF C.43](FPF-Spec.md#c43---connect-observation-choice-and-action-in-control) explains the observation–choice–action connection, including its organizational use.

A representation can omit visible detail while its established conventions retain the same model assertions. A two-box controller drawing may identify which component supplies a command while leaving timing to an associated specification. Adding the timing to the drawing can make the same model easier to read. If timing was not determined anywhere in the model, specifying it changes the model and needs its own grounds. Use [B.5.MR](FPF-Spec.md#b5mr---compare-models-by-refinement-and-abstraction) to compare what the models admit; the amount of displayed detail does not establish refinement or abstraction. Neither change by itself introduces another system constituent.

State what the account covers and what it asserts within that boundary. A temporal scale, update period or planning horizon can change the answer again: a slower update changes a timing condition, while a longer planning horizon changes the interval considered. Neither change by itself grants authority or establishes a containing system.

Bring the findings back to the decision about replacement. For one named use, trace the conditions and dependencies that determine its compatibility. To recommend a common replacement across several uses, account for each affected use in that decision. A shared argument can cover them together. If the old whole cannot carry the required behavior or composition claim, reconsider its boundary and identity. [FPF B.1.5.RS](FPF-Spec.md#b15rs---evaluate-a-method-replacement-in-its-encompassing-uses) supplies the constituent-replacement construction. [FPF C.30.ASV:4.5a](FPF-Spec.md#c30asv45a---select-structures-for-a-method-and-its-uses) helps choose the Method structures that can change the decision; [FPF C.32.MWA](FPF-Spec.md#c32mwa---synthesize-an-architecture-account-of-methods-and-their-use) develops their connected architecture account, alternatives and moved burdens. Where the unresolved question is how the Methods will be performed and supported together, [FPF C.32.MWA.PP](FPF-Spec.md#c32mwapp---construct-and-compare-method-performance-and-provision-arrangements) constructs and compares those arrangements.

These are different uses of one constructive return, not a universal calculation. A control design needs its error dynamics and physical limits; a service arrangement needs usable completion, qualified capacity and commitments. The two cases below show how their subject Methods determine different changes from the same whole-to-contributions reasoning. A construction explains what would hold under its premises. Actual systemhood, Method composition, performed Work, authority or disciplinary membership needs the corresponding subject grounds. In particular, a proposed larger discipline returns to [FPF C.20](FPF-Spec.md#c20---composition-of-udiscipline-discipline-cal) for its knowledge–practice couplings and continuing whole.

#### Library.Reference:6.1.1 - What did it contribute?

Start with what the recipient needs to decide. What would have to be true of the person, work, source or quantity for the answer to hold? A *contribution* can name the money transferred, the file delivered or the calculation performed. In “the review contributed to the release” the verb leaves open how they are connected. Recover that connection when the difference matters. An agreement can specify what a participant owes. Evidence of performance can establish what they did. Acceptance, use and an improvement in the recipient’s work each have conditions of their own. Keep ordinary wording when its context already settles the question. [Recovering the relation and its participants (A.6.P:4.2)](FPF-Spec.md#a6p42---state-the-direct-relation-participant-meanings-and-obtaining-condition)

In a model or record, read the declaration and the particular value together. What is named here, what does the entry say about it, and what follows for this use? A field can identify a contributor, describe a requirement or return the inputs to a calculation. A local name can also identify an assertion about several participants. The rule for that assertion tells you what must hold for it to be true. If the entry fits situations that call for different answers, state what the record establishes and what remains open. Follow a reference or seek further evidence when the decision requires that distinction. If the evidence is unavailable, give the conditional answers the record supports. When the same conclusion and its required grounds hold across the readings the record permits, the answer is already available. A submitted plan can already settle what was proposed. [Reading a field and the claim it represents (A.6.P:4.4)](FPF-Spec.md#a6p44---keep-world-side-declaration-assertion-designation-and-representation-objects-distinct) · [A named claim about actual work (A.12:5.3)](FPF-Spec.md#a1253---lathe-and-workpiece)

For a part of a working system, name the service or condition of the whole that matters. Trace how the part and its partners can achieve or maintain it. The approved design can identify an assigned function. Evidence from a particular occasion can show which path supplied the service, or just that the service was delivered. A reserve can preserve a capability without carrying the normal load. For a decision about withdrawal, ask which paths would still work under the specified conditions and by the required time. Select the actual change under consideration. A different bearer may perform the same Method. [Functional claims (A.6.F:4.1)](FPF-Spec.md#a6f41---trigger-rule) · [Engineering the joint functioning (SYSE.5:4.1.1)](DPFs/Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/SYSTEMS-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#syse5411---construct-the-joint-functioning)

When the question is whether a shared practice can continue, say what must recur, for whom and over which occasions. Work back from that result to the operations, people, material and permissions it requires. What can the next participants obtain in time? What does one occasion use up, preserve or make available for the next? Several people together can take over a former participant’s work. An existing agreement can cover the whole series or a single use. Giving someone credit, making their material available to a new group and arranging another performance each depend on the terms and evidence for that act. To report that the practice actually continued, establish the qualifying occurrences in the period claimed. To credit one group with passing it to another, trace what the recipients obtained from that source and used. [Continuing a community arrangement (CB.11:4)](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/COMMUNITY-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#cb114---solution) · [Assessing reproduction (MDPE.19:3.2)](DPFs/MUSIC-AND-DANCE-PRACTICE-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#mdpe1932---describe-the-arrangement-that-supports-reproduction)

Under this agreement the customer owes the review team a single fee of 100 exactly when it has received both the technical approval and the access approval by Friday. The two reviewers split that fee equally. The signed record identifies who approved each part and confirms timely receipt of both approvals. Each reviewer earned 50. If either required approval were missing at the deadline, the fee due to the team would fall by 100. Those two losses belong to different comparisons. If a substitute could supply the approval in time, include that possibility when judging what the named person's absence would change. The record still answers who signed. For a claim of help, establish whose undertaking the assistance was meant to support. For a claim of learning, establish what the reviewer became able to do and what shows the change from their earlier capability. To attribute that change to this review, trace how the episode affected their subsequent work. [Comparing feasible changes (C.11.CRC)](FPF-Spec.md#c11crc---configuration-relative-contribution-comparison) · [Help and joint work (§9.2)](#libraryreference92---help-enablement-and-the-organization-of-professional-support)

When one practice draws on another, first identify what the recipient needs from it. A reference may locate a source. The source may already contain an applicable rule, proof or finding, or explain how to obtain a result from facts still to be established. A report can convey a decision already made. Under a rule that makes an authorization effective on issue, duly issuing the document can itself bring the authorization into force. If a new performance or an authorized decision was commissioned, establish what fulfils that order and who can act. If the order was only to deliver a reference or an existing result, its delivery on the agreed terms can finish the work. [Using or requesting a result from another practice (A.15.9:4.1)](FPF-Spec.md#a15941---follow-the-nine-step-bounded-result-sequence)

A source can also suggest a way to construct something. Trace what its user took up to explain the origin of the idea. Judge the resulting construction by the conditions of its own use. A handbook may have supplied the criterion a reviewer used. To credit that use, establish how the criterion entered the review. To justify the decision today, establish which grounds suffice, individually or together. An independently sufficient argument can still warrant the same conclusion after an objection to the handbook. The question of what the reviewer would have decided at the time turns on the information and courses of action available then. [Assessing what a source supports (KCAE.ASSESS:4.2)](DPFs/Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/KNOWLEDGE-CORPUS-ACCESS-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#kcaeassess42---build-an-inspectable-claim-and-condition-reading)

For a numerical contribution, recover how the number was obtained. A reading can enter a maximum while another reading determines its value. A term in an equation depends on the chosen decomposition. Moving an equal quantity from one term to another can leave the modeled outcome unchanged. A share of a total depends on the counted units and the allocation rule. For a question about changing the system, specify the feasible change and its alternative. Establish whether the number bears on that comparison and what warrants using the model under those conditions. To attribute a past outcome to the change, establish what actually happened and what the evidence supports about the alternative. A bound can suffice when it places every supported outcome on the same side of the decision threshold. [Inputs to an aggregate (A.19.ULSAM:4.2)](FPF-Spec.md#a19ulsam42---interpretation-notes-didactic-informative) · [Terms in a reduced model (MMP.9:4.1)](DPFs/Foundational%20Thinking%20DPF%20Suite/MATHEMATICAL-MODELING-PRACTICE-DPF.md#mmp941---express-the-retained-result-and-its-change) · [Source shares (DTM.5:4.2)](DPFs/DEFENSE-AND-TRANSMISSION-MODELING-DPF.md#dtm542---recover-contribution-weights-and-within-group-change)

When the question concerns a candidate's effect on coverage, compare the collections that the proposed change and its alternative would leave. Another member may now cover what only this candidate supplied when it arrived. The gain from adding it then and the loss from removing it today can therefore differ. For a past choice, establish what determined it at the time. For a future project, compare what the team could actually do with the candidate and with its feasible alternatives. [Comparing collections (C.17:5.1)](FPF-Spec.md#c1751---diversity-and-illumination) · [Explaining archive membership (C.18:4.1)](FPF-Spec.md#c1841---archive-record) · [Assessing future use (C.19:4.0)](FPF-Spec.md#c1940---develop-the-continuation-basis-when-needed)

When a software team asks about *technical debt*, it may want to know how much **architectural debt** an earlier design created. A work record can establish the effort of a past change. To estimate what the design added, start when a different arrangement could have been chosen. Trace that alternative forward to the same later requirement. Which work would it still have required to deliver the same service and assurance? An option open during design may have closed by the time the change was needed. Describe the opportunity and when it closed if its value cannot be priced. To judge the original choice, also consider what was known and what it would have cost to preserve the option then. To compare ways of carrying out the later change, use the options still attainable when it came due. If the team asks whether an architecture decision was followed, compare the agreed arrangement with what was actually built. Restoring a bypassed interface and revising an interface that people used as agreed address different findings. [Comparing feasible changes](https://fpf.tools/C.11.CRC:4) · [Examining a causal claim](https://fpf.tools/C.28.CM:4)

To decide what to do now, compare the burden of keeping the dependency for the anticipated changes with the cost and consequences of each available response. Include changing over, supporting both arrangements where necessary, and the work others will take on. This comparison can justify action while the avoidable share of a past expense is unknown. It can also favour keeping an arrangement that caused a known loss. If the commission is to count outstanding evidence reviews, the team's counting rule and the relevant obligations can already give the answer. [Engineering for later changes](https://fpf.tools/SYSE.23:4.1) · [Counting evidence obligations](https://fpf.tools/B.3.4:4.3)

An education provider might call its dependence on the first course author **teaching debt**. When the required skill changes, any instructor needs sound grounds for assessing it. If each new instructor must reconstruct those grounds from the author's past judgements, making them accessible could save that repeated work. A single consultation can suffice for one more class. If two classes require simultaneous decisions, shared criteria help with interpretation, while both classes may still depend on one authorized examiner. When the concern is the person's own development, establish what they want to learn, change or stop doing, and which abilities, if any, must remain available. To attribute difficulty to their earlier preparation, identify the dependency and a feasible alternative under which the intended change would have taken less work or remained possible. A mistake on a first attempt can simply identify what they still need to learn. Assess what the person can choose or do with the help the task permits. [Changing a whole learning product](https://fpf.tools/HCD.28:4) · [Connecting capabilities, learning provision and the person's work](https://fpf.tools/HCE.Reference:4.1) · [Diagnosing a difficulty in practice](https://fpf.tools/HCD.3:4.2)

### Library.Reference:6.2 - Interpret levels, layers and scales through their relations

A level is relative to an identified relation and subject. Nested system constituents can support system-composition levels; constituent Methods can support Method-composition levels. Classification, refinement, abstraction, authority and time answer other questions even when a source also calls their positions levels. The depth of a composition depends on the selected whole. A taxonomic child is a narrower kind, whereas a constituent supplies a contribution to a whole.

When several ranges matter, say what the conversation, decision or explanation must cover. “Follow the release Method from the service procedure to the review Methods it admits” names a range of Method composition. “Compare the discipline's model with the local model for the same release decision” asks whether the models differ in abstraction for that question. Local adoption can preserve the same model. Use the comparison below to establish whether either model is a strict refinement for the same question and interpretation. The models can also distinguish different aspects and be incomparable. “Find which reviews actually support today's release” brings the relevant Work and reports into the decision. A conclusion that holds at each end of a range can still leave open what connects the ends. C.39.QR:4.1 helps repair a restatement that reduced the range to one member. C.37.MM:4.1.1 gives the method for joining the claims across ranges and kinds. The [unified term sheet](#libraryreference622---unified-term-sheet-for-scales-layers-and-model-abstraction) gives the reusable names and their grounds.

An account of functional layers groups contributions by what they do and explains how their results connect. Planning and tracking can be functional layers in a control design. Delivery, support and development can be functional groupings in an operating arrangement. Several modules or people can contribute to one layer, and one module or person can contribute to several layers. Use a layered account when this grouping helps explain the required result; it does not require a matching organization of physical components or teams.

This gives a qualified answer to “are management levels functional layers?” A source can be grouping functions, composing organizational units, allocating decision rights or comparing horizons. Delivery, support and development performed by one team can be described functionally. If that team is a constituent of a firm under the applicable system-composition conditions, its system relation remains available as well. A manager's authority and planning horizon require their own account. The word *management* chooses none of these relations in advance.

“Method level” similarly needs its local meaning. It can concern a constituent Method, a narrower family, a model's abstraction level or the order in which an explanation introduces topics. Models of one Method can occupy different abstraction levels under the B.5.MR comparison below. For a relation between Methods, first establish their identities; [FPF A.3.1:4.8](FPF-Spec.md#a3148---method-relations-composition-and-work-enactment) then asks which semantics are retained, what is narrowed or strengthened and what action or result changes. More detailed description alone does not settle this comparison. Teaching one topic before another can make an explanation usable without making the earlier topic a constituent or execution stage of every later Method.

When bodily practices are described as a stack, ask how the practitioner uses one in another. A way of dancing a turn can require the dancer to regulate contact with a partner throughout the figure. That regulation then belongs to the Method for the turn. An earlier exercise may have helped the dancer acquire the skill. To count the exercise itself as a constituent, establish that doing the figure also calls for it or admits it as a way of performing a required part. That same performance can carry two rhythmic layers, such as a pulse and a changing accent. A recording that preserves the timing of both can settle whether the accent fell where the score required it. To decide what the dancer needs to practise, establish who supplies the pulse and who must maintain or vary it. When commissioning the design, establish which choices the dancer must make. Even with the pulse and accent already assigned, the dancer may still have to devise a cue that different partners can recognize in time. The design can be judged on that basis. If the aim is to teach or assess that design work, give the learner the choices it calls for. The extent of the figure and the distinctions its model must preserve follow from the work in question. [Coordinating rhythmic layers (RHY.11:4.3)](DPFs/EMBODIED-RHYTHMICS-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#rhy1143---check-simultaneous-demands-not-just-separate-schedules) · [Choosing what to examine together (HCE.Reference:3)](DPFs/Human%20Capability%20Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/HUMAN-CAPABILITY-ENGINEERING-DPF-SUITE-REFERENCE.md#hcereference3---how-do-the-contributions-meet-in-a-whole-action)

Choose the explanation from what the recipient needs to understand, establish, design or decide. Which relations account for the property in question, and which differences must the model keep for the explanation to hold? Does each unit following its rule suffice for the whole organization to deliver? Trace the handovers and any shared limits. Does one completed delivery justify adopting the same rule across a discipline? Establish which conditions remain true when the case changes. A Core pattern often owes a transdisciplinary explanation, a DPF its disciplinary interpretation, and an LPF a decision usable in local practice. Each can also describe the same individual delivery. Name the claim in the passage when that difference matters. [Developing the account at the required scales](FPF-Spec.md#c39wa42---construct-the-organization-the-account-must-explain) and [comparing models for refinement](FPF-Spec.md#b5mr43---read-the-levels-of-model-abstraction-and-refinement).

For a delivery commitment, the recipient’s decision determines the range. To promise a shipment by noon, follow the handovers that can change that answer. Keep arrival times distinguishable wherever grouping them would put a late and an on-time delivery together. A confirmed reservation may settle capacity without opening the vehicle model. For a guarantee covering every shift, account for the competing bookings those shifts admit. An end-to-end guarantee may settle the commitment directly. When a proposed summary is supposed to preserve the decision through a change of model, establish that transfer or compare what the connected models and their summaries support. Store totals can preserve the stock count and still lose the connection that decides delivery. To assess someone's choice of scales and model, establish which of those choices the task left to that person. [Choosing and connecting the models](FPF-Spec.md#c39wa42---construct-the-organization-the-account-must-explain) · [Comparing the two calculations](FPF-Spec.md#c29143---compare-performing-and-representing-in-both-orders)

When a change may affect several Systems, choose the extent of the inquiry from the decision. One credible effect on a System outside the project can justify considering another design. To name every affected System, the identities must be recoverable. An authorised group channel with confirmed coverage and delivery can notify every intended recipient even when their names are unavailable to the sender. A claim about each System needs grounds covering each one. Evidence about a joint outcome can establish it directly for the cases and times covered. To infer it from separate findings, establish how the effects combine. The Systems may be peers, or they may be parts of a System whose properties matter to the decision. If another permitted arrangement changes the answer or its required grounds, retain that difference in the model. For example, keeping a shared service within its limit requires grounds for what the uses can demand together. A rule covering the agreed domain can also establish that no System qualifies. Follow the relation from Work to the System whose state could change and keep the time, criterion and conditions of that claim explicit. [A.1.CSD, discovering systems that may bear consequences](FPF-Spec.md#a1csd41---perform-the-move)

Start with the ethical question the recipient needs answered. A person’s account can establish their stated objection, and an effective decision can establish what the department adopted. When the judgement depends on affected interests, trace the proposed change to those who could bear its consequences, including people absent from its records. Establish participation, representation or authority where the conclusion relies on it. A duty to remedy a loss and authority to authorize the remedy can belong to different parties. A department can adopt a decision under its mandate even when a member disagrees. A benefit measured over the whole department can coexist with a loss for some members. [D.2](FPF-Spec.md#d22---solution) traces the effect to those who bear it. [D.3](FPF-Spec.md#d321---first-result) describes the claims in tension. Use [D.4](FPF-Spec.md#d421---develop-and-compare-a-continuation) to compare feasible options, identify who may decide and state which burdens remain. [D.5](FPF-Spec.md#d52---solution) checks what the evidence covers.

A project may adopt a Core rule unchanged. That act gives the rule local force within the adopting authority's mandate. To claim strict model refinement, compare the models for the same inquiry. Show a feasible completion of the local model, how every local completion satisfies the earlier commitments under the chosen interpretation, and an earlier admissible answer the local model excludes. To claim a new priority, identify the terms that make one provision prevail for the decision.

Use a qualified expression where it affects what the reader can infer or do.

| Meaning needed here | Wording for the reader |
| --- | --- |
| How an identified System is composed of other Systems | scale of System composition. *System level* is sufficient when this part–whole relation is understood. |
| How an encompassing Method is composed of constituent Methods | scale of Method composition. A *layer of a Method stack* can name such a scale where the constituent relation is established. |
| Which functions a design groups by their contribution and how they interact | functional layer, or *control layer* in a control architecture. Identify the Methods that implement it if their composition matters. |
| Modules ordered by enclosure or by a stated dependency | modular level under that ordering. Use *module* and *interface* for peers whose exchange has no higher or lower member. |
| Ordered comparison of models for the same inquiry | levels of model abstraction towards more abstract models; levels of model refinement towards more concrete models |
| Detail shown or omitted in a representation | level of detail |
| Position among narrower or broader kinds | taxonomic level; specialization |
| The temporal quantity being compared | timescale; update period; planning horizon |
| Who may change an identified choice or commitment | decision authority |
| Which applicable provision governs a disputed choice in FPF | FPF precedence rank and any applicable priority. Use [E.3:4.2](FPF-Spec.md#e342---precedence-stack) to decide the case or identify who can resolve it. |

Keep a source's well-defined term. For example, the ISA-95 account in [OPC 10030 §4.2.3](https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10030/4.2.3) uses *activity levels*. Recover that activity relation rather than automatically replacing *levels* with *layers*. [FPF C.30.STRAT:4.2](FPF-Spec.md#c30strat42---recovered-meanings-and-patterns-to-use) gives the local functional-layer/modular-level distinction and the other meanings to recover. A spatial or temporal “scale” does not automatically identify a *Scale* in a measurement model. State the quantity, unit and comparison required by the present measurement claim.

Increasing the number of installations under one design changes the size of the rollout. Leaving open which sites require an adapter changes what the model can say about the work. If both choices of site imply the same cost, that omission preserves the cost answer. If one choice calls for additional qualification or a different price, recover the relevant site conditions before committing to one estimate. The same disciplinary model can compare rollouts of five or forty installations. [C.31.ASAP §4.3](FPF-Spec.md#c31asap43---architecture-scale-preference-rule)

One Russian word, «управление», can become *speed control* in a drive specification, *team management* in a staffing plan, or *governance* in a charter that allocates decision rights. Identify the operation or authority in the passage before choosing the English term; [FPF E.10:9.8](FPF-Spec.md#e1098---control-management-regulation-governance-steering) and C.43 retain their different uses. When translating into another natural language, carry the subject, relation, conditions and example first, then choose the locally useful expression. Keep a qualified phrase or explained source term if one target word would merge action-changing meanings. Revisit a translation when it changes which contribution is selected, composed, authorized or revised.

**Model refinement and model abstraction compare models for the same inquiry.** A model of a system may admit several designs. A model of a method may permit several executions. Each admissible design or execution is a *completion* from which the reader obtains an answer. To establish that C refines A, show that at least one completion of C exists and that every such completion satisfies A under the chosen interpretation. A is then an abstraction of C. Call C *more concrete* and A *more abstract* for that inquiry only if A admits an answer that no completion of C gives. [B.5.MR, Compare Models by Refinement and Abstraction](FPF-Spec.md#b5mr---compare-models-by-refinement-and-abstraction)

Start with the work the model must support. Which alternatives would change what the recipient can construct, perform, maintain or explain? To refine the model, obtain grounds for restricting those alternatives and apply the refinement test above. To abstract it, choose the distinctions that may be left open and apply the same test with the starting model on the concrete side. Recheck the conclusion needed by the recipient over everything the abstraction now admits. If the conclusion is lost, identify the premise that supported it and decide whether to restore it. To extend a method claim to another discipline, establish how the operation is interpreted there and why its conditions hold (C.20:4.8). A claim about actual uptake or achieved performance there needs evidence for that further claim.

**Read L0 → L4 as levels of model abstraction and L4 → L0 as levels of model refinement.** L4 supplies a foundational formal or computational construction. L3 states a principle across disciplines for which its interpretation is warranted. L2 specifies a disciplinary interpretation, constraint or method, L1 the conditions of a recurring local practice and L0 the identity of the individual entity or occurrence being modeled. The individual can still have unknown properties. Local adoption can leave all the rule’s answers to the inquiry unchanged. For example, if a site adopts an L2 rule unchanged for the inquiry, report the rule at L2. An L1 model can record which rule the site uses for recurring work. One named level can encompass several strict refinements. Two local rules on different branches can give the same answer while requiring incompatible actions. Test each pair before claiming refinement. When a decision also uses a C.23 maturity rating, name each scale. A disciplinary model is at model abstraction level L2. Maturity L2 (Replicated) records evidence about a specified family of methods in the tested conditions.

Use those levels to decide what a reported result licenses. If someone proposes adopting a rule for a class of systems, identify what in the argument covers that class. If adoption depends on every permitted design meeting a condition, one admissible design that fails it defeats that basis for adoption. If the decision needs only one feasible design, finding one can settle it. If someone claims to have developed the rule, compare what they received with the choices they actually made. The same rule can be fit to use even when authorship remains open. [C.39.QR:4.3](FPF-Spec.md#c39qr43---establish-what-the-performed-work-obtained)

Core, DPFs and LPFs supply models that can participate in these comparisons; the publications themselves do not occupy fixed ranks. A DPF can supply a disciplinary model used to develop a local model, and a local observation can expose a defect in that disciplinary model. Establish the actual assertions and relation at each transition.

For a model to conform to a metamodel, it must satisfy that metamodel’s conventions. When a decision or handover depends on several models, use [C.37.MM](FPF-Spec.md#c37mm---coordinate-related-models-for-use-and-handover) to recover their governing conventions and test the conclusion they support together. To decide which expressions a language admits, how they can be read or which entity a designation picks out, use [E.5.2.L](FPF-Spec.md#e52l---languages-expressions-and-interpretation). The same language can express different commitments. A.2 governs a separate question about the system assigned a role.

For a connected account of epistemes, descriptions, models, views, expressions, notation and publication, read [C.2.1:4.0](FPF-Spec.md#c2140---first-use-completeness-questions). It explains when these names concern the same individual and which additional relations a use needs. Its :4.0.13 helps choose what to establish when the task concerns changed claims, interpretation, viewpoint conformance or publication availability.

When deciding how different practices can use a shared description, start with what each recipient must decide or explain and what they must accomplish together. One view may support several uses. Several individually useful views may leave a relationship needed for their joint decision invisible. [E.17.0:4.8](FPF-Spec.md#e17048---keep-selection-for-one-describing-use-separate) helps choose the viewpoints and correspondences that preserve that relationship. To decide how the work itself should be organized, compare the available arrangements and their consequences for the whole under [C.30](FPF-Spec.md#c30---architecture-and-selected-structure-adequacy) and [C.32](FPF-Spec.md#c32---architecture-candidate-synthesis). A request for an existing framework decision may be answered from its current [Architectural Reference](FPF-Spec.md#e11dsg---architectural-reference) or another authoritative account. For a proposed change to FPF content, [E.9:4.2](FPF-Spec.md#e942---develop-an-unsettled-decision-through-a-working-expression) connects that decision to its grounds and the texts that must carry it.

#### Library.Reference:6.2.1 - Reason about the types used to model a domain

Take the subject of the present inquiry as your point of reference. Name it and what you need to establish, explain or do. If a decision depends on the answer, identify that decision. “The rules for issuing a release in this laboratory” is more informative than “one level up.” To move, name the next subject and the relation you intend to follow. A record may direct you to the rule used to produce it, to the work it represents or to the episteme that states the rule. Check the issuing practice, the interpretation of the record or the content of that episteme, respectively. These transitions license different inferences. Stop when the result answers the inquiry or supports the intended action. When you change the question, identify the new point of reference.

A model can describe the work done on a particular occasion. A different inquiry asks what a discipline counts as work, which types it uses to represent it and what follows from those choices. Here the domain's own types and relations become the objects of reasoning. This is metamodeling. Identify what is being modeled and what the practitioner needs to do with it.

To explain how an L3 principle becomes an L2 method, state the principle in terms that apply across the eligible disciplines. Then work out what the principle helps practitioners in the discipline accomplish. When they must make or maintain something, derive the operations and connections through which they can obtain that result. When they need to explain a phenomenon, develop the grounds that account for it. When they must decide, show what warrants the decision, including when several choices remain open. Which entities does their method act on? Which differences must it preserve to do that work? If the principle concerns composition, show how the discipline identifies the relevant whole and constituents and what their arrangement makes possible. If practitioners must connect several models, explain the relation their use requires. A claim about joint operation needs compatible conditions. Comparing successive configurations may require a common measure. Rival models may warrant the same decision. Explain how these disciplinary choices satisfy the principle and where the resulting method applies. A worked case can exercise the choices. When the learner must make them, give the learner an opportunity to make them before showing the author’s choices. Ask for their grounds when the exercise calls for them. For an architecture comparison, C.33:4 establishes what one view can settle, C.34:4 identifies conclusions that carry between views, and C.35:4 explains what a search result can establish.

If the task is to design a table for future work, examine who decided what a row represents and when two entries refer to the same thing. Filling rows under conventions supplied by the author can demonstrate use of those conventions. To credit readers with designing the conventions, establish which choices they made themselves from their work and the help available to them. The design can show those choices. Ask for a justification if it is part of the work or needed to interpret the result. A correct completed table may be usable in either case.

When a summary replaces a table, start with the decision the reader must make from it. Two departments may each report a count of completed orders for the same month. Adding the counts gives the number of distinct orders if no order occurs in both departments. With possible overlap, the same counts can represent different totals. If each department completed ten distinct orders, their combined total is between ten and twenty. That settles a target of at least ten. A target of fifteen may need shared identifiers or another way to bound the overlap. The calculation also depends on what counts as completion for both departments. Use the notation rule to decide what the displayed counts establish. Compare models when the summary is to define which joint outcomes are permitted. [E.5.2:4 on replacing an expression](FPF-Spec.md#e524---solution---keep-meaning-portable-while-choosing-a-useful-expression) · [B.5.MR:4.2 on comparing the models](FPF-Spec.md#b5mr42---test-preservation-and-strictness)

Keep the words appropriate to the job. The design office may need a rule about a type of joint. The workshop may need the identifier of one defective unit. With the same product and people, an editor can ask whether a passage still teaches a repeatable check or merely records one use. Use the terms the recipient needs to distinguish the possible answers and follow their grounds. Several distinctions may matter only in combination. [F.19:4.3.1, keeping what a passage claims and teaches](FPF-Spec.md#f19431---keep-what-a-passage-claims-and-teaches-through-a-change-of-wording).

An abstract model can be expressed clearly, while a passage about one individual can leave its assertion unrecoverable. To examine a complaint that wording is too abstract, first establish the intended use from the request and the material available to the recipient. An unresolved purpose prevents a sufficiency judgement for that purpose; it does not by itself make a clear assertion unintelligible. Keep a passage's local function distinct from the larger explanation requested.

Read the words the recipient will actually receive beside the question the explanation is to answer. A passage can be perfectly clear, and a separately issued exercise can be easy to complete, even after a different subject or a ready-made choice has displaced the work the reader needs. Recover the assertion, relation or operation the recipient needs. A precise term helps when its definition and use are available; an unexplained label can fail as readily as an ordinary umbrella word. Keep ordinary wording when the connected explanation supplies its meaning. If supported clarification reveals the same model assertions, the model has not thereby become more concrete. If an assertion, premise, derivation or way of performing the instruction is missing, obtain that content through the subject Method. [Explanation Design, Reference:3](DPFs/EXPLANATION-DESIGN-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#exdreference3---obtain-and-carry-the-subject-explanation) follows the whole explanatory attempt and returns supported content and its limits. [F.19:4.3](FPF-Spec.md#f1943---one-connected-reading-and-repair) carries it into the wording and repeats local recovery. Use B.5.MR when the repair changes a model's assertions and the refinement or abstraction relation matters. The text's production history need not be known to find the missing content.

Use the discipline as the point of reference when the question concerns how a field works. C.20 identifies the discipline as a whole of knowledge and reproducible practice. An episteme can state its rules or model a situation. Identify the subject of the claim and what establishes the claim. A rule may be stated directly in an authoritative policy or expressed in a model. Use the source needed for the question at hand. A reusable ontic can settle identity or relations needed by several uses. For a local application the existing field rule and one model may suffice. If the recipient needs several governed claims together, a bounded local episteme can connect them. When identified dependents require a shared identity or relation rule across uses, examine the case for a durable ontic. [FPF C.20:4.1](FPF-Spec.md#c2041---run-the-complete-recognition-test) · [E.24:4.3–4.5, choosing how to connect the claims](FPF-Spec.md#e2443---check-3-direct-relation-and-declaration-test) · [E.24.PUB for access to the edition](FPF-Spec.md#e24pub4---solution)

A project provides a local setting for applying or adapting types. Name the work or plan, the object being considered and why it matters to the inquiry or action. In systems engineering, the system-of-interest is the product System that an extended enterprise is organized through agreements to create, change, sustain in use or retire. Agents acting for the participating organizations establish or accept the objectives and commitments. The extended enterprise is an organization System. An actual project is composite Work; its plan states intended work. The system-in-hand is the system being investigated or changed for the immediate work. The same system can qualify in both respects. A temporary project can help realize a product while working on another system such as a test facility. Its current subject can also be a Method or an episteme. [FPF A.1.STM:4.1](FPF-Spec.md#a1stm41---establish-the-project-system-of-interest-its-use-and-the-participants-agreement) · [FPF A.15.6:4.1a](FPF-Spec.md#a15641a---connect-project-work-the-system-of-interest-and-the-local-subject)

A testing discipline can teach a metamodel of the types used in testing. A particular project can adopt or adapt those types for its own tools, work and records. The project may select one of several disciplinary practices, narrow the conditions, or use another name for the same role. To identify the metamodel, name the types or modeling rules being modeled. The same disciplinary type can appear in several project models, and different metamodels can serve different questions about it.

| Scope of the account | Distinction used to reason | What to do with it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Transdisciplinary reasoning about the types and methods used in a field | A Method is a reusable way of doing something. Work is a particular performance. A description can represent either. | Identify which claim matters to the receiving work. Recover the method and its conditions for a claim about how to obtain a result. For a claim about performance, establish which work took place and on what object. |
| A discipline’s domain metamodel | A testing discipline distinguishes a test procedure, a test run and a verdict on the item tested. | Specify what must be observed, under which test conditions, and how those observations warrant a verdict. |
| The local practice and metamodel of a named project or organization | A project may use “inspection” for both the procedure and a signed record. Its types and rules can be recovered from the local practice and the data schema. | Establish how a record identifies a run, who may issue the verdict and whether the schema preserves the distinction the decision requires. |
| A particular situation (model of the case) | A record describes a run on an identified item under stated conditions. | Decide what that run establishes about that item. If the record refers to a different configuration, check which grounds carry over before applying the verdict. |

Choose the relation that supports the inference. Classifying a domain type requires grounds for applying the higher-order distinction to that type. To establish that one type specializes another, show that meeting the narrower criterion also meets the broader one within scope. This can follow from the definitions. In a deliberately closed finite domain, checking every candidate can establish the inclusion for that domain. To infer something about a subject from its model, establish what the representation preserves. An existing kind criterion can be used at either scope. An ontic can coordinate the identity and direct relation rules that several uses need together. These distinctions allow a transdisciplinary method to be performed on a particular case. [FPF C.3.1:4](FPF-Spec.md#c314---core-objects) · [FPF C.39.WA:4.3](FPF-Spec.md#c39wa43---identify-the-relation-used-in-each-consequential-transition) · [FPF E.24:4](FPF-Spec.md#e244---solution)

When the result you need is a new or revised method, identify the work its users must learn to do or be able to rely on. Recover what the contributing practices already establish. If a choice that is valid in one practice changes what another must do, establish the condition for using those choices together. Follow each independent contribution to its user. The contributions can settle separate questions, or a recipient can need several of them for one conclusion. In the latter case, establish that they concern the required subjects under compatible conditions and explain what follows from them together. From these grounds, construct the missing operation or choose an existing method that supplies it. A conceptual synthesis can itself answer a question about the relationship between the source accounts.

For example, to develop a method of checking proofs, distinguish the argument from the record of a checker run. Establish which version the record covers, follow the inference from the premises under the stated calculus and verify that it reaches the claimed conclusion. If the version or extent of checking is unknown, obtain a result tied to that argument or check it directly before relying on the verdict. The same service may also need a conceptual synthesis of what “checked” means in both systems for subsequent audits. A supported account of their agreement or difference can settle that inquiry. [FPF F.0.2:4.6](FPF-Spec.md#f0246---semantic-and-methodological-results) · [FPF C.39](FPF-Spec.md#c39---find-and-develop-a-way-to-obtain-a-result)

**Use general distinctions in local speech.** A heading, a form or a checklist can carry a domain type without displaying its transdisciplinary name. Check what the field accepts, which relation the entry asserts and what action that licenses. An empty field can mean “unknown,” “not applicable,” or “no observation required”; establish which meaning the local rule gives it before acting on it. Explain the distinction explicitly when teaching it or repairing a disagreement. A practitioner may work it out on paper, in conversation or with a software agent. The recipient needs a usable instruction or conclusion in the vocabulary of the receiving work.

For instance, a maintenance form has a box labeled “ready.” The team enters the date a unit was adjusted. The release authority needs a verdict on operation within tolerance. The date can document an intervention. To decide on release, identify the unit after adjustment, obtain the required measurements and apply the applicable acceptance rule. If a separate authorized test has already done this, retrieve its verdict. A revised form may point to that verdict. Changing the heading cannot supply the measurement or the authority. One agent can design the form, another carry out the test and a third use the result.

To practise constructing or challenging a mapping, give the learner work for which a mapping must be chosen or justified. For a question about transfer, vary the material, conditions or support in the way the intended work requires. A supplied problem statement can support an exercise in checking its grounds or selecting a method. An exercise in framing the problem must leave that choice open. Inspect the brief, working record and available help to establish what the learner supplied. A record of an earlier attempt can show that choice. For the design of the exercise see [E.8:4.2.1.3](FPF-Spec.md#e84213---choose-examples-by-the-explanation-they-make-usable). For the different question of acquiring a capability through inquiry, a demonstration, a description or supported practice see [E.23.CDI:4.2.1](FPF-Spec.md#e23cdi421---choose-how-method-structure-will-become-usable).

Obtain the result the learner was asked to produce. If the commission includes explaining a conclusion, examine the grounds the learner gives in the terms of that discipline. When the commission concerns adapting a method to changed conditions, vary a condition relevant to that use and examine which inference the learner retains, revises or leaves open. For an individual, establish who made the consequential choice. For a human–AI team, examine the division of work. For a software agent, identify its configuration and tools. Performance on this task provides evidence about the action observed. Claims about learning or durable transfer need evidence appropriate to the holder.

#### Library.Reference:6.2.2 - Unified term sheet for scales, layers and model abstraction

At first reading, ask what the question itself asks the recipient to compare. An author may give a whole System as material for a question about one part, or use one part to ask about a repeatable relation. In “at all levels”, recover the kind of holon and the part–whole relation, the functional ordering of layers, or the comparison of models for one inquiry. Where several are involved, identify how they are connected in the requested conclusion. “All” can range over the branches relevant to that question. [C.39.QR:4.1.1, Recover what “all” covers](FPF-Spec.md#c39qr411---recover-what-all-covers)

To choose a word for a project document, start with what you want the recipient to compare. How long is the system? Which systems make up its enclosing whole? Which functions take part in control? Which Methods make their performance possible? Which situations admit the claim? What does a more concrete model determine for the same decision? The answers can vary independently. A branch office can occupy two buildings while remaining one unit of the service. Two investigations can each cover fifteen offices and cover different offices. State the physical quantity, composition relation or covered population that answers your question.

Use the three settled model-comparison rows directly. *Model refinement* and *model abstraction* are converses for the same inquiry and interpretation. In a chain established by that comparison, L0 to L4 reads towards more abstract models and L4 to L0 towards more concrete models. A strict difference requires a feasible more concrete model whose completions preserve the earlier commitments and exclude an answer the more abstract model admits. These are the existing `UTS.ModelRefinement.2026-11-16`, `UTS.ModelAbstraction.2026-11-16` and `UTS.ModelAbstractionLevel.2026-11-16` with their original cells and NameCards. [F.17:12.6](FPF-Spec.md#f17126---model-comparison-direction-and-level-rows) · [F.18:4.5](FPF-Spec.md#f1845---model-refinement-abstraction-and-relative-levels) · [B.5.MR:4.2](FPF-Spec.md#b5mr42---test-preservation-and-strictness)

To choose the composition scales and model abstraction for a task, start with the difference the recipient must be able to act on or explain. Follow the relevant part–whole relation for each kind. Test what becomes impossible to decide if the model merges two allowed situations, and which choices can remain open. A bound or common argument may cover the whole range. [C.39.SA:4, Choose Composition Scales and Model Abstraction for the Task](FPF-Spec.md#c39sa4---solution)

For a range of composition, name the kind of whole and the relation through which its parts belong. Two ends can identify a range on a given chain. In a branching structure, say which branches or members the question includes when the ends leave that open. The question may call for a property at each scale, a relation across scales or a result of several contributions together. For each kind, follow the range its part in the answer requires. Across kinds, establish the relation that connects them. A Work can enact a Method, a report can describe that Work and two reports can draw on one observation. A change in how many composition scales a model represents can leave unchanged the distinctions it makes for a given question. If the relation is already clear, ordinary terms such as *part*, *level* and *layer* remain usable.

**F.18 naming decisions.** Each key below identifies a separate NameCard `NC.RangeTerms.<key>`. It concerns the kind defined by the cited owner in this edition. The selected designation is the Tech expression. The explanation gives the Plain reading for discussion. It also specifies the local sense of that expression under the FPF Core scheme. The last column records why a plausible competing term changes the inference.

| Key | Selected designation and owner | Local sense for this use | Competing reading and reason for the choice |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SizeScale | **Scale**, A.18, C.16.P:4 | The CSLC kind of an organized set of possible values for a specified Characteristic, with its permitted comparisons and operations. Say *measurement scale* in discussion. | A common scale can be used to report many different physical sizes. |
| SizeValue | **Coordinate**, A.18, C.16.MR | The CSLC kind of a single value in the domain of a declared Scale. To report physical size, name the system, characteristic such as overall length, measurement conditions and unit where applicable. Give the supported coordinate, or report the supported interval, set or bound when the information warrants only that. A known upper bound on length can settle a clearance decision. For two otherwise comparable lengths of 2 m and 3 m, the scale stays the same while the coordinates differ. | A coordinate alone identifies neither the system measured nor the body of evidence that supports the measurement result. |
| System | **ComponentOf**, A.14, B.1.2:4 | The A.14 structural-component relation kind. For system composition, identify both systems and establish the integration of the component into its encompassing system. A chain of those relations supports talk of *system-composition levels*. | Two systems can differ greatly in length without standing in this relation. |
| Method | **MethodPartOfRelation**, B.1.5, B.1.5.CM:4 | The relation kind whose ordered participants are the independently identified part Method and whole Method. The whole’s stable construction requires its contribution or admits it as an alternative for a required contribution. Follow the relations to describe *Method-composition levels*. | A supplier can provide an input from outside that Method. An admitted alternative retains membership even on a day when another was selected. |
| Work | **OperationalPartOf_work**, A.15.1:4.1a, B.1.5.EW:4 | The operational-part relation kind between two independently identified Work occurrences. The performed content of the first is constitutive of the second at the stated operational grain. Use *Work-composition levels* along this relation. | For a proper temporal sub-occurrence A.15.1 specifies the distinct TemporalPartOf_work relation. Mere temporal overlap supplies neither relation. |
| Episteme | **ConstituentOf**, A.14, B.1.3:4 | The semantic-constituent relation kind. Establish the integration of the selected content into the encompassing episteme. To rely on its conclusion, B.1.3 also asks how the grounds combine. Follow such constituents when referring to *levels of epistemic composition*. | Reports can share an observation without being parts of one another. A page in a binder gives a publication location. |
| Discipline | **DisciplinePartOfRelation**, C.20:4.2 | The relation kind between an independently identified part entity and a candidate discipline. The knowledge or practice has to be integrated into the discipline’s organization for its field problem under C.20. Use *discipline composition* for the resulting organization. | A cited discipline can supply a useful result while remaining outside this composition. A further level requires a further admitted composition. |
| Layer | **functional layer**, C.30.STRAT:4.2, C.30.LCA, C.32.LCA | The local kind of functional grouping identified by the organizing contribution, the membership criterion and its member functions in the named architecture. In a control account *control layer* may be sufficient once the exchanges are understood. | Different Methods can implement that grouping. A claim that one of them is composite needs the admitted Method participants and their construction. |
| Claim | **U.ClaimScope**, A.2.6:6.3 | The set of context slices in which an identified claim applies. In speech, state where the claim is applicable. | The number of observed cases cannot identify this set or prove the claim throughout it. |
| Ability | **U.WorkScope**, A.2.6:6.4 | The set of work-condition slices designated by a qualified claim about a holder's ability. | Scope membership can license evaluation of the ability claim. A performed job and its outcome need their own grounds. |
| Publication | **U.PublicationScope**, A.2.6:6.5 | The slices where a publication view or form is admissible for use, within its applicable underlying claim or work scope. | Timely access to the publication and its actual use by a recipient are separate questions under E.24.PUB. |

**F.17 citation rows.** This thread is `RangesOfCompositionAndModelAbstraction`, edition `2026-11-20`. Each row below concerns the exact kind in bold in its NameCard. For System, Method, Work, Episteme and Discipline, it is the indicated relation kind. For SizeScale and SizeValue, it is the indicated CSLC kind. For Layer, it is the local kind of functional grouping defined in C.30.STRAT:4.2. Claim, Ability and Publication name the three existing scope kinds in A.2.6. Each such kind token has kind `U.Kind`. A place in a chain of relations, an actual instance, a measurement result and an entire discipline remain available for discussion in their own right.

The ReferenceScheme is the FPF Core interpretation stated in the exact sense for each key K. `SenseCell.RangeTerms.K.2026-11-20` pairs the selected expression with that sense. Each cited owner edition that supplies a needed premise has a separate basis relation to this cell. For instance, A.14 identifies semantic constituency and B.1.3 establishes how common grounds affect the composed inference. The section collects the existing CSLC, USM and composition designations in one sheet. The ordinary phrases for levels in §6.2 are retained for following chains. The qualified *functional layer* sense retains C.30.STRAT's functional grouping when a source also uses *layer* for the implementing Method. These are new citation rows for the established meanings. No cross-discipline Bridge follows from shared spelling.

| Stable row ID | NameCard and sense-cell key | Admitted citation use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `UTS.RangeTerms.SizeScale` | SizeScale | State the scale for comparing a named characteristic under compatible conditions. |
| `UTS.RangeTerms.SizeValue` | SizeValue | Report the coordinate of a specified size characteristic on that scale for the identified system under its measurement conditions. |
| `UTS.RangeTerms.System` | System | Identify the system composition and range under discussion. |
| `UTS.RangeTerms.Method` | Method | Identify the admitted constituent Methods and the range needed for the encompassing Method. |
| `UTS.RangeTerms.Work` | Work | Trace actual constituent performances to the encompassing occurrence. |
| `UTS.RangeTerms.Episteme` | Episteme | Identify the composition of interpreted content and its shared grounds. |
| `UTS.RangeTerms.Discipline` | Discipline | Explain which knowledge and practice a discipline actually integrates. |
| `UTS.RangeTerms.Layer` | Layer | Name a functional grouping and explain how it contributes or exchanges results. |
| `UTS.RangeTerms.Claim` | Claim | Ask whether a target context slice belongs to the stated claim scope. The domain of “each” identifies the members quantified over within the claim. Its applicability is a separate question. |
| `UTS.RangeTerms.Ability` | Ability | Ask whether the target work conditions are within the qualified ability claim's scope. |
| `UTS.RangeTerms.Publication` | Publication | Ask whether the intended use is within the publication's admissible scope. |

Each row uses its NameCard's reason for selecting the name and the competing reading it excludes. Reopen the affected row when its governing criterion, exact sense, selected expression or receiving use changes, or when its wording repeatedly licenses the competing reading. A term row lets the reader cite a meaning. The corresponding Method still determines whether the relation holds in the work at hand.

### Library.Reference:6.3 - A faster command can defeat an unchanged controller

Consider a constructed sampled controller with plant `x[k+1] = x[k] + u[k]` and tracking law `u[k] = (r[j] - x[k])/2`. The planner's command `r[j]` is held for `N` fast steps. The state is fully observed; this model has no disturbance, computation delay or unmodeled saturation. Commands satisfy `|r[j]| ≤ 8.9`; consecutive command changes have magnitude at most `δ`. Required sampled limits are `|x[k]| ≤ 10` and `|u[k]| ≤ 0.6`.

Planning and tracking are two functional contributions that could run on one processor. A separate system composition could include a controller, plant and their constituents. The functional grouping alone does not establish two physical Systems or two system-composition levels. The current question is whether the command and tracking contributions are compatible under the sampled limits.

Let `e[j] = x[jN] - r[j]` immediately after a command is selected. During the held-command interval the error halves at each fast step. At the next command change,

```text
e[j+1] = 2^(-N) e[j] - (r[j+1] - r[j]).
```

If the initial error satisfies `|e[0]| ≤ E`, the interval `|e| ≤ E` at command changes is invariant for `E = δ/(1 - 2^(-N))`: the next error is at most `2^(-N) E + δ = E`. Within each interval the error decreases. Hence `|u| ≤ E/2`, and `|x| ≤ |r| + E` is a sufficient state bound. This is the consequential join: the planner's jump and update conditions determine the tracking demand.

With `N = 4` and `δ = 1`, `E = 16/15`. The input magnitude is at most `8/15 < 0.6`, and the sufficient state bound is `8.9 + 16/15 = 299/30 < 10`. Under the stated initial and model conditions, every command sequence within this interface respects the sampled limits. Holding the current command is allowed by those constraints; the calculation does not establish attainment of every further planner objective.

Now require commands every two fast steps while retaining permitted unit jumps. The plant, processor and two functional contributions are unchanged. The error bound becomes `E = 4/3`, so the earlier sufficient input argument fails. There is also an actual violating trajectory: take `r[0] = 0` and `x[0] = 16/15`, admitted by the earlier design. After two steps, `x[2] = 4/15`. The permitted command `r[1] = -1` then gives `u[2] = -19/30`, whose magnitude exceeds `0.6`. The failure is in the changed temporal and command interface, not in the number of component boxes.

One repair keeps `N = 2` and the command-magnitude bound `8.9`, but reduces the jump bound to `δ = 0.9`. Require initial conditions `|e[0]| ≤ 1.2` and `|x[0]| ≤ 10`. The error envelope is invariant because `1.2/4 + 0.9 = 1.2`, so `|u| ≤ 0.6`. Check the state separately: `x[k+1] = (x[k] + r[j])/2` is a convex combination. From `|x[k]| ≤ 10` and `|r[j]| ≤ 8.9`, it follows that `|x[k+1]| ≤ 9.45 ≤ 10`. Thus the state set is invariant as well.

The coarser bound `|r| + E` would have suggested reducing the command magnitude to `8.8`. The separate state invariant shows that this extra restriction is unnecessary in the stipulated model. The revised interface still sacrifices the original unit-jump demand, and a transition from an operating controller must establish both new initial conditions. Accept this candidate only if the intended task permits that smaller jump. Retaining `N = 4` instead sacrifices the faster response. A timed command segment, another tracking law or stronger actuation changes other premises and requires another derivation.

[CAD.1–CAD.4 in Control Architecture Design](DPFs/Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/CONTROL-ARCHITECTURE-DESIGN-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) develop the corresponding architecture, interface, feasibility and co-design questions. This sampled calculation leaves real-plant adequacy, continuous-time constraints, shared-processor scheduling and the planner's further objectives to their required constructions. Keeping only two boxes loses the recurrence and cannot decide this change; retaining the recurrence, command conditions and initial state is enough for the demonstrated sampled-limit comparison. Processor detail becomes relevant if computation or delay changes that premise.

### Library.Reference:6.4 - The same organization can require different performance and provision

Consider a prospective diagnostic service with two accepted reports due by hour six of an eight-hour day. Each report needs two hours of analyst preparation followed by one hour of an authorized expert's judgement and acceptance. The analyst has eight available hours. The expert has four allocable hours with no further fixed calendar restriction in this constructed case. The protocol in use is qualified, and the expert is authorized to accept both reports. Under this service's agreement, the recipient obtains the report as soon as the expert accepts it in their shared register.

The expert must also perform one hour of support that keeps the service usable and one hour of development of the reusable diagnostic protocol. A feasible schedule gives the analyst preparation at hours `0–2` and `2–4`, the expert support at `0–1`, protocol development at `1–2`, and reviews at `2–3` and `4–5`. With this schedule, the accepted reports can be available to the recipient at hours 3 and 5. The expert is occupied for four hours. If the recipient needs a separate delivery, include its conditions and duration before promising arrival.

Delivery, support and development are different functional contributions made by the same person. Their three rows establish no three organizational units or management ranks. The reusable report Method also differs from the two planned performances and the separate protocol-development work. Developing a protocol changes a Method used in delivery; that development need not be a constituent of each report-producing Method. Yet all three kinds of contribution draw on the same expert capacity. Their distinct boundaries must remain connected in the service account.

Change the case class: additional evidence now makes each qualified review take `1.5` expert-hours. Keep the reporting promise, preparation and authority unchanged. Expert demand becomes `3 + 1 + 1 = 5` hours against four available. A total of analyst and expert labour would hide the shortage: spare analyst time cannot supply the qualified expert judgement. An unchanged organization chart therefore carries an unavailable promise.

One bounded alternative defers the hour of protocol development. This works only if the current protocol remains qualified and the development commitment permits a one-day delay. Keep analyst preparation at `0–2` and `2–4`; schedule support at `0–1` and reviews at `2–3.5` and `4–5.5`. The reports can be available at hours 3.5 and 5.5 under the shared-register agreement, with four hours of expert occupancy. The schedule meets today's deadline by moving development into another period. This is not a sustainable recurring arrangement by arithmetic alone. If the protocol must change before today's reports can be accepted, deferral fails at that dependency despite the hours fitting.

A different arrangement obtains one complete review from a qualified external provider. The provider must be able to receive the prepared material, return an acceptable result before hour six and supply the acceptance contribution allowed by the service's rules. Count transfer, preparation, coordination and checking in the arrangement. The internal expert's retained review, support and development require `1.5 + 1 + 1 = 3.5` hours, leaving at most `0.5` expert-hour for additional coordination and checking; those activities must fit their windows as well. That capacity remainder is a necessary condition, not a completed external-service schedule. The provider relation can support delivery without making the provider organization part of the internal team. If the provider cannot meet those conditions, report which commitments the available arrangement can meet. A manager authorized to defer a report can use the assessment to weigh deferral against obtaining more capacity. When the undertaking includes revising the promise, the authorized party chooses under the agreed terms. Whoever is entrusted with obtaining any required consent seeks it through the authorized route. If the authority or consent is still missing, keep the proposed change conditional.

Replacing judgement with an automated field-completeness check changes the supplied result. Complete fields do not establish the diagnostic conclusion the recipient needs. That proposal returns to the subject Method and acceptance criterion, whereas qualified external review returns primarily to provision, capacity and authority. The same slot in a route can conceal different contributions.

[OPS.11.1 in Operations Management](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/OPERATIONS-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) explains receiving completion, route, occupancy, provision and authority. [STR.14 in Strategy](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/STRATEGY-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) reconciles the simultaneous service, support and development commitments. To develop the arrangement, use [FPF C.32.MWA.PP:4.5–4.8](FPF-Spec.md#c32mwapp45---generate-whole-alternatives-by-changing-an-operative-relation): change the relation that makes the missing contribution obtainable, follow the moved burden, place qualified work in its receiving windows, and compare the complete alternatives. Its :4.9 gives the selected realization its remaining contribution and conditions. A proposed schedule or provider choice still needs effective commitments before performance can rely on it.

Here the changed premise is work content and qualified resource demand; in the controller it is the dynamic command interface. Both changes are found by returning from the required result to the contribution and relation that could defeat it. The resulting calculation then changes the feasible whole. System composition can remain intact in either case while the Method connection, permitted interface, provision or promise changes. C.32.MWA connects these accounts; the subject Methods supply the operations that make each conclusion warranted.

### Library.Reference:6.5 - Compact cycles in working control arrangements

Use this section when familiar cycle names leave you unsure which way of working fits, what its diagram assumes, or why another repetition has stopped helping. Begin with the result to obtain or maintain. Identify which observations can change the next action, who can carry it out and which conditions must already hold. If an adequate way and its provision are available, use them directly.

The comparison below connects selected improvement, decision and learning approaches through their relationships. It explains when a short cycle is sufficient and which changed dependency requires another method or arrangement. [C.43](FPF-Spec.md#c43---connect-observation-choice-and-action-in-control) supplies the general connection between observation, choice and action. The subject methods supply their own operations and conditions.

#### Library.Reference:6.5.1 - Establish what the cycle describes

A compact cycle can prescribe a way of acting, explain a process or remind a prepared practitioner of a known way. Recover that contribution from the source. A prescription needs operations, conditions and a rule for choosing the next move. An explanatory model represents relationships and needs grounds for the claims made about them. A reminder relies on a fuller way that the practitioner can already recover. A source may combine these contributions; distinguish its particular claims.

For a prescribed way, a description of the Method lets a performer recover its operations and conditions. A person, team or software system performs particular work with actual inputs, means and authority. Organizing that work makes information and coordinated action available when needed.
