A competent responder may identify an external correction that their current permission does not allow them to publish. Return the finding to the recipient authorized to receive it. When referral or follow-up forms part of the responder’s duty, use the permitted route and check publication to the extent that duty requires. Use coaching observations in the organization's decision about permissions, under its allocation of that decision right.

**Return a shared difficulty to the owner who can change it.** The KCS Council brings together experience of the content standard, workflow and tools; changes beyond its remit need the corresponding management or functional decision. Knowledge optimizers and domain experts develop questions from reuse and work with product, process or policy owners on the appropriate change. Begin [Knowledge Optimization](https://library.serviceinnovation.org/KCS/Knowledge-Centered_Success_Practices_Guide/301-Evolve_Loop/Practice_5_Content_Health/Technique_5.3) when the Solve Loop is implemented, using the experience already available. A small observation may warrant one correction without establishing a stable pattern or its cause. Carry the selected change into the content, tool or practice, then observe later resolution and the requestor's result. A revised rule or recommendation supplies only its part of that return.

For example, a responder can link an article for the wrong product version. Obtain the applicable answer for the current request. If the relevant distinction was available but the responder could not use it, a supported attempt can address that operation. If the interface concealed the distinction, ask its owner to make the needed version information usable. If a correct answer still leaves a substantial recurring burden, the product owner can compare changing the product with retaining the current service at its actual cost. Select among these continuations from the supported explanation of the difficulty. The fuller service application is [OPS.17:5.6](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/OPERATIONS-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md).

A familiar matching request with an adequate answer, capable performer and effective provision can finish through that arrangement, preserving the required trace of use. Obtain only the further contribution the remaining question needs. A current answer may stay useful while a separate investigation or product change proceeds.

The workflow and knowledge-analysis basis is [Structured Workflow Integration](https://library.serviceinnovation.org/KCS/Knowledge-Centered_Success_Practices_Guide/301-Evolve_Loop/Practice_6_Process_Integration/Technique_6.1) and [Knowledge Optimization](https://library.serviceinnovation.org/KCS/Knowledge-Centered_Success_Practices_Guide/301-Evolve_Loop/Practice_5_Content_Health/Technique_5.3); the linked assessment, proficiency and coaching treatments supply the further connections above. Using the whole practice requires its relevant implementation and participation conditions. [OPS.17:4.2.1](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/OPERATIONS-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) connects it to operational choice and its subject sources.

##### Library.Reference:6.5.3.8 - Hoshin/catchball: develop direction through feasible contributions

Use this connection when direction, local proposals and shared support do not yet form an undertaking the participants can carry out. A common aim can leave departments planning incompatible contributions. A locally sensible target can also misrepresent the result wanted by the recipient. Hoshin connects direction with investigation of the work, proposals in return, joint execution and reconsideration. The [Liker–Shook account](https://www.lean.org/the-lean-post/articles/hoshin-kanri-as-a-foundational-piece-of-a-lean-management-system/) relates this construction to horizontal as well as vertical dependence and to the development of problem-solving capability.

**Develop the proposal in both directions.** Start from the question that is open. An existing direction can be adequate; a shared concern may instead need a worthwhile direction to be constructed. A participant investigates the present work and proposes a contribution, explaining why the way should help, what they can undertake and what they need from others. The return can reveal an unsuitable local measure, an unsupported causal assumption or unavailable provision. Those imply different changes: refine the local interpretation, investigate or replace the means, obtain support, or reconsider the aim through its owner. [Graban's account of catchball](https://www.leanblog.org/2026/03/strategy-deployment-catch-ball-chuck-rock/) makes adjustment in both directions explicit. The proposal can inform a decision before it warrants a promise.

Bring the proposal to the people whose contributions it needs or whose work it displaces. Keep the dependencies between departments explicit: whose specialist time, permission or result must be available to whom and when. Compare the whole demand and timing, obtain the relevant decisions, and return changes to the affected participants. Some can contribute knowledge while another holds the authority to change a goal, commit resources or accept revised terms. [Reich's account of getting started](https://www.lean.org/the-lean-post/articles/getting-started-with-hoshin-kanri/) makes leadership participation and differing stakeholder powers consequential conditions. Select an attainable organizational scope with the required participation; introducing a form cannot confer the missing mandate.

**Choose the form of exchange for the result it must supply.** A group can examine a prepared plan, expose dependencies and improve it. When the participant must also become able to construct such a plan, provide for their own substantive attempt: they investigate the work, formulate the proposal and use a capable coach's response to improve their reasoning. [Howell's comparison](https://www.lean.org/the-lean-post/articles/expanding-the-purpose-of-catchball%EF%BF%BC/) explains this distinction between group revision and the problem owner's development. The latter needs practice time and suitable help. A better group plan does not by itself establish each participant's ability, and a management title does not supply coaching competence.

When relevant knowledge is widely dispersed, individual proposals followed by collective examination can make contributions available that a small planning group cannot supply. In the historical [Tennant–Roberts Rover study](https://doi.org/10.1016/S0024-6301(01)00039-5), interviews, a Delphi survey and workshops contributed to a revised milestone proposal before executive approval. Survey estimates informed discussion; they did not determine binding dates. The authors report dropping the Delphi stage in later iterations once shared understanding was available. Select the more involved exchange when obtaining the missing contributions warrants its cost. Use an adequate ordinary conversation or existing agreement directly when it already supplies the needed result.

**Use execution results to reconsider the undertaking.** Once work begins, compare what happened with the proposal's grounds and with the commitments still in force. A stalled contribution may need a different method, support, sequence or agreement; a changed recipient need may require revising the goal. Obtain the decision that makes the selected change effective. In [Reich's interview account](https://www.leanblog.org/2025/06/hoshin-kanri-managing-on-purpose-mark-reich/), review examines actual work and missing support, while the organization can also revise how it develops direction, gathers proposals and reviews them. Choose the review interval so that needed operating responses can occur in time. The subject of revision determines the relationship; annual and monthly meetings do not by themselves establish two control levels.

Hoshin can supply direction and provision for Improvement Kata; experiments and acquired capability can inform later proposals. [Rother's clarification](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/mike-rother-472900297_this-question-comes-up-from-time-to-time-activity-7374093405125087232-8c7X) calls for revisiting the challenge at the end of each target-condition cycle, using what has been learned. It does not justify changing direction merely because a step is difficult. Keep clear which target condition and experiment the returned results concern, and obtain the authority needed to change any affected commitment. Either practice can be sufficient without the other when the other difficulty is absent.

For the common operations, [GOALS.1, GOALS.3 and GOALS.4](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/GOAL-CONSTRUCTION-AND-REGULATION-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) supply goal meaning, joint feasibility and warranted revision. In [Leadership](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/LEADERSHIP-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md), LDR.5 develops direction and participation when they remain unresolved; LDR.2 obtains responses to a complete feasible proposal where negotiation is needed. Their results can identify a contribution that remains unsupported or state a warranted refusal. Use HCD for the development of a person's capability and C.40.JD for its connection to the method and provision, as in §6.5.6. This comparison explains Hoshin's characteristic connection of those contributions; undertaking full implementation requires the relevant subject method and its conditions.

#### Library.Reference:6.5.4 - Choose a sufficient move and return when its conditions change

Start with the result to obtain or maintain. If the method is adequate, performers can use it, information arrives in time, authority is effective and resources are available, use that arrangement. Several relationships do not by themselves require another architecture project.

For a familiar process, a short PDSA can answer a bounded change question. If the constraint on useful output is unknown, diagnosis and focusing may be needed first; a subsequent PDSA can test the selected change. If interaction changes faster than the plan remains useful, orientation and initiative become material. For a claim about a person’s ability, use relevant evidence of that person’s work with the support available for the intended use. Arrange an attempt or feedback where it contributes to learning, or where the undertaking requires that work.

For a learning question, locate the result that later work needs. A bounded lesson from an existing episode may suffice. If a person needs to acquire the operation, use work in which they can practise it with the support they need. Establish later use when transfer is at issue. If different practitioners need a reusable way, connect their experience to its development and receiving practice. If participation or authority is missing, explain how this limits the work. Seek a change through the responsible party when the undertaking includes securing that condition. Preserve current service and useful learning while obtaining the missing contribution.

Methods can contribute to one undertaking under compatible conditions. Five Focusing Steps can select the relationship on which a PDSA test focuses, while Buffer Management continues current regulation. Reuse observations when their subjects, periods and conditions fit each use. In Kata, direction can remain stable while experiments change knowledge of the way to a target condition; revising the direction is a different question.

Follow the dependency that matters to the pending decision. A qualified alternate reviewer who has access, authority and time can keep today's release on schedule under either explanation for the regular reviewer's absence. Arrange that review. If the choice of a lasting repair depends on why interruptions recur, obtain the evidence that distinguishes the explanations. A bounded change can itself supply that evidence. A proposed transfer beyond the other organization's capacity calls for a feasible agreement or a revised commitment. When the result depends on delayed effects or feedback, use the corresponding causal and dynamic inquiry. Preserve work that can proceed on the available grounds.

For the first choice, recover the required result, available conditions, an action that can obtain it and an observation that could change the next choice. If the source is a heuristic, use it to investigate those relationships and then select a method of action. Add detail where the answer does not yet permit action or warrant the required reliance. Obtain another observation when its attainable contribution justifies the full burden.

The following returns apply C.43:4.6. Use the returns for the dependencies that changed.

| Changed dependency | Useful return |
| --- | --- |
| Direction or the joint proposal remains unsettled, or a required contribution lacks the necessary agreement or authorization. | Use LDR.5 for unresolved direction or participation and LDR.2 for negotiating the whole proposal; GOALS.1/3 preserve goal meaning and joint feasibility. Use an adequate existing arrangement directly. |
| An observation is stale, concerns another subject or does not distinguish material alternatives. | Obtain usable information or choose a permissible bounded action under the remaining uncertainty; C.43:4.2. |
| The model or method no longer warrants the expected result. | Revise the explanation, select another subject method or formulate a useful inquiry; C.43:4.6. |
| Participants lack the information, authority or timing needed for a decision and its execution. | Construct and compare a different observation–decision–execution relationship; C.32.LCA:4.4–4.8. |
| Individually possible work requires incompatible use of people or means. | Change allocation, sequence, access or the promise while preserving material results; C.32.MWA.PP:4.5–4.9. |
| A need, success criterion or commitment changes. | Reconsider it with its owner and make the decision effective for affected participants; GOALS.4. |
| A useful work result is obtained, but the required development of the performer's ability is not provided for. | Connect the learner's own operation, help and later use, keeping grounds for the results separate; C.40.JD. |
| Practitioners' useful experience does not reach a reusable way that later users can obtain and apply. | Use CB.14 to recover an adequate answer or arrange the missing method contribution, qualification and receiving use; HCD supplies learning where needed. |
| The way participants learn together is obstructed by access, participation, support or a disputed decision mandate. | Use CB.4 for the interaction, LDR.1:4.5 for learning support, CB.13 for a missing shared mandate or OCE.14 for revision through an established owner. |
| An object's improvement evaluation becomes unsound. | Repair the evaluation basis and reconsider dependent conclusions while preserving the earlier result; E.23:4.1a. |
| The change opens another problem that calls for further development. | Construct subsequent work, its recipient and conditions, or conclude the present line; C.40.OD. |

A fresh observation or an already available answer can close the return. Keep the Method when it permits the needed variation. Replace it when necessary operations differ or its conditions no longer fit. Change the organization when adequate performance requires different relationships between participants and means. These decisions can combine.

A compact move is insufficient where a necessary operation, evaluation basis, feasible action or right to change a binding condition is unavailable. Identify that missing contribution and an obtainable continuation for the remaining work. Ending inquiry or retaining the current option is appropriate when no further investigation earns its burden.

#### Library.Reference:6.5.5 - A flow problem becomes a commitment problem

This constructed case shows how a changed dependency changes the useful move. A print shop must complete four accepted orders in a shift. Each needs one hour of finishing, and the finishing station has four available hours. Assume no other limiting operation or setup time; materials can be prepared beforehand. Traced episodes show the station losing time because kits arrive unready.

The required result is ready work reaching the operation that limits output. Five Focusing Steps supports focusing there. [OPS.9](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/OPERATIONS-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) uses sufficient observations already available and compares a serious alternative only when it remains live and could change the treatment. Once the grounds support a preparation or release change, the team can make it within its existing authority.

The team predicts that complete kits prepared before scheduled release will remove the observed starvation while preserving quality and available time. It tests a bounded change on suitable orders, observing release, waiting, completion and unexpected effects. [OPS.16](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/OPERATIONS-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) permits direct use of an adequate existing PDSA record.

Suppose two prepared orders finish in two hours without the former wait. The team retains the rule for that order type and tested conditions. Applying it more widely needs the corresponding provision; this small test does not establish the effect for a different order mix or unavailable preparation.

The selected methods guide focusing and studying the change. Workers prepare kits, the dispatcher decides release and the operator performs finishing. Effective agreements give the dispatcher readiness information and authority over release. An architecture account can represent these relationships and their timing conditions. Observations of performance establish what happened.

Now sales accepts two more orders for the same deadline. The dispatcher cannot change promises or purchase outside finishing. Six finishing hours are required within four available hours. Under the stated assumptions, capacity establishes the shortfall.

Return to joint commitments and authority. [OPS.19](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/OPERATIONS-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) compares permissible continuations: renegotiating a deadline, obtaining qualified capacity or reducing promised scope. Suppose an already authorized provider offers two hours of suitable finishing and can return the orders before the deadline at known cost and conditions. The entitled owner can select four internal hours and two external hours. Until that decision is effective, the dispatcher can continue permissible work and return the unsupported additional promise.

Use C.32.LCA if future work requires changed information about new commitments, authority to agree them or a provider relationship. A direct agreement among existing participants may suffice. The kit-preparation rule remains useful: the changed premise is the promised total output and its provision.

The general distinction is between changing a local release policy under available conditions and reconciling commitments with resources when load changes. The arithmetic makes one condition inspectable; the required relationship explains the return.

#### Library.Reference:6.5.6 - A useful AI answer can leave a learning goal unmet

Consider a team that wants both useful process-change decisions and development of an employee's ability to formulate a testable expectation, choose a small informative experiment and learn from a discrepancy. Assume a qualified practice already supplies the criteria for permissible experiments.

Improvement Kata connects direction, current and target conditions and experiments. Coaching Kata guides help with the learner's performance. They are useful candidates where this difficulty and qualified help are present.

The employee proposes an expectation and experiment. A helper examines the distinction the employee used, supplies relevant feedback and leaves a substantive next attempt to the employee. Operational work can use the result now. [HCD.9 in Human Capability Development](DPFs/Human%20Capability%20Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/HUMAN-CAPABILITY-DEVELOPMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) returns what the person actually performed and with what help; HCD.12 examines transfer when later work differs materially. C.40.JD connects development of the method, performer and provision. A useful experiment alone does not establish all these results.

Now an available AI can formulate the expectation, design a permissible experiment and prepare a good report. Delegation can improve immediate performance. If the person's ability remains a required result, delegating every relevant operation removes the action through which it was to be developed and observed. A higher report score does not settle that question.

In a suitable case, give the employee the problem, the permissible conditions and access to the observations. Let them state what they expect, choose an experiment whose possible outcomes matter for the team’s decision, and use the outcome to decide what to retain or change. An observation already available may settle the operational question. The employee can identify which outcome would have changed their next step, including when the evidence supports the initial expectation. A helper or AI can explain a constraint, check feasibility or give feedback. If it supplies the experiment, the employee can still interpret the result; credit the choice of experiment to whoever made it. A comparable earlier piece of the employee’s own work can already show the required choice. If the necessary time or help is unavailable, deliver the service result the team can obtain and return the remaining learning commitment to its owner.

If independent human ability is no longer required and its goal has been legitimately removed, full delegation to AI can be appropriate. If the person must later act without AI, evidence must concern that future condition. The goal and recipient of the result change the arrangement.

[GOALS.4 and GOALS.Reference:2.1](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/GOAL-CONSTRUCTION-AND-REGULATION-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) supply reconsideration of goals and the service/learning distinction; C.40.JD supplies their connection to development. Unlike the print-shop case, obtaining an external contribution may leave one of two required results unobtained. Both cases use the same general question: which relationship must hold for the required result, and what changed in it?

#### Library.Reference:6.5.7 - What this comparison supports

The comparison preserves significant relationships in selected methodological and explanatory sources. It supplies conditions for choice and combination, not a complete ranking of current cycle families. A historical source can explain an enduring distinction; a current institutional account can establish a current formulation; a bounded study can support a mechanism or measured result under its conditions. A best-known-practice claim needs a comparison for the question and conditions at hand.

The cited passages delimit the family claims. Imai's first chapter supports the maintaining/improving distinction. The Cox–Boyd treatment supplies one POOGI line; the further PASS, healthcare supply-chain, DDAE and TOC/qualitative-SD accounts support the distinct relationships and limits stated in §6.5.3.2. Boyd's original briefings support the described orientation and organizational connections. The named Rother passages supply the connected learning arrangement and the role of starter routines; HCD supplies the conditional practice design connected to it. Argyris's main text includes Models I and II; the selected Engeström–Sannino account identifies the heuristic's status. The AI papers retain their task and experimental bounds. The KCS workflow, knowledge-optimization, assessment, proficiency and coaching passages support the described connections and selected conditions; they do not establish every implementation condition or comparative advantage of the whole practice.

The AFDP 3-0.1 connection uses its 2025 doctrinal account of authority, operations and enabling conditions. It supports the stated distinction, not a universal military-derived organization for civilian work. The project application uses C.32.LCA's existing construction of goals, rights, exchanges and joint conditions.

The newer AI accounts support the particular changes and feedback relations described in §6.5.3.6. ACE, AEL, GEPA and TSRL retain their version, task and evaluation conditions; none supplies a general ranking of memory, prompts and parameter learning. The Shen–Tamkin study supplies a bounded human-learning comparison, and the Anthropic account supplies engineering experience. Selecting an intervention here follows the required contribution and the available Library methods; a claim of comparative benefit or lasting transfer needs the corresponding evidence.

The DMAIC/SDCA connection uses ASQ's current institutional description, selected NIST measurement and process-monitoring guidance, and the named statistical studies. Panahi and colleagues' linear model makes the separation of causal sensitivity and ordinary input variation explicit under its stated assumptions. An intervention consequence still requires its own causal model; an observational explained share is insufficient when common causes matter. The 2025 leveraged-sampling review supports conditional study choices, with its baseline, selection and model restrictions. These sources do not establish that one study design or variance-reduction strategy is generally best. The Kaizen Institute and TWI accounts supply methodological explanations of standards and instruction; they do not establish a universal training schedule or comparative benefit. The numerical illustration here is a constructed model calculation, not an observed improvement.

The current IHI testing, implementation and spread accounts distinguish learning from a trial, sustained use and receiving-site work. The 2026 Core Components article supplies an integrated design account and reports use, while explicitly leaving formal validation open. The 2026 QOS article explains its organizational method with cases; those cases do not isolate its comparative effect. These main articles support the connections above. Applying their detailed templates, assessment scales or curricula requires the corresponding fuller material, including supplements and books.

The [official KCS release account](https://library.serviceinnovation.org/KCS/KCS_Certification_and_Training/Knowledge-Centered_Success_Training_and_Certification) distinguishes the versionless Practices Guide released in Q2 2026 from certifications scheduled for April 2027. The source's [announced further work](https://library.serviceinnovation.org/KCS/KCS_Resources/New_in_Knowledge-Centered_Success) identifies additional AI-related and adoption guidance still to be developed.

The Hoshin account combines current methodological explanations with a historical catchball variant. The authors' proposals and experience support the described operations, not a universal estimate of implementation benefit. Its use of Rother's clarification concerns revision of the challenge; it does not establish a complete current Kata method. An A3 or X-matrix format is useful where it makes the needed relationships intelligible. The subject method and its conditions still need to fit the work.

The *Toyota Kata Practice Guide* account uses its 2018 electronic text (ISBN 9781259861031): Introduction, chapters 1–12, Conclusion and the working forms ground the process and coaching applications above. The [publisher's 2022 listing](https://www.mheducation.com/highered/mhp/product/toyota-kata-practice-guide-rother.html) calls the book a first edition, while the linked author excerpt identifies itself as a 2022 second edition; the full revised text of that second edition is outside this account. *Toyota Kata Culture* (2017), chapter 4, supplies the horizontal coordination account and its constructed Acme cases; its Introduction, chapter 2 feedback passage, chapter 3 and Conclusion supply the connected roles, fact return and deployment account. These methodological explanations and illustrations support the described ways of working, without estimating their comparative effect. Rosenthal's 2025 account is a practitioner's methodological clarification, with limited DMAIC experience stated by its author. Aulinger's 2026 learning-group guide supplies the selected organizational variant and its proposed coaching and support operations.

The organizational-learning sources have different explanatory and prescriptive reach. The selected Kolb, Senge, SECI and action-learning passages support the connections in §6.5.3.5. Restrepo and colleagues' 2014 paper supplies a methodological synthesis of collaborative learning from theory and case reports; the DITSL brief describes one realization. The circular-organizing and urban-water studies retain their case boundaries. Tosey and colleagues distinguish meanings of triple-loop learning. The full [Kwon–Kang–Nicolaides review](https://doi.org/10.1108/TLO-01-2024-0030), published in the 2026 issue, synthesizes 23 studies found through a search last updated in December 2022. It identifies themes in accounts of learning processes and outcomes. Its selected excerpts and differing conceptions do not supply a common effect measure, a validated sequence of three loops or a comparative ranking.

Use the fuller subject source when the selected method needs further operations or conditions. New evidence can change a family boundary, an arrangement or the preferable method. Unavailable evidence leaves the claim that depends on it open while other adequately grounded choices remain usable.

## Library.Reference:7 - How the language makes connected work intelligible

The Library connects understanding with what to do and what to develop next. Its pattern languages are declarative: their order of publication does not prescribe a project process. A current question selects a relevant contribution; a dependency requires another result only when the selected operation needs it. Existing adequate results can let the reader enter farther along the connection.

A supplying Method returns something of an identified kind, a receiving operation uses it, and the conditions on that result remain available at the join. A model with an error bound may support one decision but not another. A proposed contribution needs agreement and authority before it can be relied on as a commitment. An observed successful run can support a bounded performance claim while leaving transfer or independent capability open.

Establish what the recipient needs to understand or do and what would suffice. [FPF C.39](FPF-Spec.md#c39---find-and-develop-a-way-to-obtain-a-result) develops a missing way to obtain the result. [FPF E.4.CM](FPF-Spec.md#e4cm---develop-connected-methods-as-framework-contributions) distinguishes a missing Method or connection from an adequate one whose explanation or access needs repair. A reference can direct the reader to a full pattern that supplies the operation. When teaching a general Method, explain what to do, when it applies and why its result answers the question or supports the work that follows. Use the account directly when it suffices. [EXD.2:4.1](DPFs/EXPLANATION-DESIGN-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#exd241---select-the-relation-and-a-discriminating-instance) helps choose a case that exposes a needed step or distinguishes two plausible rules. Show which choice in the case enacts the Method. If a case reveals a missing condition, establish where it applies and carry it back into the general explanation.

To author a reusable pattern, carry the intended practitioner’s work through the explanation and, where they are issued, the assignment and its criterion. Someone asked to devise a Method needs grounds for choosing a fitting operation or arrangement. Where the result depends on several contributions, show how they work together. An assignment that supplies that design can teach someone to use it. Decide which of these the original question entrusted to this reader. [Pattern authoring (E.8:4.2.1.3)](FPF-Spec.md#e84213---choose-examples-by-the-explanation-they-make-usable)

An example can itself be a disciplinary model. When the reader is to apply a transdisciplinary principle, explain how it supports a conclusion in the receiving discipline. If the reader must construct that interpretation independently, leave that construction to them in the task used to assess their work. When the reader must design a procedure, identify the decisions that were already settled in the worked case. Recover the grounds for making those decisions through [EXD.Reference:3](DPFs/EXPLANATION-DESIGN-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#exdreference3---obtain-and-carry-the-subject-explanation). [EXD.6:4.1](DPFs/EXPLANATION-DESIGN-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#exd641---fix-the-receiving-question-and-its-criterion) compares what the author was asked to teach with what the reader is actually asked to do. [NSTD.8:4.1](DPFs/Narrativization-and-Narrative-Studies-Principles-Framework.md#nstd841---check-what-the-learner-must-do-next-and-what-help-is-available) makes the same comparison across a learning narrative.

Connected use also depends on shared meanings across publications. [FPF.Reference:9](FPF-Spec.md#fpfreference9---thinking-through-writing) explains how writing can develop and revise the needed understanding; [FPF.Reference:10](FPF-Spec.md#fpfreference10---thinking-oriented-architecture-not-a-descriptive-upper-ontology) explains the choice between direct assertions, classification and ontological coordination. Apply these grounds when a result or definition crosses publication boundaries.

For such a use, recover which identity, characteristic or relation each contribution relies on. Resolve a consequential difference through its defining pattern. A DPF can add domain conditions for using a Core result; retain those conditions with the receiving use. Shared spelling or a common card layout cannot establish that two local meanings agree. Written comparisons can expose the difference and preserve the grounds of a qualified connection. Keep a sufficient direct use instead of creating another shared definition merely because several publications are involved.

When a common rule changes, reconsider the dependent claims in the publications that use that rule, while preserving uses whose grounds still hold. A layout change can preserve the meanings and grounds needed by the receiver; omitting a condition or rationale can leave that use without a recoverable basis. The receiving explanation should make the dependency and the useful return clear, with the common definition maintained at its subject pattern.

There is also a connection within ongoing work. Checking the units in a calculation can participate in obtaining a model result, comparing a design and supporting a larger engineering decision at the same moment. The encompassing purpose determines which distinctions the check must preserve. [FPF B.1.5.EW](FPF-Spec.md#b15ew---recover-how-constituent-actions-enact-encompassing-work) recovers this constituent-to-whole connection; [FPF B.1.5.RS](FPF-Spec.md#b15rs---evaluate-a-method-replacement-in-its-encompassing-uses) examines a proposed replacement in its encompassing uses. A missing intermediate capability or unavailable shared resource can defeat the whole even when elementary operations are individually available.

Advice connects an answer to work for its recipient. Following it can require action or inquiry, delay other work and consume means. [FPF C.11.DUA](FPF-Spec.md#c11dua---make-advice-and-evidence-demands-worth-their-burden) recovers that demanded work and relates three questions: what the present basis supports, what the recipient can reasonably do with it, and what additional inquiry could improve that use. A limitation on a claim does not by itself require a particular investigation. Equally, the difficulty of obtaining evidence cannot strengthen the claim. Give the supported answer and the continuation available to its recipient.

Compare following the advice with keeping the present way and with feasible narrower uses or smaller changes. Include the recipient's attention, learning and adaptation, coordination and execution, and what is delayed, displaced or put at risk. When proposed changes rely on the same means, compare their combined demand with what remains available alongside ongoing commitments. Judge the gain at the horizon of the intended result. New knowledge can itself be that result, before an operational intervention is selected. Use the domain research Method to establish how attainable inquiry could produce the knowledge or new possibility sought.

[FPF C.11.CRC](FPF-Spec.md#c11crc---configuration-relative-contribution-comparison) compares what would happen with and without a proposed change over the same period under the same external scenarios. Include the transition, later options and who would obtain each benefit or bear each burden. The decision holder can use C.11 to choose among the feasible options; C.19.2 limits the effort of applying apparatus under the required result and guarantee. A cheaper route that drops that guarantee is a different proposal. DUA can appraise a requirement's purpose and burden; a binding requirement continues to govern until the relevant authority or agreement changes it. Inquiry can matter even when the physical action stays the same, because it changes the supported claim or admissible use.

Passing a use condition can admit a second option while the comparison between the two remains open. For a Pareto front, [FPF C.18](FPF-Spec.md#c18---open-ended-search-archive-and-front-stewardship) asks which eligible rivals could dominate each option on the chosen characteristics. Sometimes an advantage already established for each option settles the trade-off. Suppose three bidders each submit one eligible service bid and all chosen characteristics except cost are equal. The third bid is 100. The other two are 80 and 120 in the same currency, but their price labels were mixed up. Under either assignment of those prices, the third bid is dominated. The sole nondominated price is already known to be 80. Awarding the work at 80 requires identifying the bidder. If a fourth bidder submits one eligible bid known only to cost at least 80 and all other comparison characteristics remain equal, the price answer still holds. The fourth bidder could tie at 80 and would then belong on a list of every efficient bidder. A certified lower bound above 80 excludes that bidder from the list. When the evidence permits both membership and exclusion, name what remains open. A team may keep an option for a later use while its place on the front is still undecided.

When observations, conditional future choices and realization still need to be connected, [FPF C.43](FPF-Spec.md#c43---connect-observation-choice-and-action-in-control) supplies that broader control construction. Explain which possible observation would change which later action, whether that action will remain available, and how the selected continuation can take effect. DUA appraises advice and demanded work within this construction. Changed consequences can make an earlier bounded action inadequate; lost access or time can make a previously useful inquiry unavailable. Reconsider the dependent continuation and preserve the uses whose grounds remain sound.

Compare arrangements that can provide the result the recipient needs. Identify who can supply each contribution, who is authorized to carry out any required act, and who will combine the results. A supplied result and the recipient's own mastery are different goals. [FPF C.32.MWA.PP](FPF-Spec.md#c32mwapp---construct-and-compare-method-performance-and-provision-arrangements) compares complete performance and provision, including coordination, checking, waiting and correction. Assignment and permission remain separate from capability. The Library supplies ways to reason about the arrangement; the actual situation supplies its facts, powers and available performers.

Finish the present undertaking when its intended result and the basis for its receiving use are sufficient, including an explicit limit or justified stop where needed. That result can also change what becomes worth attempting. [FPF C.40.OD](FPF-Spec.md#c40od---construct-and-continue-open-ended-development) connects development of knowledge, Methods, capability and provision; [FPF C.40.NP](FPF-Spec.md#c40np---continue-from-a-result-to-new-attainable-problems) uses the changed position to construct a further worthwhile attainable problem. Conducting one's own development additionally requires the ability to notice a consequential new difficulty or possibility and organize the next transition. [Section 9](#libraryreference9---open-ended-development-belongs-across-these-fields) develops these connections across the Library. A bounded undertaking can finish while its result supports that wider continuation.

### Library.Reference:7.1 - When a way works in the small but fails in the large

An analyst's personal tool can work because the analyst interprets its inputs, supplies missing context, checks unusual answers and decides what to do. Asking several units to use the visible calculation autonomously changes that arrangement. More examples of the successful calculation do not establish the meanings, provision, authority or recovery that the new use needs.

“In the small” and “in the large” are useful search expressions for this difficulty, not two sizes to which every Method belongs. One author can create dependencies that require coordinated change; several performers can work independently when the necessary conditions are provided. A larger batch for the same analyst can leave the original way sufficient. Begin with C.39:4.1 to recover what the receiving work needs and what the earlier success depended on. Its remaining Solution constructs the missing contribution and joins it to the useful whole.

If several offices report using the same practice, first establish what the organization needs to decide. For the number now using it or for a change among continuing teams, [IS.Reference:6](https://fpf.tools/IS.Reference:6) works from the eligible offices, the work they performed and the period the records cover. To design the enquiry yourself, try the assignment in [IS.Application:1.4](https://fpf.tools/IS.Application:1.4) on a commission of your own. A report for one office can establish a particular use. For evidence that another office took a method from it, see Library.Reference:9.3.1 and C.36:4. For a decision about keeping the service, OCE.13 and CB.10 help trace what reached the affected recipients, whether they could use it in time, and who bore the cost. A current coverage threshold can often be settled before the origin or benefit of the practice is known.

The Library keeps the following questions with different fields because their first useful results differ. Use the row that fits the difficulty.

| Difficulty in the receiving work | Read for the needed contribution |
| --- | --- |
| A faster local operation leaves the accepted output unchanged or increases waiting elsewhere. | OPS.9 follows accepted output, waiting and resource occupancy to the current constraint. B.1.5.EW recovers how the constituent action participates in encompassing work; B.1.5.RS examines a proposed replacement in those uses. |
| Independently supplied components or versions do not make one usable configuration. | SYSE.11 develops and qualifies integration for the system's bounded use. For digital executable and documentary components, SYSE.53 selects a compatible configuration. For software and persistent data, SYSE.34 develops coexistence, transition and recovery when versions cannot change together. |
| Contributors need to vary their work without repeatedly involving a central expert. | SYSE.27 derives the extension boundary from the intended independent variation and supplies a reproducible contribution, examples and repair. SIE.12 provides the common semantics needed while preserving local models; SIE.7 uses that agreement in implementation. |
| The same identifiers or formats conceal different meanings. | SIE.3 constructs sufficient distinctions, SIE.4 qualifies correspondence and substitution, and SIE.5 distinguishes identity from other relations. A.15.9 obtains a missing result from the appropriate practice and uses it within the supported conditions. |
| Data variants and operations have been extended separately, but their new combination is undefined. | CMP.15 constructs the missing semantic intersection and a composition that reaches it, including nested uses. CMP.12 supplies the interpretation being extended; CMP.14 addresses interaction when state or continuing execution changes the observations. |
| A person can perform the parts separately. Can they perform the whole task with the required support or independence? If the combination failed, what explains the difficulty? If a change is wanted, what could restore coordination? | HCD.3 distinguishes a capability gap from missing conditions or interference. An external service may supply the required result. HCD.11 assesses the person’s own contribution with the help the task permits, identifying the help actually used. HCD.12 compares that contribution before and after a meaningful change, using suitable existing performances or a transfer test. If the person needs practice coordinating the actions, use HCD.9 for the combined task and feedback. HCD.10 helps choose whether and how to vary the practice for later work. |
| A workable plan depends on actors who have not agreed, or on a community treated as a permanent project team. | ECO.3 and ECO.4 construct feasible proposals and commitments; LDR.2 obtains the responses needed for joint contributions. CB.4 and CB.9 organize participation and maintenance under the community's terms. Technical compatibility supplies none of those agreements. |
| What conclusion do these sources support together? | Use F.0.2 to establish what the source claims allow separately, which assumptions connect them and what follows from that connection. A result can remain warranted even when the accounts disagree on a distinction the present question leaves open. For a body of research evidence, [RMP.7](DPFs/RESEARCH-METHOD-PRACTICE-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#rmp7---synthesize-a-research-evidence-corpus) considers which findings share data and how far each applies. State what they jointly establish and where conclusions still differ. If an analysis is disputed, RMP.5 examines its grounds. RMP.6 uses existing evidence to address a credibility concern or helps choose a worthwhile test. |
| Dividing work among contributors risks losing a shared premise, a required authorization or the ability to combine their results. | SYSE.44 helps compare feasible divisions, including keeping the work together when one performer can meet all requirements. C.32.MWA.PP compares complete performance and provision, including checking and correction. KCAE.SEARCH and KCAE.DELIVER obtain and supply sufficient material; SYSE.52 chooses what the next operation must receive. |
| A changed technology may remove an old constraint or open a different contribution. | STR.17 investigates technological change separately from public attention. STR.5 uses a qualified possibility to develop a direction; STR.12 reconsiders an existing commitment when its grounds change. |
| A useful prototype is proposed for a use with different consequences. | C.39 identifies the result needed by the changed use. SYSE.14 develops an engineering release decision with its evidence and authority. For software, SYSE.35 and SYSE.36 qualify bounded exposure and comparison, and SYSE.34 supplies practiced recovery. C.11.DUA helps choose additional inquiry by what it can change, while applicable obligations retain their force. |

An operational model built from documents may serve a person planning work, an interpreter executing instructions or a reviewer establishing what a rule prescribes. Start with the work the model must support and which distinctions would change its outcome. A.3.1.DR recovers the prescribed actions and conditions. Use SIE.2 to recover what each source means under its governing terms. Use SIE.3 to develop a model where the receiving work needs a distinction the available model leaves open. Use NOT.1–NOT.4 to design or rewrite the expression and recover its interpretation. Changing the governing rule requires the authority to change it. Under a rule already in force, a particular permission may take effect or end through a grant, a withdrawal or a condition such as expiry. Establish the scope, effective time and authorized act or condition for the permission in question. To correct the model of an existing permission, use the applicable rule and the relevant facts of that case. A revised prediction needs grounds in the modeled process or in the inference from its premises. CMP.12 supplies a computational interpreter when one is required. KCAE.SOURCE preserves access to the original material. When several separately governed sources are needed, SIE.13 connects the conditions under which their contributions support the same answer. For a question about what happened, use evidence of the action or result in that instance. A record of the state reached may settle the outcome while leaving open whether a dispatcher chose the response or a service applied a standing rule. Recover the responsible performer when the answer depends on that difference. The governing text alone can settle what the rule prescribes.

Subject expertise can remain an external contribution. For a production ML change, the general engineering Methods do not teach every test of the relation between an offline score and live effects, differences between training and serving features, or quality loss as a model and its inputs age. Use the external [Google production-ML monitoring guidance](https://developers.google.com/machine-learning/crash-course/production-ml-systems/monitoring) as an entry to that specialist work. Obtain the results needed for the system, with their limits, before relying on them; an unresolved result can narrow or stop the affected change.

Return each contribution to the original receiving question. If stable inputs, an explicit interpretation and an adequate recovery arrangement restore independent local work, use that arrangement. If only more provision is needed, retain the sufficient Method. If a promised operation is still unwritten, C.39 develops it. The different DPFs keep their own subjects while contributing to this common question, without acquiring additional Suite memberships.

### Library.Reference:7.2 - From available information to useful questions and possible actions

An engineer, manager or researcher may know that some understanding is needed while being unable to say which answer would change the work. [B.5:4.1.2](FPF-Spec.md#b5412---find-useful-questions-and-possible-actions-from-incomplete-information) explains how to examine accessible material, propose an account of the subject and derive questions or possible actions from it. Its inputs can be ordinary material already at hand; its result can be a better question, a conditional explanation or a justified next attempt. It does not require a complete account of the future.

Start by examining something that can make the subject less opaque: an actual episode and the use of its result, an available artifact and the operation that produced it, an explanatory source or a proposed capability tried in a possible use. [B.5.PI](FPF-Spec.md#b5pi---initiate-inquiry-from-work) obtains a question from ordinary work before a problem is felt. [F.1](FPF-Spec.md#f1---find-and-select-sources-for-a-current-question) helps seek sources even when the vocabulary is unfamiliar; [A.15.9](FPF-Spec.md#a159---use-or-request-a-bounded-result-from-another-practice) obtains help with a formulation as well as a formulated question.

Determine what the encounter already establishes for the question. If the answer is incomplete, use what you learned to build a provisional account. A participant's demonstration can reveal the unit they need to distinguish. An operation can reveal a premise it silently relies on. A new capability can suggest a use not included in the original aim. [B.5.FM](FPF-Spec.md#b5fm---construct-a-model-for-the-question) connects the relevant participants through a subject relation and derives a small consequence. Reasoning from intended use toward needed means can change how a fixed question will be answered. Reasoning from an available means toward a new use can instead suggest a different question. Take up that direction if choosing it belongs to the inquiry. Where a recipient still needs the original answer, connect the finding to that answer or explain what remains open. A scheme or mantra can direct this examination toward a consequential relation before anyone complains, but its questions still need subject content.

Follow the relation to the consequence the question requires. Construct a contrast when it can reveal a distinction that still matters. If an operation requires a premise, examine what follows when the premise differs. If a representation merges two cases, try to derive whether their receiving uses can differ. If an attempt exposes an unavailable contribution, recover what it supplied and seek another way to obtain that result. These operations can produce a new possibility, a reason to change the question or a limit.

The attendance-report case in B.5:5.8 illustrates this move for a course leader deciding whom to contact. Reading the grouping operation and asking a leader to demonstrate the use exposes a distinction between a participant and a displayed name. Constructing two cases with the same report but different people reveals what that report cannot decide. A documented identifier then supplies a candidate repair. The relevant alternatives were obtained through the source operation and its use. The case illustrates a way to find a consequential distinction. A claim about how all course leaders use their reports would need grounds about those uses.

Once a distinction has been constructed, ask which further inquiries or actions it supports. Keep an action, an uncertain condition, a consequence under that condition and a later action chosen after an observation distinguishable. [C.39](FPF-Spec.md#c39---find-and-develop-a-way-to-obtain-a-result) develops an absent operation or a way to connect operations; [C.40.CD](FPF-Spec.md#c40cd---develop-problems-and-ways-of-solving-them-together) allows the question and the way to change together. [C.43](FPF-Spec.md#c43---connect-observation-choice-and-action-in-control) connects the observations and choices of a control arrangement. These constructions preserve jointly necessary contributions and sufficient alternatives; they do not require every inquiry to become a branching plan.

Establish what the new distinction enables the receiving agent to understand or obtain. Explain what changes in the result, supported claim, admissible use or research direction. [C.11.DUA](FPF-Spec.md#c11dua---make-advice-and-evidence-demands-worth-their-burden) then compares further inquiry with using the present basis, a smaller sufficient contribution or stopping. Include assistance, access, learning and delay. The same physical action can remain appropriate while the claim supporting it changes. Conversely, a discovered possibility can justify constructing a new action instead of refining a prediction over old options.

Unavailable help changes which inquiry or action can be carried out. In the report case, an analyst can derive the grouping limitation and prepare a repair while waiting for the data owner's qualification. Neither act establishes what the absent owner would have said or permitted. Keep the dependent claim conditional, use another qualified source if available, or defer that part. Preserve independent work that still contributes. When the result sought is the practitioner's own mastery, an externally supplied answer alone does not fulfil it.

Open research has a different sufficient result from selecting an intervention. The measurement case in B.5:5.9 begins with unexplained traces and inspection of their production; it obtains a previously unrecognized reference operation and a question that can distinguish explanations. The initial exploration was justified by access to the measurement process and an attainable kind of contribution, not a promise to discover that operation. Its later candidate explanation still needs appropriate evidence. A mathematical inquiry can likewise produce a new representation or an obstruction before an application is selected.

Re-examine a provisional account when a finding contradicts it or exposes a distinction needed for the intended use. If help is unavailable, seek another way to obtain its contribution and carry the remaining uncertainty into the answer. A new possibility can warrant a proposal to change the aim. Pursue it directly when choosing the aim is part of the work. Otherwise return what the inquiry established for the existing aim and explain what accepting the new one would change. A sufficient result can finish the present inquiry.

## Library.Reference:8 - A worked connection across the Library

The following constructed case illustrates how these contributions fit together.

Three materials laboratories must decide how to report instrument measurements for a new class of samples. Their calculation works within its validated range, but results for the new class disagree. The directors want dependable reports for the tests due this week and a way for their own practitioners to investigate future unfamiliar cases independently. They are considering better access to sources, an external specialist, a shared office and local support. Which arrangement can provide both results?

### Library.Reference:8.1 - Different contributions to one useful result

Start with the two uses. For a report due this week, establish what can be concluded from that sample's record in time to issue the report. For future cases, establish what the local practitioner needs to recognize, choose and infer with the tools normally available. A specialist may supply the entire first result and still leave the local choice untried. A good local proposal for investigating the disagreement may in turn leave this week's test unresolved.

[Decision Design (PSDA)](DPFs/DECISION-DESIGN-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) helps construct the question and compare alternatives without assuming at the outset that the answer is an office or a course. [Research Method Practice (RMP)](DPFs/RESEARCH-METHOD-PRACTICE-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) supplies inquiry when existing knowledge cannot explain the disagreement. If the issue is a model premise, [Foundational Thinking](DPFs/Foundational%20Thinking%20DPF%20Suite/) connects physical explanation, model correspondence and calculation. A faster or more accurate computation of the same inadequate model does not resolve that premise.

For example, one laboratory has a dated peak reading and another has the last value read after the instrument settled. To decide whether the specimen ever exceeded a limit during the test, check whether either record already answers. One reading tied to this specimen and test establishes an exceedance if its lower error bound lies above the limit. To certify compliance throughout, an upper bound must cover the physical maximum, including any motion between samples. A peak indicator with suitable response and a justified error bound can supply that result even when read later. Use both records when their verified coverage together bounds the complete interval, when the order asks for their comparison, or when a conflict between relevant readings needs resolving. If there is an uncovered interval, establish a bound for it before claiming compliance throughout. To decide only whether the two laboratories followed the agreed intervention, their records of performing the prescribed work may already suffice. [Foundational.Reference:7.3](https://fpf.tools/Foundational.Reference:7.3)

When deciding whether a local practitioner reached a conclusion independently, establish what they could use at the time. Suppose the first laboratory recorded a discrepant reading on Tuesday, decided on Wednesday and circulated the reading on Thursday. [Narrative order (NSTD.2)](https://fpf.tools/NSTD.2:4) and [participant viewpoint (NSTD.4)](https://fpf.tools/NSTD.4:4) help reconstruct when a reading or a specialist's interpretation reached each decision holder. A receipt can establish access by a date. For a claim of unassisted judgement, check the other relevant channels and examine the practitioner's own reasoning. A late circulation date alone establishes neither what caused the measurement disagreement nor who worked out its explanation.

The resulting explanation changes what must be obtained. If a usable method already exists and the only defect is access, [Implementation Facilitation (IS)](DPFs/IMPLEMENTATION-FACILITATION-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) can supply the missing help. If the desired result is each practitioner's independent judgement, external answers alone are insufficient; [Human Capability Development (HCD)](DPFs/Human%20Capability%20Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/HUMAN-CAPABILITY-DEVELOPMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) supplies the relevant learning and capability qualification. When organizational contributions or authority must change, [Organization Change Engineering (OCE)](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/ORGANIZATION-CHANGE-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) constructs that realization while ongoing obligations remain in view.

For the shared investigation, trace the two reports to the measurements on which they rely. They could reproduce the same reference reading and share an error that matters to the proposed explanation. Independent judgements about that one reading could still fulfil a request for two independent reviews. [EXD.Reference:3](https://fpf.tools/EXD.Reference:3) helps explain which distinction affects the laboratory's decision. To obtain the total from lists, establish what population they cover and whether they use the same inclusion rule. Count a shared sample once. Partial lists alone give a count only for their union. If the sites cover the whole population in disjoint groups and their counts use that common rule, the verified counts add directly. One adequate record can settle the question about a sample. A second report that reproduces it adds no independent measurement.

The office alternatives concern how these transformations will be performed and supported. [FPF C.32.MWA.PP](FPF-Spec.md#c32mwapp---construct-and-compare-method-performance-and-provision-arrangements) compares their complete performance and provision. A common office for several masteries is an alternative when the same contribution can serve them without erasing their different criteria, and the gain from sharing exceeds the extra coordination and interpretation burden. Specialized development support has a case when it supplies methodological inquiry that each workplace lacks while receiving subject expertise from the laboratories. Local leadership, distributed specialists or external provision can be sufficient when those arrangements supply the whole needed contribution under attainable conditions.

[STR.16](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/STRATEGY-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#str16---construct-a-feasible-portfolio-with-shared-or-branch-specific-support) compares shared and branch-specific support while preserving each branch's purpose. [LDR.2](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/LEADERSHIP-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#ldr2---agree-feasible-joint-contributions) obtains responses to a feasible joint proposal. Where independent groups require a common mandate, [CB.13](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/COMMUNITY-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#cb13---establish-a-workable-mandate-for-shared-decisions) supplies that contribution; the participants retain powers they have not delegated.

Suppose the first laboratory's calibrated peak record settles the question on its sample. The other two each need a specialist determination before their own reporting deadlines. One specialist has eight hours available. All five hours of each determination, including preparation, are specific to its sample and cannot serve the other determination. Either fits alone and the pair needs ten hours. If a second qualified practitioner can take one entire determination in its available window and both have timely access to their samples and records, each can complete one. Under the original conditions, scheduling both jobs within the same eight hours or sharing administration leaves the shortfall. A genuinely shared calibration changes the calculation. Two common hours plus three hours per sample require eight hours in all. One specialist can then cover both if the access and timing allow it. An authorized change to the deadlines can also alter the choice. Establish who can provide the required hours before relying on the arrangement.

For a guarantee about all three laboratories, establish that their conditions can hold together and that one proposed arrangement meets each laboratory’s promised use in every allowed situation. If an existing comparison establishes that every allowed explanation of the discrepancy leads to the same decision today, the adviser can finish the recommendation while its precise cause is open. The party authorized to commit the affected laboratories can decide. A decision can wait if each possible observation leaves the authorized holder time to take an action that meets the brief. The action can differ with the observation. If a guarantee was promised, the action chosen for an observation must work in every situation still consistent with it. Check access and deadlines for each branch, including any advance booking. A service allowed to miss an agreed fraction of deadlines needs evidence about that fraction or a sufficient bound.

### Library.Reference:8.2 - What a changed condition sends back

If the new specimen class defeats a model assumption, revise the assumption and the conclusions that depend on it. If the model is sound but the specialist withdraws, reconsider how the laboratories will obtain the service and what they can promise. Successful diagnoses show what the service delivered. To judge a practitioner's ability to investigate unfamiliar cases, examine their own work under the required conditions. If the records contain only the specialist's diagnoses, the practitioner's ability remains open. For a sound finding that a laboratory cannot use, [EXD.Reference:2](DPFs/EXPLANATION-DESIGN-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md#exdreference2---the-distinctions-that-change-the-work) helps identify what the practitioner needs. They may need access to the calibration table or an explanation of how its values justify a correction. If evidence identifies a skill the practitioner needs to develop, arrange practice in that skill.

Give local practitioners the records and ordinary tools for a suitable unfamiliar case. Ask them to develop and use their own grounds before disclosing the specialist's interpretation. If the records settle the discrepancy, a reasoned inference can be enough. If the conclusion needed for the report holds under every remaining explanation, give that conclusion. If the cause matters and several explanations still fit, the practitioner can choose and justify an attainable check that would distinguish them. When the sample is gone and all available records fit both causes, a justified account of what remains unknown and which observation would have distinguished them can be the right result. A separate case can be used when withholding help on today's sample would delay a report. If the only commission is to design the support service, the specialist may supply a diagnosis and the local lead may still choose and justify the service.

Return to the directors' joint choice. With the qualified local record and two timely specialist determinations, the three laboratories have a way to settle this week's reports. An external service doing all the reasoning leaves the second aim open. Retaining a practitioner at each site with time, access and responsibility for the initial judgement makes room for independent investigation. Check that the time and resources for practice are available alongside the reporting work. If those conditions hold, the directors can select that arrangement for both aims. Whether reports were delivered depends on the actual reports and deadlines. Whether practitioners can handle another unfamiliar case depends on their decisions in such work, including which help they used.

Once the practice works adequately, one branch can finish. The new understanding may reveal a harder but attainable problem, such as recognizing an unfamiliar failure before it affects a measurement. [FPF C.40.NP](FPF-Spec.md#c40np---continue-from-a-result-to-new-attainable-problems) develops that continuation. If several independent practitioners need to reconstruct and adapt the method, [FPF C.36.RP](FPF-Spec.md#c36rp---sustain-and-renew-shared-ways-of-working) supplies cultural retention and renewal, with [Community Management (CB)](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/COMMUNITY-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) where participation or common work needs organizing. Neither a permanent office nor an endlessly open project is required by that continuation.

The same architecture can serve a question that has no laboratory or system-realization goal. A mathematical investigation may finish with a warranted construction; a narrative may finish with a source-faithful account suited to its purpose.

## Library.Reference:9 - Open-ended development belongs across these fields

Open-ended development expands knowledge, understanding and reproducible ways of obtaining results, then uses that changed starting position to open further worthwhile and attainable problems. The novelty can be new to a person, organization or community without being new to humanity. An adequate knowledge contribution can matter before all prospective applications have been realized.

The general connection is developed in [FPF C.40.OD](FPF-Spec.md#c40od---construct-and-continue-open-ended-development), [FPF C.40.JD](FPF-Spec.md#c40jd---develop-a-way-its-holder-and-its-provision-together) and [FPF C.40.NP](FPF-Spec.md#c40np---continue-from-a-result-to-new-attainable-problems). They connect a consequential difficulty, problematisation, a useful direction, construction of a way of working, preparation of a capable performer and the means it needs, application and the next possibility. They keep the branch that supplies an immediate result distinct from the branch that develops a performer's capability and the branch that develops the Method itself.

That construction is transdisciplinary. It can concern a person, organization, AI system or distributed cultural carriers while requiring different mechanisms in each case. HCD explains human practice, feedback and transfer. OCE explains organizational realization and authority. RMP explains research. Community Management explains participation where a common undertaking exists. An executable learning system also needs its own challenge representations, learning and transfer mechanisms; the general development account is not their implementation.

The general construction remains incomplete for a concrete use until its needed subject mechanisms are supplied. Keeping those mechanisms in their domain languages preserves their distinct premises, evidence and revision. Putting the general connection inside one domain framework would make a common Method depend on that field. Keeping only scattered citations would leave the reader to invent how a sufficient result opens the next problem. The selected architecture develops the common connection in Core and retains its subject-specific suppliers.

Repeated delivery, a new version or a higher current score alone does not establish this continuation. The useful claim concerns the growth obtained, the problems it enables and the conditions supporting further development. A bounded service or project can end while its result supports that wider practice.

Sections [9.2](#libraryreference92---help-enablement-and-the-organization-of-professional-support) and [9.3](#libraryreference93---acquiring-sustaining-and-changing-a-shared-humanai-professional-culture) develop the help, learning and cultural relations within this wider account. They explain both a sufficient current contribution and the conditions for continuing development.

### Library.Reference:9.1 - Open-ended development through connected contributions

Development can begin with a faint difficulty in ordinary work, a possibility that changes what is worth attempting, or a newly noticed limit after success. The problem, the way forward and the means of development can all need construction. The Methods connected by this account help those undertaking the work connect the necessary contributions and continue from the position reached. It also retains independently useful opportunity inquiry and advice: a bounded useful result can finish such an undertaking without pretending that development itself is complete.

The developed subject, the provider of help and the chooser of a commitment need not be the same. Self-development is the branch in which the subject itself makes the relevant contribution to conducting its own development. An externally supplied improvement does not establish that contribution. A technical or non-agent subject can develop under another performer's work without being assigned agency by analogy.

#### Library.Reference:9.1.1 - One connected practice and independently useful contributions

The connected question is how a worthwhile change becomes attainable and actual and how the result opens further development. C.40.OD connects current work, problematisation, choice of direction and Method, performer preparation, provision, work and the new problem made attainable by its result. STR.16 addresses independently worthwhile undertakings that compete for shared means or support. The opportunity and advice branches below also have useful results of their own.

**Connected formulation.** Characterize the situation. Formulate a worthwhile, attainable problem. State what would count as solving it and how to check a proposed solution against that criterion. Find or develop alternatives, compare them and secure the means for the selected work. Carry out the work and check its effect. Use the changed position to judge which problem is now worthwhile and attainable, or whether to stop. When the limiting factor is the way problems are formulated or solved, improve that way and test it on a subsequent occasion.

If two alternatives obtain the same current result, compare the next worthwhile change each makes attainable. Name what has to survive, which operations and resources can be used and how the next performer would receive the result. A proposal may fail to appear, be discarded during evaluation or lack the means for a first trial. C.40:4.7 and :4.11 locate these different constraints. C.40.OD:4.9 connects the resulting opportunity to a formulated problem and the work that could address it.

Use the connected formulation to locate the work that would change the present situation. Start with an episode, a newly available possibility or evidence from a previous attempt. Establish whose work or knowledge is at stake. Choose characteristics that show what matters for the affected work or inquiry, what is already attainable and what would count as improvement under these conditions. Formulate alternative problems when different purposes or observations warrant them. Use the chosen problem, its conditions and a way to recognize success in the search for a solution. Pass them on if another performer takes up the search. Receiving that formulation is a useful result even when no one yet knows how to solve the problem.

A practitioner can use an established Method, seek help or try several different constructions. A trial may reveal a better way or expose a premise that changes the problem itself. Compare feasible alternatives under common conditions. A way that does at least as well on every selected criterion and better on one dominates its competitor. Apply the governing selection rule or obtain the choice from its authorized holder. Establish the resource commitments the chosen work needs. The party that allocates capacity may be different from the one entrusted with choosing the Method. When a new characteristic matters, compare again on that basis. A choice can support several trials, authorize one undertaking or end a branch. Once the way is known, the performers can take on tasks whose results can be checked. They carry out the work and examine what its recipient can actually use. For a research problem, an argument or an observation that settles a claim can be the intended result.

Return the evidence to the performer or decision-maker able to act on it. An unsuccessful attempt may call for another way, a different problem or a stop. After success, establish what has become attainable, with whose help and who can take the next initiative. For self-development, the developing agent must make and act on a further choice. A recurring failure to formulate problems, generate alternatives, check a result or secure the means can itself become the problem to work on. Judge the change in that practice by what it enables on later occasions. Enter this account wherever a needed contribution is missing and use the results already established.

Identify what changed in this episode. A system can now do something under conditions that previously defeated it. An episteme supports a new claim or inference about its subject. A change in a Method can make a new result reproducible, extend the conditions of its use or lower the cost of obtaining an established result. A discipline changes what practitioners can establish and do through its organized knowledge and practice. Use comparable evidence for the earlier and current conditions to establish what changed and which further problem, if any, is now within reach. To attribute the change to particular work, establish how that work contributed. To credit a particular contributor, identify what that contributor did. A change in capability can be established while the author of the change remains unknown. For self-development, show which choice and first step the developing agent made and what help it used. C.40.OD:5 and C.40.NP:5 show how to recognize these results.

#### Library.Reference:9.1.2 - What result are we trying to obtain?

A request for a development office can name a proposed arrangement before anyone has established its useful result. Recover that result first. Which result is needed: performance of the current work, a gain in the performer’s capability, the ability to organize its own further development, or the ability to help others continue? Does a provider need a viable offer, a company need a different capability, or a community need useful participation? These results can depend on each other without being the same.
