A professional direction in a common office can supply it when existing scheduling, intake and infrastructure serve these requests and preserve that judgement. Distributed local hosts can supply the same arrangement when they can prepare and interpret their cases, with the specialist serving only the remaining need. Compare the setup, continuing coordination and qualification needed by each form over the four-week horizon. In this example those quantities have not been supplied, so the organizational choice remains conditional even though the one-hour service comparison is determinate. Do not create an office on the strength of a saving calculated for only one of its functions.

Now distinguish three possible promises. If the teams need only qualified reports, the clinic can be a continuing service. If each team must later produce its own report, establish which judgements the team must make, how its members can share the work and what support it may retain. A qualified member with access to the data may already be able to do the work. If the team still needs to learn, provide for practice and feedback and count that effort. To establish that a team actually produced a later report, examine its contributions to that report. If the teams dispute the reporting Method, establish what the disagreement changes for the report or its grounds. When every admissible reading supports the required finding on adequate grounds, the team can deliver that finding while the dispute remains open. An applicable decision or a qualified interpretation can resolve the dispute. Commission method development when a needed way of working still has to be devised. Attendance figures cannot settle that question.

Suppose one team cannot disclose its project data to the other teams. Shared preparation may remain possible, while the discussions require separate access. Suppose another team needs a decision before the clinic meets. Retain a response route for that window, stagger the work or obtain another provider. A shared calendar does not make the request timely. If the changed arrangement consumes the saved hour, the original cost advantage disappears without disproving all shared provision.

Establish who must be available during each discussion. The teams may already have reserved their hours, or a manager may control the time they need. If the commission permits a written report using records already available to the specialist, the team can receive the judgement without attending. Compare the work and timing of that arrangement before attributing the clinic’s saving to it. LDR.1 and the organizational-change Methods address the needed participation and duties; Human Capability Development addresses an acquisition goal. The organizer returns a feasible allocation and its limits, rather than promising all three results from the office's existence.

#### Library.Reference:9.2.9 - Use an unlike case to expose the boundary

A volunteer association wants members to interpret technical documents and discuss proposals. It has no common employer, no authority to assign study and no predictable stream of cases. A permanent corporate training office is a poor default.

A workable arrangement can combine an accessible explanation, a recurring discussion with actual documents, peer attempts and an obtainable expert for unresolved technical questions. Participants choose the contribution they want. Give a member who needs an interpretation today a usable answer. For a member who wants to interpret future documents, find out what they can already do and which resources they will have. Access to the applicable edition can enable an experienced reader. Practice with feedback can help someone who still needs to learn how to interpret it. The organizer should explain which results are offered and which expertise is unavailable.

The same misconception may arise each week because new members arrive with it. Check what members can understand or do after the available explanation or practice. Keep help that serves its purpose. If a consequential difficulty persists, improve the part responsible for it or return the finding to whoever can do so. If necessary facts are missing from the documents, tell the authorized recipient what cannot be established. Where the job includes obtaining a correction, find the source that can establish those facts and the party authorized to revise the document. If members disagree about a proposal's values or authority, a better glossary alone will not settle it. Community Building supplies the participation and continuing community relations; it does not turn membership into acquired competence.

Compare this case with the office case. Both need a useful contribution, actual capability, conditions, an intelligible interaction and a justified continuation. Their authority, participation, funding, professional content and capacity are different. That shared structure supports transfer of the organizing explanation; the corporate numbers and role assignments do not transfer.

#### Library.Reference:9.2.10 - Return the result to the recipient's next use

Close the question at the strength of what happened. A delivered contribution may complete urgent work. A supported attempt may establish a usable arrangement. A performance can show what the person or team can do with the support used on that occasion. To say that the holder acquired a capability, establish a change from an earlier condition. To credit a particular intervention, examine what else could explain the change. Section 9.1.5 develops these distinctions. Repeated independent use, adaptation and transmission may support a broader cultural claim. None is an automatic substitute for the others.

Keep useful continuing help where the intended practice relies on it. Reduce or withdraw help when that is a justified change, with a way to detect failure and recover. Reopen the choice when the target, recipient, demand, provider, authority or available technology changes. A departure of the only specialist can change the best arrangement even when the Method remains sound.

The practical result is an arrangement the participants can use and revise: a recognized need, an appropriate contribution, understood reciprocal actions, available conditions and a warranted continuation. Section 9.3 explains what must additionally happen when the intended result is a shared professional culture.

### Library.Reference:9.3 - Acquiring, sustaining and changing a shared human–AI professional culture

A professional culture is visible in what participants notice, explain, attempt, accept, challenge, repair and pass on. Its written canon is an important source of common distinctions and Methods. The culture also depends on how people and technical agents actually use that source, work with one another and reproduce or change the practice. Publication, membership, a completed course and one good team result establish different facts.

Use this account when people and AI are expected to share a professional practice, or when a practice fails to survive a change of participant, context or conditions. It joins the cultural Methods in C.36 with Human Capability Development, agent engineering, coordination and the organization of help. It also applies when an existing culture contains useful contributions and harmful habits together. The aim is a warranted continuation or change, not uniformity for its own sake.

A professional culture may span organizations and communities that never form one team. Conversely, one team can use several professional cultures. Specify the practice and the participants relevant to the current question. Neither an organization chart nor a universal human–AI label gives that boundary.

#### Library.Reference:9.3.1 - A shared repertoire with different holders and different claims

A repertoire includes available ways of recognizing and dealing with situations, their explanations and working examples, expectations about good work, and ways of obtaining help and handling exceptions. Some of this may be explicit in a canon; some may be carried by competent participants, tools and arrangements. Make tacit contributions explicit when their loss or transfer matters. Do not suppose that putting all material in one repository makes every contribution available in work.

People and AI can participate in one cultural process without acquiring or retaining the repertoire through the same mechanism. A person can develop understanding, skill, habits and a professional identity through learning and participation. An AI system can select relevant material, execute procedures, use tools, retain information in an external memory and alter its working configuration; a model's parameters may or may not change. State which system, configuration and mechanism the claim concerns.

Keep four results separate:

| Result | What would support the claim | What remains open |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A useful current output | Appropriate examination of this result under its actual production conditions | Who acquired a capability and whether another case will succeed |
| A holder's capability | Relevant performance of that holder with the intended available support | Team coordination, wider distribution and retention after change |
| A team's joint capability | The participants can combine their contributions under the required conditions | Whether every member can perform every part, or whether newcomers can join |
| Cultural continuation or change | The generation, transmission, recognition, selection, memory, retention or loss relation is supported for the named participants and interval | Other cultural relations and effects not examined |

To decide what work can be entrusted to the holder, use [A.2.2:3](FPF-Spec.md#a223---positive-solution). Start with the proposed or required result for each recipient and the judgements entrusted to the holder. Include any work already promised. An earlier performance under the available assistance may settle that fit. When several performances or services are promised, establish how demand and operating conditions may vary, which decisions the recipients retain and when shared help is available. Check whether the same arrangement can meet the agreed criterion alongside existing obligations. [Management.Reference:4.8](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/MANAGEMENT-DPF-SUITE-REFERENCE.md#managementreference48---personnel-decisions-and-supported-participation) applies this reasoning to recommendations for one or several posts, appointment and actual support.

C.36.MC helps qualify these claims. To explain how practitioners in a discipline can recognize transmission, identify what they must recognize across different performances of the method. Which choice or operation has to be preserved, what may be adapted, and how can they tell that a recipient obtained it from the stated source? A later participant can repeat a visible action for a different reason. Conversely, changing an instrument or a phrase can preserve the operation the recipient needed. A record of one handoff can establish one such transmission. To report a change across a whole practice, use the population, period and observations that bear on that claim. C.36.RP helps arrange continued access through people, tools or services where the later work depends on them.

A person and an AI may produce a usable analysis because the person repeatedly corrects the agent's interpretation. Inspection of the analysis can establish its quality for the client. A record of the corrections can show which judgements the person supplied. If that person will be available on the next job under comparable conditions, the team can use this arrangement again. If the agent is to assume that judgement after a restart, establish that its working configuration and the available support enable that choice. To establish learning by the person, look for what the person could do before and after under the relevant conditions.

#### Library.Reference:9.3.2 - The transformations form a network, not a funnel

The phrase “produce mastery” can help draw attention to a real change in a capability holder. It becomes misleading when it treats the learner as passive material, assumes that the course itself changes the person, or makes all development follow a fixed manufacturing sequence.

The relevant transformations have different direct subjects:

| Work and its changed subject | Contribution to another use | A return that can change the work |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Investigate, construct or revise a professional Method | A warranted way and its limits for practitioners, explainers and tool designers | Work reveals an invalid premise, missing operation or useful variation |
| Develop a Method account and learning material | An intelligible explanation, contrasting cases, tasks and feedback grounds | Recipients cannot recover the reason or distinguish the relevant cases |
| Arrange and perform human learning | A change in this person's capability, with its actual support conditions | Attempts reveal a misconception, unavailable practice, motivational difficulty or transfer failure |
| Engineer and configure an AI system | Available execution under a particular model, context, procedure, memory, tool and permission arrangement | Traces and results reveal a missing input, failed operation, context loss or capability limit |
| Organize participation, work and provision | Compatible contributions and usable conditions at the required time | Demand, authority, workload or a participant's undertaking changes |
| Perform the professional work | The receiving result and observations of actual use | Results or outside effects call the way, allocation or accepted goal into question |
| Preserve, transmit and renew the repertoire | An obtainable contribution for later participants and situations | A carrier disappears, a newcomer interprets it differently or the practice becomes unsuitable |

These rows are not mandatory stages. A sufficiently qualified Method can be used directly. A person can learn while contributing to real work. A technical agent can supply an explanation while a human tests it. Development of the Method, learning material, platform and teaching practice can proceed together, with their actual dependencies and changes kept coherent. A later observation returns to the affected subject; it need not restart every branch.

For a training programme, the methodological work establishes what competent performance requires. Instructional design establishes how this audience can acquire the needed capability. Teaching and coached practice realize that learning arrangement. A platform makes material, practice, assistance and feedback available. Organizational work makes the time and roles feasible. None of these results automatically supplies the others.

Anatoly Levenchuk's Person Engineering (R9) usefully separates the developing person, learner, developmental guide and teaching team. It distinguishes work on the professional content, didactics, programme architecture, subject teaching, participation and provision. These are functions that can be combined or distributed, not a compulsory staffing chart. Its account of concurrent development also prevents a false waterfall from finished theory through finished course to finished people. Its engineering metaphors remain useful only with the learner's agency and the actual change mechanism explicit.

A learner journey is one participant's changing trajectory through this network. It can include work, a consultation, practice, a pause, a new responsibility, return to a prerequisite or no further course. The journey must explain what each encounter supplies and why it is needed now. An acquisition funnel describes population movement toward an offer or conversion; it cannot by itself explain capability acquisition, reciprocal work or cultural transmission.

Nor does a graph of skills establish a universal learning order. Distinguish a prerequisite for acquiring an operation, a component used during performance, a tool or expert contribution available at use, and a skill merely correlated with another in employment data. HCD.2 supplies composition and comparison of development programmes. Its result should preserve existing capability and obtain only the missing preparation, rather than require every person to repeat the same course sequence.

##### Library.Reference:9.3.2.1 - Derive the next contribution from the result and its conditions

The learning purpose here is to derive a useful continuation from the required result, the relations between its parts and the conditions now available. Start with those relations before choosing a course, helper or tool. The same position can permit several continuations; a missing condition can block one and leave another useful.

Consider a constructed case extending the forecast example in section 9.2. A team needs a justified forecast of what it can deliver by Friday. Two independent jobs each require six hours from the same specialist; eight hours of that specialist's time are available before the deadline. The jobs are otherwise ready, their estimates are accepted for this simplified case, and this specialist is the only constrained resource. A correct forecast must account for the shared demand and expose any unresolved choice about scope or capacity.

The human planner can estimate an individual job and read the resource calendar. In an observed attempt, however, the planner judges each six-hour job separately against the eight available hours and concludes that both fit. The AI assistant retrieves the same facts and makes the same comparison in its job-by-job procedure. Neither attempt combines the twelve hours of demand on the shared resource. The missing contribution is the joint feasibility judgement. It forms part of making this forecast; listing or estimating the jobs has not supplied it.

A qualified colleague has agreed to supply today's forecast and can work with the planner in the available time. The planner wants to acquire the missing judgement and has agreed time for practice with feedback. The delivery manager decides which jobs to commit; the resource owner determines what specialist time can be allocated. Someone has suggested obtaining four additional specialist hours, but the resource owner has not yet confirmed their availability. These facts support the following alternatives at this position:

| Continuation | What must be available | Present judgement and contribution |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Release the planner's current forecast, assisted by the unchanged AI. | A forecast that accounts for the shared resource. | Blocked: the observed procedure omits the joint constraint. Access to the facts and correct individual estimates remain useful. |
| Have the qualified colleague produce the forecast. | The colleague's capability, time, inputs and agreement to supply it. | Available on the stated facts. The colleague can establish that twelve required hours exceed eight and return the shortfall to the delivery manager, who can consider a scope change or request additional capacity. |
| Work through the forecast together. | The same qualified judgement and the planner's participation. | Available. It can produce today's forecast while exposing why the separate comparisons fail. The planner's own later attempt is still needed for an acquisition claim. |
| Practise the missing comparison with feedback. | A suitable task, the planner's attempt and obtainable feedback. | Available under the agreed learning conditions. It aims to change the planner's capability and must return to a complete forecast. |
| Obtain four more specialist hours and revise the forecast. | Four usable hours before Friday, without displacing another required commitment, and the responsible owner's agreement. | Unknown: the suggestion establishes none of those resource facts. Ask that owner whether the hours can be provided before relying on this continuation. |

These are alternatives for obtaining different contributions, with some possible combinations. They are not stages through which everyone must pass. The specialist hours govern performance of the jobs; the colleague's time and the planner's practice conditions govern production and learning of the forecast. Adding job capacity would change the delivery possibilities without by itself repairing either forecaster's procedure.

If the undertaking requires only today's justified forecast, the colleague's sufficient performance can complete the help. The planner can use that result without first becoming an independent forecaster. If personal acquisition is also promised, the same delivered forecast leaves that promise open. A provider and recipient can agree both results, or choose one; the declared result determines what remains to do.

For the human learning branch, the colleague asks the planner to identify where both jobs draw on the same capacity, make the combined comparison and explain the consequence. Feedback addresses the missing relation. The planner then applies it in a complete forecast, retaining the known individual estimates, checking the shared calendar and distinguishing a forecast from the delivery manager's scope decision and the resource owner's allocation decision. Correct addition alone would leave the encompassing task unfinished. A later case with another shared resource tests recognition beyond the familiar display. HCD supplies the design and assessment of that practice; the arithmetic here makes the intermediate contribution visible.

The AI repair targets the job-by-job procedure that missed the shared demand. The engineer examines that procedure and introduces a step that groups demand by shared resource, retrieves the available capacity for the same interval and compares the totals before drafting the forecast. A representative run and a contrasting case can establish what this configuration now does. After a restart, the procedure and task facts must still be available at the point of use; A.15.10 and the engineering Methods address a failure there. Judge the changed execution support by the performance it produces. A successful configured run does not establish that the underlying model acquired a durable new capability. The human branch changes the person's performance through attempts and feedback; the technical branch changes the agent system's access and execution arrangement.

Now change one condition: the resource owner confirms four additional usable hours before Friday, with no displaced commitment in this case. The accepted job estimates, the shared-resource relation and the required justified forecast remain. Twelve hours are now available for twelve hours of demand, so the capacity condition no longer excludes completion of both jobs. The earlier eight-hour forecast remains correct for its original conditions. If it already contained the branch for twelve hours without displaced work, apply that branch to the confirmed calendar. Recalculate if the prior reasoning leaves the new case open. Issue a new forecast when that delivery is due. The planner's learned comparison and the repaired AI procedure should both use the new capacity rather than repeat the old conclusion. If the owner's answer instead denies the extra hours, the unknown provision branch becomes blocked, while a forecast exposing the shortfall remains obtainable. An effective priority decision may already settle which job the forecast should place first. If the choice remains open, identify it in the forecast. When the undertaking includes settling that choice, decide within the authority granted or obtain the decision from the party authorized to defer the work.

This episode connects the transformations in section 9.3.2 through their contributions. The forecasting Method supplies the relevant feasibility relation. The explanation makes that relation intelligible. Human practice and AI engineering obtain different performer changes. Organizational work obtains time and authority. Joint performance returns a forecast to the team's delivery decision. The planner's learner journey follows the missing judgement into practice and back into forecasting; another recipient who only needs the colleague's output can leave after that sufficient result.

A useful return to the shared repertoire is the instruction to account for shared resource demand before making a delivery commitment, together with the example, its conditions and the procedure that uses it. A newcomer or replacement agent must recover that relation in its own work. Observing this transmission supports that bounded cultural claim; it does not establish all of the team's culture. Where the deficiency is intermediate coordination during performance rather than resource aggregation, A.22.CGUS:5.1 supplies the dancer's case: practising the missing coordination must likewise return to the whole figure. A.22.CGUS:4.1 explains the ordinary comparison of available, blocked and unknown continuations used here. Its contribution is to make the conditions of the next move explicit; the subject Methods still supply the professional work and its learning or engineering.

#### Library.Reference:9.3.3 - Human acquisition requires the person's learning work

Start with the work the person wants or legitimately needs to do, the capability they already have and the conditions of its use. Ask for an informative attempt where it can change the diagnosis. A fluent explanation, a training attendance record and a failure in a hostile work setting are weak substitutes for this information.

In coached practice, several contributions can work together. The learner may need to recognize the relevant situation and intended result. An explanation or demonstration can expose the consequential operation and its reason. The learner attempts that operation. Feedback can identify what changed the result and why. A subsequent attempt lets the learner use that information. Choose the contributions the learner needs for the intended work. Later performance under the required conditions supplies grounds for a claim about that capability.

The content and difficulty of each contribution matter. A novice may need a worked example and a bounded part before constructing the whole. A capable practitioner may need one contrast that repairs a misconception. Repeating the same exercise can improve familiarity while leaving the ability to recognize and adapt the Method untested. Variation should preserve the learning purpose while changing a consequential condition, rather than add random difficulty.

Motivation and participation are part of the arrangement. “Wants to learn” does not supply time, energy, equipment or a safe opportunity to attempt. A person can value the capability and still reject a particular course, provider or role. Recover the reason for the difficulty before intensifying persuasion. Agree a feasible commitment, change the support or retain an explicit unmet condition. LDR.1 addresses the role contribution; HCD supplies the learning response. Their results can be needed together.

Transfer into work requires more than success in the instructional setting. Identify which choices and operations belong to the learner in the receiving work. A technician can transfer a checking operation to a new job even when another service selects it and supplies readings. A job that also entrusts recognition, selection or adaptation to the technician needs grounds for those further contributions. Compare projects and circumstances under HCD.25. Use the intended supports in the comparison. If the job includes reference material or a qualified colleague, removing them tests a different capability unless independence is the requirement.

A learner may understand the Method but be unable to use it because a supervisor rewards the old practice or denies the necessary time. That calls for a change in work or participation, not another explanation. Another learner may use a familiar classroom cue and fail to recognize the same relation in a new project. That calls for discriminating practice and feedback. The same “no transfer” report can conceal these different causes.

AI can provide examples, questions, feedback and accessible practice. Assign it the contribution it can supply and examine representative results. The person should still perform the operation whose acquisition is wanted. If an AI generates every diagnosis and repair, a correct final product can conceal that the person never made the decisive judgement. Conversely, banning AI from all learning can remove useful explanation or access without improving the learning design.

HCD.7 and HCD.8 address obtainable support and missing development provision; HCD.26 addresses regulation of shared learning. HCD.28 addresses a learning product for a future audience. Use those Methods at their proper scale. A complete learning product requires an audience, explanation, practice, feedback and provision that this cross-Library account does not invent for every profession.

Embodied practice makes the holder distinction visible. In a partner dance, the learner needs to perceive timing, move, coordinate with a partner and adapt physically. An AI may supply an explanation, analyse a recording or help arrange practice within its qualified capabilities. A good textual account does not establish the person's movement skill, and a text-only system does not become a physical dance partner by describing that role. A robotic or other embodied system would require qualification of its own sensing, control and interaction. Preserve the actual professional Method and conditions of the embodiment rather than transfer a document-work result by analogy.

Maintain the capability through use and appropriate renewal. Decide what must remain available after a gap, what changes require new preparation and where the person can obtain assistance. The relevant claim concerns a particular person and work family under stated conditions.

#### Library.Reference:9.3.4 - AI acquisition requires a change in available execution

Treat the operative participant as the selected model within an agent system: instructions, context, retrieval, memory, tools, permissions, orchestration and available computation all affect what it can do. The same model name can appear in materially different arrangements. A successful prompt does not qualify every later invocation.

For the present question, distinguish these interventions:

| Intervention | What changes | Evidence needed for the receiving use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Supply or retrieve relevant canon | The system can access the selected explanation or evidence in this execution | For access, establish the rights and working route to that edition. For use, establish how the system selected or applied the supplied grounds in the actual operation |
| Change instructions or working procedure | The instructions governing how the system should work | For a rule change, show what the old and new instructions require, permit or rule out under the relevant conditions. For an effect on performance, establish what actually differed in the work and how the changed instruction contributed |
| Preserve and recover task state | The system can recover the goal, decisions, performed work, unresolved questions and side effects after interruption | For recoverability, establish what information can be recovered and under which conditions. For a claim about a resumed execution, establish which assignment it continued and how prior effects were handled |
| Provide an external memory | Information or experience can be selected again under defined conditions | For the promised memory service, check the required retention, retrieval, revision or removal. For an effect on performance, establish which retrieved material influenced the work |
| Change tools, access or orchestration | A required operation becomes available, or contributions are combined differently | For readiness, establish effective permissions, interface compatibility and the required means. For a claim of successful execution, establish what ran under those conditions |
| Change or train a model | A different parameterized system is used | Qualification of the changed system for the relevant work, with the support and limits retained |

These interventions can interact. An instruction may correctly require a source that retrieval never exposes. A memory may retrieve an old decision that was superseded. A stronger model may still act on the wrong objective. A familiar reminder can help one failure while obscuring another. Diagnose the first unsupported contribution from the actual input, actions and result.

SYSE.46 supplies the comparison of unaided, supplied and agent-selected support and the distinction between missing input, failed selection, missed execution and a remaining capability problem. Its related agent-engineering Methods supply the corresponding repairs. KCAE.MEMORY supplies selective, qualified memory use. A.15.10 supplies recovery of interrupted purposeful work.

Attention in this practice means keeping the governing task, applicable Method and current work connected while acting. The practical question is whether the agent performs the required operation under those grounds. If it repeatedly replaces a request for a whole explanation with the latest local correction, restore the original receiving question, inspect what the correction supplies and construct the remaining whole. More context alone may not change that working habit; a procedure or other causal repair may be needed.

After an interruption, recover the actual instruction and consequential updates, what has already happened and what remains uncertain. A summary can help locate the record, but it may omit a correction or the outcome of a side effect. Where an action may already have taken effect, establish its state before repeating it. Recovery is a return to the task and Method, not a compulsory recitation of every historical message.

When a failure recurs, use the actual traces and conditions to compare causes. Did the agent never obtain the instruction, misunderstand its object, fail to call the needed tool, ignore its return, lose the goal, encounter a denied permission or lack the capability to perform the operation? Change the implicated source of the failure. A new warning appended to every output is not a causal repair unless it changes the relevant action under the conditions that produced the failure.

Qualify the repair on the receiving work. Preserve a supported prior success. Use another representative task, interruption, delayed return or changed condition when the next claim requires it. Record whether help was supplied or selected, and whether a person continually supplied the missing judgement. A bounded operational success is useful even when the long-term mechanism remains uncertain.

Do not equate these changes with human learning. Contextual adaptation can be temporary; external memory can persist outside the model; parameter learning has a different change process. Equally, do not assume that AI participants cannot originate variations, coordinate, criticize a canon or maintain conventions. Observe those contributions under their actual conditions and qualify their meaning through C.36.

#### Library.Reference:9.3.5 - Common meaning is an achievement of joint work

First establish what the participants need to do together and what each role is responsible for. At a handoff, one participant may classify an item and the other may act on the classification. The work can succeed with different actions in the two roles.

If future work depends on both participants classifying items by the same criterion, give them the same question and facts. If their answers differ, find what each checked and which criterion each used. When the difference blocks the handoff, agree with the responsible participant on the fact or criterion to use, repair the instruction or the information passed and return to the disputed item. Agreement can settle a choice the participants are authorized to make together. If a report must satisfy an acceptance criterion, check the report against it. To distinguish two plausible criteria, compare the participants' decisions on a report for which those criteria predict different answers. Record which alternatives that comparison rules out. For reliance across a class of reports, show why the criteria actually used give equivalent decisions throughout that class, or limit reliance to the reports and conditions the evidence covers. Engineering.Reference:19.6 develops this work on understanding a partner's contribution.

For example, a human reviewer and an AI assistant both return a report for revision. The report contains an arithmetic error and draws a causal conclusion its evidence cannot support. Either fault suffices to explain the return. A report with correct arithmetic and the same causal fault would distinguish a reviewer who checks only arithmetic from one who checks only the causal argument. Other review practices may still fit both answers. Telling the assistant which review to perform settles the assignment. Its work on reports shows how it followed the instruction.

Private shorthand can reduce effort among familiar participants. It also creates a transfer condition. A newcomer needs access to the meaning, examples and a way to ask for clarification. If agents invent a compact code that their human colleagues cannot interpret, AI–AI coordination may improve while the joint practice deteriorates. If the code carries a valid distinction, preserve or translate that distinction; if it hides an unsupported decision, recover the basis.

A shared culture should make legitimate clarification and correction practicable. This requires time, access and a response from someone who can resolve the issue. Merely telling participants to “speak up” is inadequate if their objection is ignored or punished. Conversely, endless clarification can prevent a sufficient bounded action. Use the consequential uncertainty: obtain what can change the next choice and proceed within the supported conditions.

#### Library.Reference:9.3.6 - Assistant, teammate, principal and agent describe different relations

An assistant helps with another participant's work. A teammate contributes within a joint undertaking. These descriptions can apply to the same participant. Use Library.Reference:9.2.2 to identify their actual operations and contributions. Neither label fixes initiative, expertise, permission, dependence, accountability or the right to change a Method.

Principal–agent analysis addresses a delegated relation in which interests, information and incentives can differ. Collaboration toward a common purpose does not remove that relation, and a contract does not exhaust the team's joint practice. An AI can be a delegate for one operation and a delegator for another. Identify the parties, the authority they can transfer and the responsibility for the combined result.

Use a concrete undertaking. Who requests which result? Who accepts which work? What inputs and conditions are promised by the requester? Who can judge completion, permit a change, cancel, dispute or continue? What happens to already incurred effort and outside effects? A technical acknowledgement is evidence of a protocol event; establish what commitment that event represents in this arrangement.

Dietz and Mulder distinguish production from coordination, request from promise and declaration from acceptance. In their human account, feasibility problems can lead to discussion of the product or timing, while challenged grounds of rightness or sincerity lead to discourse. These distinctions help locate a disagreement. Their premise of free human coordination cannot simply be assumed in relations of dependence or mapped unchanged onto a technical agent.

For a decision about a whole order, start with the agreement and the records of what the participants did. [A.6.C:4.1](FPF-Spec.md#a6c41--four-questions-for-contract-like-boundary-wording) explains how to find each independently owed result and establish which decisions settle the order. An agreement may let one authorised party accept for all recipients, require each to accept a part, or make a recorded delivery itself decisive. A positive technical assessment can then have different consequences under different agreements. Dietz and Mulder also connect transactions within one order, including a delivery that only some orders require. Their actor role carries authority to execute a kind of transaction. In FPF identify the assignment, any mandate to act for another party, and the act actually performed. For an organisation changing its work use OCE.2:4.1. For a service with continuing obligations use OPS.11.1:4.2 and OPS.13:4.1. [Enterprise Ontology, ch. 8, §§8.2.5–8.2.7](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-53361-7_8)

For an AI participant, establish whether it acts within an accepted organizational undertaking, is authorized to make a bounded commitment through a protocol, or merely proposes a continuation for an authorized party. Name the party or organization responsible for the relevant consequence. This preserves useful technical agency without inferring human social or legal status from fluent dialogue.

Apply reciprocal scrutiny. A delegate may conceal missing capability, misuse access or substitute its own easier target. A principal may make an infeasible demand, withhold agreed conditions, move the acceptance criterion or exploit dependence. The recipient of help may be harmed by a sponsor's objective; the helper may be pressured to certify an unsupported success. Preserve an evidence-based objection and return the contested authority or condition to the appropriate decision.

A justified objection, an honest mistake, a technical failure and opportunism require different responses. First identify the disputed proposition, promised act or observed action. Correct a false fact, repair an unavailable means, renegotiate a feasible undertaking or obtain a competent ruling on the contested ground. A performance failure alone does not prove intent. An agent's explanation of its intent does not settle the causal question.

Revision also has a history. The 2024 second edition of Enterprise Ontology explicitly includes reconsidering earlier decline and reject acts. Agreement on a return in the coordination process does not erase production costs or other effects. The practical continuation must account for those facts, including a separate compensating undertaking where appropriate. “Reset the task” is not an adequate model of every relationship.

#### Library.Reference:9.3.7 - Preserve and revise the canon through actual use

A canon can provide authoritative distinctions and reusable Methods while remaining open to correction. Establish what status a claim has, where it applies and who may change the maintained source. A local permission to edit a working note does not authorize changing the profession's standard. A useful local variation does not need to be misrepresented as an already adopted norm.

When experience challenges the practice, recover the relevant input, operation, result and conditions. Decide which subject needs change. An inaccessible explanation calls for access repair. A misleading account calls for explanation repair. A person's misconception calls for learning. A failed agent procedure calls for an engineering change. An unsuitable Method calls for subject development. A conflict of purpose or authority calls for its own accountable resolution. Several repairs may be needed, but name their dependence.

C.36.MC connects the cultural claim and continuation; C.36.RP connects recoverable repertoire and future use. C.40.JD connects a changing Method, holder and provision. Method-development and variation patterns qualify changes to the way itself. Use these results together when the cultural practice depends on them. Preserve sufficient current work while an unresolved improvement is developed, unless the current use is no longer justified.

A community can contribute examples, criticism, novice questions, new variations and expertise. It can also reproduce prestige bias, obsolete conventions or unexamined shortcuts. Popularity, longevity and frequency of use establish neither validity nor usefulness. Make the reason for selection and the route for a contrary finding available to later participants.

The retained contribution may need several forms: a developed account for learning or adaptation, a short cue during familiar work, examples that expose a distinction, and technical material usable by an agent. Preserve their correspondence to the same governed Method or claim. A short cue should not become an independent authority that silently drops a condition. An AI-specific instruction can differ from a human teaching explanation because the receiving operations differ.

Cultural continuity requires renewing carriers and capability. If the only expert leaves, a repository may retain documents while losing the interpretation that made them useful. If a model or tool changes, old evaluations may no longer qualify the same performance. If the professional domain changes, faithfully reproducing the old canon can be the failure. Reopen the affected use, obtain the missing contribution and retain the justified remainder.

#### Library.Reference:9.3.8 - A worked corporate culture project

Consider a constructed application to a company that wants a more reliable practice of explaining decisions and using professional Methods. The initial service offer supplies twelve contact hours of a mentor per month for four months, with possible monthly continuation. Those numbers define part of the provider's capacity; they do not define the culture, the company's total effort or a guaranteed result.

Begin with a representative piece of company work, such as a project decision whose grounds must be understood by operations and engineering. The participants identify the decision, relevant Methods, actual difficulties and desired later capability. They distinguish immediate assistance with the decision, staff development, agent configuration and changes to the company's working conditions. An urgent decision can use expert help while the longer development proceeds.

A possible first-month contact allocation is two hours to establish the work and undertakings, four hours of supported work on two real decisions, two hours of feedback on participant attempts, two hours with the people responsible for AI configuration and two hours to examine the resulting continuation. This totals twelve contact hours. Preparation, participant practice, technical changes, access administration and review outside those contacts require their own feasible provision. If the commercial offer includes some of that work, make the included scope explicit rather than counting it as free.

For the human branch, choose a consequential operation: distinguish a claim from its supporting observation and state the condition that would change the decision. Staff explain a worked contrast, attempt the operation on their own case and use feedback. A later case asks whether they recognize the distinction with the support the job permits. Attendance and agreement with the mentor do not settle that question.

For the AI branch, choose the same receiving contribution but a different intervention. Configure access to the current Method and relevant task facts; establish how the agent recovers the original decision question after an interruption; examine whether it uses the evidence and returns unresolved claims. If it repeatedly converts uncertainty into a confident recommendation, locate the source of that behaviour before adding a generic instruction. The technical provider implements and qualifies the selected change.

The joint branch asks whether the staff and AI understand one another's contribution. A human tests the agent's interpretation of “support this conclusion”; the agent exposes which observation it used and which inference remains. The team agrees who may request another source, who accepts the decision input and how a disagreement returns. A good combined result can be retained while one participant's independent capability remains unresolved.

At the end of an early period, compare actual work, participant capability, available provision and the cultural claims. The company may have two usable decisions and a promising learning arrangement without a demonstrated company-wide culture. Continue the useful contribution and name what the next month must obtain. A plausible four-month plan can adapt as the real demand and difficulty become known.

Now change a condition: the sponsor requires independent staff performance at renewal. An offer that previously supplied expert-assisted results no longer answers the full question. Allocate the requisite attempts and examination, reduce the number of supported cases, obtain more qualified support or revise the promise.

Change another condition: the sponsor wants a continuing decision-support service and does not require every employee to master the analysis. Retaining an expert or AI service may be appropriate. The company still needs enough capability to request, interpret and contest its contributions. This is a different justified outcome, not failure because the provider remains involved.

This example illustrates a bounded arrangement. The four months, contact hours and B2B relationship do not transfer to every office or culture. The organizing distinctions also apply to the volunteer case in section 9.2, a quality service, a project office or a distributed professional community.

#### Library.Reference:9.3.9 - Variation that changes the continuation

| Observed difficulty | First distinction to resolve | Consequential continuation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A skilled participant cannot perform | Capability versus available time, input, tool or authority | Repair the missing condition; use learning only for a capability gap |
| A willing learner does not practise | Intention versus feasible participation and a useful attempt | Change the practice arrangement or commitment, then examine the attempt |
| Classroom performance does not transfer | Recognition and adaptation versus workplace constraints | Vary relevant contexts and feedback, or change the work conditions, according to the cause |
| A team succeeds while one member remains dependent | Joint output versus the member's required capability | Preserve justified joint work; add development only if later use requires that capability |
| An AI cites the canon but omits the operation | Source access versus interpretation, selection and execution | Inspect the decisive trace and repair the implicated procedure, tool or capability |
| An AI loses the whole task after interruption | A usable record versus actual recovery and resumed action | Apply A.15.10 to the actual assignment and effects; then test the required continuation |
| Participants use identical words differently | Vocabulary versus shared referent and intended action | Work through a discriminating case and repair the consequential interpretation |
| A participant objects to an instruction | False premise, infeasibility, disputed authority or opportunism | Obtain the corresponding factual repair, feasible undertaking or accountable decision |
| A sponsor asks a helper to certify success | Assistance versus an assurance claim and its independence | Establish the qualified judge and evidence; preserve conflicts and unresolved claims |
| New participants cannot use a successful practice | Available carrier versus explanation, capability and access | Reconstruct the missing contribution and qualify its use by the new recipients |
| The canon is faithfully followed but unsuitable | Execution fidelity versus validity and present fit | Revise or replace the affected Method or conditions through its subject owner |
| Everyone needs the same scarce helper | Local difficulty versus common provision and total demand | Compare shared provision, distributed capability, external help and revised scope |

This table is an entry into explanation, not a substitute for diagnosis. Several rows can apply together. Use the smallest sufficient return and retain the unaffected contributions. In particular, a technical intervention can change a team's capability without changing a human's skill, and a human learning success can leave a cultural transmission problem unresolved.

#### Library.Reference:9.3.10 - Sources, currentness and the strength of the claims

The general explanation joins conceptual distinctions, professional Methods and bounded empirical findings. These have different evidential roles.

Anatoly Levenchuk's Person Engineering (R9) supplies a useful role and transformation account. The account here preserves developing person's agency, the distinction between professional content and didactics, the contribution of teaching and provision, and concurrent development. It does not adopt a universal learning sequence, quantitative promises of accelerated learning or the claim that a motor-learning mechanism has already explained every kind of professional acquisition.

Dietz and Mulder's [Enterprise Ontology, second edition, 2024](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-031-53361-7) supplies the coordination distinctions and their human social premises. It helps analyse what was requested, promised, declared, accepted or reopened. [Wagner's account of the opportunistic principal](https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1111/kykl.12213) supplies a conceptual counterweight to treating only delegates as possible opportunists. Neither source makes every participant selfish or settles the governing obligations of a particular institution.

[Team Topologies](https://teamtopologies.com/key-concepts/) supplies professional distinctions among interactions in software delivery. The current [ETAP account](https://teamtopologies.com/news-blogs-newsletters/accelerating-value-delivery-how-enabling-teams-and-the-etap-dynamic-drive-scale) relates enabling work to platform improvement. The [AI-era discussion](https://teamtopologies.com/news-blogs-newsletters/team-topologies-for-the-ai-age-stewardship-of-value-flow) motivates attention to continuing value and accountability. Its analogy between human cognitive load and AI context is not an established equivalence of mechanisms. The present account therefore keeps human and technical interventions distinct.

[Microsoft's AI centre-of-excellence operating models](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/agents/center-of-excellence/operating-models) provide a contemporary professional comparison of centralized, hybrid and federated arrangements in that domain. They inform the organizational alternatives without proving a best arrangement for quality, project management or literacy.

[Intelligent AI Delegation](https://arxiv.org/html/2602.11865v1) proposes a framework connecting capability, authority, monitoring, adaptation and accountability across delegation. It is a design proposal, not a validated universal protocol. Use its questions to expose a needed condition; do not infer that a signed event or a technical verification proves the full professional result.

[The Cybernetic Teammate](https://pubsonline.informs.org/doi/10.1287/orsc.2025.20702) reports a bounded workplace innovation experiment. Its team-performance findings do not establish long-term culture or personal learning. [Shen and Tamkin](https://arxiv.org/html/2601.20245v1) distinguish immediate task performance from acquisition in a short study of developers learning a new library. That result motivates separate examination of learning; it does not prove inevitable deskilling with AI.

[SCARCE](https://arxiv.org/html/2610.21029v1) examines conventions and relationships among agents competing for a resource. Whether the emerging arrangements improve collective task performance remains a separate question. [GlossoGen](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.01491v1) examines formation and transmission of communication conventions under particular agent and memory conditions. [Jones and colleagues](https://arxiv.org/html/2602.08208v1) show that mixed human–AI pairs in a reference game can improve while still coordinating less well than homogeneous pairs. These results justify examining actual transmission and mixed interaction; they establish neither a single mechanism of all culture nor a ceiling on later models.

Keep model, configuration, task, permitted assistance and observation interval with any AI performance claim. A benchmark's human-time horizon is not the agent's elapsed run time or a universal limit on organizational work. [METR's account of its time-horizon measurements](https://metr.org/time-horizons/) explains that boundary. Refresh the particular premise when the model, tool or receiving task changes; a later publication date alone does not qualify the new use.

#### Library.Reference:9.3.11 - Obtain the supplying Methods at the point of need

| Needed result | Public supplier | What returns to this account |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Select a configuration of performance and provision; develop the way, holder and provision together | [FPF Core, C.32.MWA.PP and C.40.JD](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md) | A complete comparison or development construction, including shifted burden and joint conditions |
| Construct help for establishing, using or revising a practice | [Implementation Facilitation, IS.1–IS.3](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/IMPLEMENTATION-FACILITATION-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) | An undertaking, response to the actual difficulty and obtainable support strategy |
| Obtain a legitimate, feasible role contribution | [Leadership, LDR.1](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/LEADERSHIP-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) | Understood participation and its conditions, including unresolved refusal or unavailable provision |
| Diagnose and develop human capability, support and transfer | [Human Capability Development](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Human%20Capability%20Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/HUMAN-CAPABILITY-DEVELOPMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md), especially HCD.2, HCD.7–HCD.8, HCD.25–HCD.26 and HCD.28 at their stated scopes | An appropriate development arrangement and evidence about the particular human result |
| Diagnose and change an agent's available execution | [Systems Engineering, SYSE.46 and its related agent Methods](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/SYSTEMS-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) | The implicated mechanism, a selected engineering change and qualified subsequent performance |
| Supply selective, maintained memory | [Knowledge Corpus and Access Engineering, KCAE.MEMORY](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/KNOWLEDGE-CORPUS-ACCESS-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) | A warranted memory contribution with its access, revision and use conditions |
| Recover interrupted purposeful work | [FPF Core, A.15.10](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md) | The actual assignment, performed effects, justified continuation and unresolved state |
| Establish mutual understanding and permissible continuation | [Engineering Architectural Reference, 19.6 and 21.3](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/ENGINEERING-DPF-SUITE-REFERENCE.md) | A repaired interpretation or an explicit dispute and its authorized continuation |
| Reconstruct a repertoire and judge a cultural change | [FPF Core, C.36.RP and C.36.MC](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/FPF-Spec.md) | A bounded supported cultural claim, useful retained contribution and continuation or precise uncertainty |
| Establish continuing organizational and community conditions | [Management DPF Suite](https://github.com/ailev/FPF/blob/main/DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/MANAGEMENT-DPF-SUITE-REFERENCE.md), with the organization-change and community Methods | The arrangement and commitments needed for the practice to remain obtainable |

These suppliers retain their independently useful problems and results. The connections explained here concern result use, shared conditions, different holder mechanisms, changes of allocation and selective returns. They do not make every engagement one composite Method or every professional field part of a new discipline.

The practitioner should finish with a way that the relevant participants can use, question and renew: appropriate help, actual acquisition or available execution where required, shared interpretation and obligations, and a justified cultural continuation. A precise unmet condition remains a useful result.

### Library.Reference:9.4 - From a thinking repertoire to continuing development

A team can master delivery and still lack a way to decide what becomes worth doing next. A practitioner can understand a worked example yet be unable to choose the operation that an unfamiliar case needs. The connection explained here is how a repertoire of thinking operations contributes to creating a way, carrying it out, learning to use it and continuing from the resulting position.

The constructive entry is already developed in [section 7.2](#libraryreference72---from-available-information-to-useful-questions-and-possible-actions) and B.5:4.1.2. Examine accessible material, recover participants and relations, derive a consequence and, where it is needed, use a consequential contrast. Work out which questions or actions the revised account makes possible and which result, if any, it already supplies for the question that brought the reader here. The material may be a successful episode, an artifact, a source argument or a capability whose possible uses are not yet understood. A useful distinction can be obtained before a complete problem or a set of alternatives is available.

[C.39](FPF-Spec.md#c39---find-and-develop-a-way-to-obtain-a-result) develops the needed way and its connections. C.40.CD permits the problem and the way to change together; C.40.OD and C.40.JD connect the resulting Method, performers and provision; C.40.NP constructs a further worthwhile problem from the changed position. The following account shows how concrete reasoning contributions enter those Methods. The operations have different outputs and can be used separately. Performing all of them is not a condition of a good answer.

#### Library.Reference:9.4.1 - Choose the transformation by the difference it must make

When the unresolved contribution is already recognizable, begin there. When it is not, use the accessible material and constructive entry in section 7.2 to obtain a distinction or question first. A conclusion may lack a premise; a representation may merge cases that require different answers; a plausible way may lack a necessary contribution; or a person may need practice with an otherwise adequate way. These difficulties can coexist. Inspect enough of the actual material to distinguish them, use an adequate existing result directly, and construct the missing contribution through the appropriate operation.

**Recover what is being claimed and why.** A statement such as “the customer rarely opens the application, so the operators need training” contains an observation claim, an explanation and a recommendation. Recover what was counted, whose use matters and how inability is supposed to explain that use. The same observation may be compatible with the application supplying no needed result. A further premise is needed before the recommendation follows.

[B.5.RA:4.4a](https://fpf.tools/B.5.RA) develops this reconstruction from prose and dialogue: recover the question and claims, retain consequential alternative readings, work backward from the conclusion and forward from the offered reasons, and locate the unsupported inference. A source-supported implicit premise, a premise proposed by the analyst and an unanswered question have different standing. Return a conclusion whose force fits its grounds. If the argument is already clear, examine its consequential premise directly; an argument map is useful when the relations otherwise become difficult to inspect.

**Recover the result behind a proposed means.** When someone asks to “install a planning tool”, establish what they have commissioned. They may need a maintenance window that can be agreed without disrupting production, the installation itself, or both. If they have left the means open, a conversation with the authorized planner, a different representation, a specialist service and a new application are alternatives to examine against the same conditions. If the installation is due, the planning need can guide its acceptance or support a proposal to revise the order. A workable window can already settle the scheduling decision.

[PSDA.6 and PSDA.5](DPFs/DECISION-DESIGN-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) connect ends, consequences and construction of alternatives. Work from the needed contribution toward possible means, then forward from each serious means to what it would supply. Change mechanism, allocation, timing, scale or commitment where that changes the result. Several names for the same unavailable contribution do not create alternatives. When the choice of means is open, use an available one that meets the commission if the recipient needs no further comparison.

**Construct a distinction that the current account cannot express.** Two maintenance orders both say “ready.” In one, the proposed window is authorized; in the other, it is awaiting the production manager's decision. The two situations require different next actions although the displayed value is the same. Preserve the two situations and define the relation that differs: a proposal, the authority to decide it and the actual decision. Then try the revised description against the questions that the receiver must answer.

[SIE.3](DPFs/Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/SEMANTIC-INTEGRATION-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) supplies this construction: state the required answer distinctions, try an adequate existing model, locate the missing relation and develop its meaning before choosing an encoding. The result can be a short maintained description. The warrant for adding “authorized window” is the difference in permitted action, not the novelty of the term. A new table field helps only if its value has a defined source and interpretation.

**Connect the intended result backward and run the available material forward.** To confirm a maintenance window, the planner may need a compatible production interval, qualified staff, required equipment and a decision by the responsible party. Recover which contributions are jointly necessary, which are alternatives and which merely improve the prospect. Trace their inputs until they reach what is available. Then attempt the construction forward under those conditions.

[PSDA.8:4.2.1 and PSDA.20](DPFs/DECISION-DESIGN-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) develop this two-direction reasoning. It can create a combination that no participant initially proposed. It also exposes the first unsupported join: a valid calculation may need unavailable input; two locally feasible jobs may require the same specialist at the same time. Repair that join or choose a qualified smaller result. A small direct explanation is enough when the dependencies remain clear without a network.

**Reconsider the premises that make an apparent conflict unavoidable.** “Production must continue, and maintenance requires a shutdown” may concern the same machine, or it may confuse one machine's operation with the required production service. Recover the actual requirements and the inference that makes them incompatible. A spare line, a different interval or another maintenance method is useful only if it supplies the required service under its own technical and organizational conditions.

[B.5.QD.CF](https://fpf.tools/B.5.QD.CF) distinguishes a physical limit, a binding obligation, a selected arrangement and an unsupported assumption. Change a permissible premise and follow the consequences through all the requirements that remain. Do not silently weaken “required output” to “some output,” or infer permission to change a commitment from technical feasibility. The result may be an attainable alternative, a narrower claim or a well-grounded obstruction.

**Obtain an observation that can resolve a live difference.** If poor use could be explained by an unavailable decision, missing information or an unusable interface, inspect what each explanation predicts in a relevant episode. A record showing that the information was understood and used but the decision remained unavailable bears differently on those explanations than a count of logins. Use an existing adequate observation when available. Construct another comparison only when the remaining difference matters.

[B.5.2](https://fpf.tools/B.5.2) supplies explanatory abduction. [MMP.16](DPFs/Foundational%20Thinking%20DPF%20Suite/MATHEMATICAL-MODELING-PRACTICE-DPF.md) explains how to derive a discriminating observable while retaining measurement, selection and auxiliary conditions. Rival models can disagree about an unobserved quantity yet predict the same available readout. If the intended claim is instead that changing the interface causes an improvement, MMP.15 requires the causal grounds for that intervention and target. A revealing episode or a successful assisted attempt can be useful without establishing that causal effect.

**Change a consequential property and derive the knowledge the new construction needs.** Suppose a planner asks for a way to obtain an agreed window without several people repeatedly re-entering the same facts. Vary that property of the proposed way, rather than requesting more features of the familiar tool. A candidate could reuse existing source records and return a proposal to the authorized parties. Work through what would have to be true: which record identifies the relevant resource, whose information is current, which conditions can be checked automatically and who can make the remaining decision?

C.39 and [C.40.CD](FPF-Spec.md#c40cd---develop-problems-and-ways-of-solving-them-together) connect this variation to the missing operations or knowledge. An existing Method may supply the new construction; an unanswered technical question may require inquiry. If the proposed property cannot be obtained with available means, revise the property, obtain another contribution or retain the limitation.

**Reopen the criterion when the consequence it was meant to represent changes.** A short unplanned interruption and a longer agreed maintenance stop can have different effects on a customer's production commitments. “Minimize downtime” may no longer capture the receiving use. Recover whose consequence matters, over which horizon, with what costs and protected conditions. Distinguish a mistaken factual forecast from a changed priority or a disagreement over who may make the tradeoff.

PSDA.6 connects these questions to comparison. Where the parties have not authorized a tradeoff, an analyst's weighted score cannot make it legitimate. A partial ordering or an explicit unresolved preference may be the correct result. Preserve an earlier finding about interruption duration under its original meaning; changing the criterion changes its role in the decision, not the observed duration.

**Turn a successful construction into a reusable operation.** Recover which moves made the result possible, which inputs and conditions they needed, and what the performer supplied without recording it. “Ask Mira” may hide access to a production schedule, authority to agree a change and skill in finding an alternative. Generalize the relevant relations and actions, rather than replacing Mira with a variable while retaining unexplained dependence on her.

[C.39.RO](https://fpf.tools/C.39.RO) develops that generalization. Identify the variable material, retained premises and result, then establish why the operation works over the required variation. Examine a changed use if it can distinguish an unresolved assumption or a demonstration was requested. The next site may have the same scheduling facts but another authority arrangement. Its difference shows which part of the operation transfers and which needs a new contribution. A successful episode supports what occurred there; the grounds for a wider use may be a general argument, qualified evidence or further work under the subject Method. Another person's independent execution is a further capability claim.

These operations can return into one another. Recovering an argument can expose a missing distinction; that distinction can enable a new option; trying the option can reveal a criterion that the original question omitted. Choose the next contribution from what remains unresolved. Repeating an available explanation will not supply permission, and creating an experiment will not settle a disagreement about legitimate priorities.

#### Library.Reference:9.4.2 - A complete case beginning with successful delivery

Consider a constructed case. A software supplier has made weekly releases dependable. The deployment and recovery procedures meet their stated technical conditions. The supplier and a customer can now explore whether timely production information could support a more widely usable maintenance-planning service. The current planning practice continues to meet its existing commitments. The next inquiry asks what the new delivery capability makes worth attempting, without declaring the earlier work a failure. Faster release is one possible means; it is not yet the new customer result.

A product analyst follows one use of the planning report with the customer's planner in this constructed episode. The planner understands the report, then makes calls to find out whether a displayed “ready” order has an agreed window. The observation establishes the acts and information used in that episode. It weakens a recommendation to train this planner merely to read the report; it does not establish why every customer uses the product as they do.

The analyst compares two orders that receive the same displayed status. One has an authorized production window and qualified crew; the other has a proposed window and no decision yet. The current status cannot answer whether the job may be committed. The analyst and planner replace the single status, for this question, with the relations among the proposed work, resource availability and the actual decision. They keep “not yet known,” “declined” and “agreed” distinct. Existing order identifiers and technical maintenance requirements remain useful.

The receiving result can now be stated more precisely: obtain a feasible maintenance proposal and the decisions required to commit it. Working backward identifies the maintenance requirements, production commitments, resource intervals and responsible decision. Working forward discovers that two proposed jobs require the same specialist at overlapping times. Neither individual approval of a machine interval nor another software field supplies the missing specialist capacity.

The participants construct a different way. An existing source supplies the resource intervals; a planner checks the proposed combination; the relevant production representative considers its effect on commitments; the person with the applicable power agrees or declines the proposed window. A preparation view can expose the undecided conditions before that conversation. The useful product is the intelligible proposal and its supported disposition. The view's output is not itself the authorization.

For example, suppose the qualified inputs give one crew from 09:00 to 11:00. Job A requires that crew from 09:00 to 10:00. Job B also requires one hour and can fit within the production interval 09:30–11:00. These durations include the required setup and movement, and the other needed technical conditions are met. Proposing B at 09:30 conflicts with A; constructing the sequence A at 09:00 and B at 10:00 removes that resource conflict. The calculation creates a feasible proposal under the supplied conditions. The responsible parties still have to agree its use; if B actually required ninety minutes, the same sequence would no longer fit.

They compare this arrangement with continuing the existing calls and with automating more of the calculation. For the immediate receiving use, the existing calculation and a qualified planner may suffice. A new automatic scheduler is worth developing only if its attainable contribution justifies construction, qualification, adoption and maintenance. Missing reliable resource information would defeat either interface. A calculation that accepts qualified intervals but leaves authorization to the responsible party can be a useful partial improvement.

Suppose the agreed bounded attempt is carried out. The participants obtain a compatible window, issue the required decisions and use it for the maintenance job. That establishes the performed contribution under those conditions. It does not show that the interface alone caused the result, that every planner can obtain it or that every site's rules are the same. A report of the attempt preserves which facts, help and decisions it used.

A changed condition selects a different continuation. At another site, the same technical combination is feasible, but the proposed window would violate an existing production commitment and no authorized change is available. The result is a proposal that cannot currently be committed. The original scheduling calculation remains useful. The needed continuation is a different feasible arrangement or an authorized change of commitment; extra practice reading the view cannot supply either.

Now suppose the supplier also wants planners to handle future unfamiliar cases independently. The experienced planner's success does not finish that undertaking. The learning task is to identify the receiving decision, recover its required conditions and recognize when the available account cannot answer it. The learner first works a suitable case under the permitted support; the coach addresses the consequential missing relation; a changed case tests the operation under that change. Immediate customer work can continue with a competent planner while the separate learning work proceeds.

The company also has to decide who will provide and maintain this capability. If the same experienced planner must meet current customer commitments, coach colleagues and qualify every new site, those demands must be made feasible together. Narrowing the initial promise, sequencing contributions or obtaining another qualified performer can make the whole attainable. The choice changes the arrangement, not the meaning of the desired capability.

The original undertaking produced a feasible window, the decisions to commit it and a maintenance job that used it. If that was the whole order, it is complete. The later undertakings add two questions. Can another planner work out what to do when a relevant condition changes? Can the company provide that capability alongside current customer work? The first requires grounds in the planner’s own work under the promised conditions. The second requires a feasible provision arrangement, with commitments and actual service where those have been promised. One successful maintenance job leaves both questions open.
