**An inquiry whose result is mathematical knowledge.** Consider a different constructed question: when can two operations be reordered without changing their result? First identify the allowed inputs and the result that must be preserved. If the original order must work for every input in that domain, the reversed order must also be defined there and give the required outcome at each input. A match at one input can settle the whole domain if a known property carries equality to every other input. The affine case below has this property. For an unrestricted pair, equality at one input leaves the others open. For f(x) = x + 1 and g(x) = 2x, following both orders gives f(g(x)) = 2x + 1 and g(f(x)) = 2x + 2. A description that counts the operations but omits their order loses a consequential relation.

To derive a usable test for a particular family, restrict the two operations to affine functions on the real numbers. Write f(x) = ax + b and g(x) = cx + d. Applying g first and f second gives f(g(x)) = acx + ad + b. Applying f first and g second gives g(f(x)) = cax + cb + d. The slopes agree because ac = ca. The difference between the two results is constant, so exact agreement at any one input establishes agreement at all inputs. Equating the constant terms gives the test (a − 1)d = (c − 1)b. This derivation constructs a reusable comparison under the stated algebraic premises. Two translations satisfy it; adding one and then doubling does not commute with the reversed order. No observational experiment is needed to establish this algebraic result.

The coefficient test answers the question for every pair of real affine functions. For a pair outside that family, return to the original criterion of definedness and agreement on the required inputs. If the commission also calls for an effective test for a wider family, that part still requires work. If the purpose is a student's ability to construct such a representation, supplying the formula completes only the explanation; the student's constructive work remains to be developed. The example therefore uses the same distinctions among inquiry, result and learning without a business application or organizational permission problem.

#### Library.Reference:9.4.3 - Make the operation available to a person or a human–AI arrangement

To teach a repertoire, choose an operation inside a meaningful task and identify what the person must eventually do with it. Recognizing a supplied distinction, constructing it, choosing when to use it and transferring it are different targets. A learner who can repeat the authorized-window example may still treat “ready” as sufficient in another practice. The learning target is the consequential relation and its use, not the vocabulary alone.

[HCD.9](DPFs/Human%20Capability%20Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/HUMAN-CAPABILITY-DEVELOPMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) explains how to work on that operation. When useful and safe, obtain the learner's own attempt before a complete answer removes the opportunity. A novice may need a worked example with the decisive inference explained; an experienced practitioner may need help only at the point their account fails. Ask the learner to perform the move on their material, compare the result with the criterion, explain what needs changing and return the correction to the whole task.

Keep the feedback specific. “Think more systematically” leaves the missing action unwritten. “You used an available crew as a reason to promise the date; which contribution establishes permission to move the production commitment?” identifies a premise and its use. The learner can then recover the governing decision or qualify the proposal. If they already recognize this distinction and need access to the decision, further prompting is misplaced.

[HCD.12](DPFs/Human%20Capability%20Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/HUMAN-CAPABILITY-DEVELOPMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) supplies consequential variation. Preserve enough of the task to interpret performance, and vary a condition that could change the choice or its grounds, such as authorization or a resource conflict. Let the learner determine whether the earlier choice still works. When independent selection is the target, announcing the required Method supplies part of the answer being assessed. Restore assistance when it serves learning or protects the work; reducing help on a fixed timetable is not the target.

Return the operation to later work through HCD.15. A rehearsal can show what happened in the rehearsal. Retention requires a later occasion with interpretable conditions. If both the delay and the available assistance change, a poorer result does not identify which change mattered. Use existing work traces when they answer the question; arrange additional observations only for the claim that still needs them. Understanding a Method, retaining it and obtaining an improved work result need different evidence.

A human–AI arrangement can be the intended competent performer. If the person's target is critical use of an AI proposal, let them inspect a proposal, recover its grounds and decide whether it fits the case. If the target is their own construction without that proposal, observe the relevant attempt before supplying it. Neither target is universally preferable. The promise to the learner, employer or client determines which result is needed.

For the AI contribution, identify the available model, instructions, sources, tools and retained state that produce the result. A prompt repair or new reference can make later execution available without changing model parameters. Try the receiving operation in the configuration in which it will be used, including a restart or transfer when that persistence is claimed. A plausible answer in the coached interaction cannot stand in for the later result. [Section 9.3](#libraryreference93---acquiring-sustaining-and-changing-a-shared-humanai-professional-culture) develops these distinct human, agent and shared-cultural claims.

#### Library.Reference:9.4.4 - Join different representations and make inquiry feasible in the company

Team reasoning begins with the contributions that must fit. Sales may use “customer” for a buyer, service for the person affected by an interruption and product development for a user of the application. Recover those participants and the relations among them through a concrete commitment or episode. Determine which person's consequence a proposed result would improve and which other obligations it affects. Agreement on one word can conceal the disagreement.

Use the repertoire at the disputed connection. A factual dispute calls for grounds about what obtains. A meaning dispute needs cases and the interpretation that changes the answer. A priority dispute needs a legitimate comparison of consequences. A dispute over who may decide needs the applicable agreement or authority. One meeting can contain all four. A jointly accepted diagram does not make their answers interchangeable.

For a proposed change, work through the contribution crossing each organizational boundary: what arrives, what the receiver does with it, what leaves, under which conditions and how an exception returns. [OCE.3 and OCE.9](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/ORGANIZATION-CHANGE-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) connect this reasoning to allocation and implementation. An adequate existing way can be used directly. Where the needed way is unknown, give a bounded inquiry a recipient and usable material; execution cannot yet be fully specified.

In the case, the planner can start by examining an order and its use before promising a new product or a quantified business benefit. The inquiry's attainable result is a distinction or a proposed way that changes the next decision. A small allocation of access and expert attention can be justified by that contribution. Scientific inquiry may instead be justified by the knowledge sought, with no current product commitment. In either case, compare its full burden with continuing on the present basis through C.11.DUA.

Provision determines whether the question can be pursued. A responsible recipient must be able to receive an unresolved result; someone must be available for a needed answer or an alternative source must suffice. Protect ongoing work when attention is diverted. [OCE.11](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/ORGANIZATION-CHANGE-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) compares shared demand, including learning, coordination, checking and maintenance. An unfunded inquiry can leave a problem unresolved; its lack of funding does not establish that the present way is adequate.

Participation needs a similar diagnosis. A person may lack the operation, lack permission to use it, disagree with its purpose or reasonably protect another commitment. [OCE.10](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/ORGANIZATION-CHANGE-ENGINEERING-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) connects the relevant response to that difference. To credit learning, establish how the person’s performance changed with the relevant help. The governing rule may make a qualifying assessment effective as a permission, or require a separate grant. Establish any agreement the work still needs and whether the person can actually perform the authorized action.

#### Library.Reference:9.4.5 - Carry the contribution across organizations without inventing one common actor

A supplier, customer, professional community and research group can benefit from the same Method while having different ends and powers. Begin with the proposed contributions and their uses on each side. Who supplies a case, access, interpretation, competent work or maintenance? What does each participant obtain, and which conditions would make the arrangement unacceptable? A community does not acquire a company's authority merely because its members share a repertoire.

In a continuation of the constructed case, several customers would benefit from learning how apparently compatible resource plans become unfulfillable commitments. A research group wants to understand the different failure mechanisms. A software supplier wants a reusable planning service. One customer can share a reconstructed relation and a worked counterexample but cannot release operational records. Another wants paid analysis and has no reason to provide volunteer maintenance. These are different viable contributions, not evidence of defective commitment to a single common purpose.

[CB.15](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/COMMUNITY-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) develops the joint arrangement. Specify what can be supplied and received, how meanings will be interpreted and which decisions each party has to make, and which returns the parties need under the arrangement. Compare a paid service, a limited research collaboration and a continuing shared repertoire against their different obligations. Preserve powers and commitments that have not been delegated. If the necessary data cannot lawfully be supplied, an anonymized label alone does not make the transfer permissible.

A smaller arrangement can still be useful. The first customer can contribute a constructed counterexample whose relation they are entitled to disclose. A local competent person may perform a check that cannot be delegated with raw data. The research claim must then fit the evidence available. A general description of a possible failure and an empirical estimate of its frequency require different grounds. Do not silently promise the latter through the former.

[CB.14](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/COMMUNITY-MANAGEMENT-PRINCIPLES-FRAMEWORK.md) turns a useful contribution into an obtainable repertoire: preserve the operation, the reason it works, the cases that expose its boundary and responsibility for a change that consumers depend on. Another participant tries the contribution on their own problem and returns a relevant difference. Retain a local variant when its conditions justify it; common terminology is not a reason to erase it. A shared office or permanent community is one possible provision arrangement, not a prerequisite to cultural transmission.

#### Library.Reference:9.4.6 - Use success to construct the next problem and develop the means of development

After a result, recover what is now different: a known relation, an available operation, a capable performer, a usable resource or an agreement. Ask what this permits that the previous position did not. Follow the relation to a possible use; change an input, desired result, condition or connection and derive what would be needed. This is C.40.NP's constructive continuation, not a demand to make each project larger.

The planning service can now coordinate one site's maintenance windows. That success makes it possible to compare different kinds of interruption with the customer. Suppose the customer reveals that a longer agreed stop is preferable to unpredictable short stops because the former permits production commitments to be rearranged. The service team can now formulate a different problem. To rearrange production, the people making production commitments need dependable notice in time to act. A fixed window can meet that need. If a committed window may move, establish who may change it and how production will hear in time. In this case, the service team and the customer's planner agree on the notice production needs and use it to schedule the next window at this site. Their earlier measure of total downtime remains valid. This establishes the notice agreed for that site and its use in scheduling one window. To decide who may move a window already committed to production, consult the applicable mandate. To establish that such a move occurred, relate it to the decision and its effect. A claim about predictable notice for later windows needs grounds covering those windows and the conditions under which notice reaches production.

A different continuation concerns new carriers. The first planner can obtain an agreed window, but the next planner only copies the example's steps. To develop continuing judgement, recover how the first planner noticed the missing decision and selected the next contribution. Give the next planner a materially different situation in which the missing contribution is not announced. Their justified choice and use of an operation can support that capability claim. Requiring them to reproduce the original answer cannot.

This also exposes a problem in the means of development. If all later cases still require the first expert to formulate the question, writing more task instructions may leave the bottleneck untouched. The expert can construct a useful contrast, explain why the two cases require different continuations and help the next person practise that choice. A better explanation, acquired judgement and an available support arrangement are distinct changes. Qualify whichever the undertaking promises.

For a team, the new ability may be to locate and repair a boundary between members' contributions. For a company, it may be to receive an initially unclear question, obtain a bounded competent inquiry and make its result usable without suspending current obligations. For a professional environment, it may be to retain and revise that operation across changing people and organizations. Identify the organizing relations for the holder and conditions claimed. One end-to-end performance can show how a team and its company supplied their respective contributions on that occasion. Continued capability or wider reach calls for grounds that cover it.

Cultural continuation is visible when another carrier can recover the reason, use the operation under appropriate conditions, recognize a consequential limit and obtain or construct the next contribution. A manual, a successful event and broad dissemination can help make that possible; they do not by themselves establish it. Keep the operation and its qualifications available at the next real occasion, along with a feasible way to return a correction. The next carrier may preserve the Method, develop a variant or establish that it no longer fits. Return the changed explanation, decisive cases and limits to those who will use or teach it next; retaining only the latest answer would lose the means of further renewal.

The current undertaking can finish. Consolidation, rest, use of an external service or stopping an exhausted inquiry can be the warranted continuation. A culture of development preserves the ability and conditions to form another worthwhile problem when it arises.

#### Library.Reference:9.4.7 - Choose the depth of further development by what remains missing

The resulting repertoire can be enough for the immediate work. Explain the chosen operation, why the situation selects it, what it returned and how that result changes the receiving use. Keep a consequential unresolved condition with the conclusion that depends on it. A competent specialist can supply a result when independent mastery is not promised. Where mastery is promised, its learning work remains part of the undertaking.

A request for a named technique can have a different scope. Learning C–K theory, conducting a Change Laboratory intervention or applying a particular interval HPM model requires the corresponding Method's constructions and qualification; the ordinary operations above do not claim to reproduce those whole approaches. Conversely, their names do not establish that this work needs them. Locate the additional operation or knowledge the chosen approach would supply, compare adequate existing means, and account for learning, competent participation, implementation and later maintenance before commissioning its development.

A theoretical question can justify such inquiry on its scientific merits. Establish an attainable knowledge contribution and the means to investigate it without inventing a product benefit. For a current practical undertaking, preserve the adequate result while an independent research question proceeds.

## Library.Reference:10 - What justifies another pattern, DPF, Suite or LPF

The Library's boundaries follow useful constructions and their subjects. A new topic or an awkward folder location is a reason to inspect the architecture, not a sufficient reason for another framework.

[FPF E.4.DPF](FPF-Spec.md#e4dpf---domain-and-local-principle-frameworks-whether-and-how-to-author-and-publish) and [FPF E.4.PFAD](FPF-Spec.md#e4pfad---principle-framework-architecture-decision) compare the recurring problems, available full Methods, their connections and the independently useful result. That comparison can retain an adequate supplier, improve its explanation, add a missing Method, regroup a developed field or identify another field. Existing elementary operations do not prevent a new composition; a useful summary does not establish one.

| Proposed contribution | What gives it the corresponding boundary |
| --- | --- |
| A new or revised pattern | A recurring problem and reproducible construction with its own useful result, conditions and cases; an existing owner is preferable when the addition completes that owner's promised result. |
| A DPF | A coherent field account of its entities, relations and operations; how the practice obtains, tests and uses knowledge; and how its Methods are constructed, selected, connected and revised. Where the practice creates new units, representations or operations, the account explains that construction. Recurring problems and independently useful results justify the boundary. |
| A DPF Suite | A composite discipline established through C.20, constituent DPFs and developed whole connections; more patterns or more cited suppliers do not supply that construction. |
| An LPF | Guidance for a bounded local practice, such as an organization, project, tool arrangement or practitioner setting, using applicable Core and domain contributions under its local conditions. |
| An Architectural Reference | A needed public account of an existing whole, its architecture, alternatives, connections and returns. The underlying Methods remain developed at their substantive owners. |
| A pedagogical companion or tool | Learning support or an operational means of access, calculation or checking with its own intended use and limits. |

An LPF can select local responsibilities, tools and working arrangements without turning those choices into universal rules. A domain framework can receive an existing Method or split into better field descriptions; novelty of every operation is not the test of its identity. A proposed Suite must explain the larger field's knowledge-practice coupling and its continuity through permissible changes.

The consequential comparison is what each boundary changes in obtaining, understanding, adapting and maintaining a useful result. Shared content can reduce duplicate explanation while increasing reliance on access and compatible sources. Independent variants can preserve necessary differences at the cost of maintaining them. A new framework is warranted when its field and useful whole justify that burden.

## Library.Reference:11 - Publications, access and changes of boundary

The public arrangement gives Core its full home in [FPF-Spec.md](FPF-Spec.md). The [DPFs catalogue](DPFs/) locates four Suite folders and ten standalone DPFs. Engineering has five direct constituent descriptions, Management fourteen, Foundational Thinking five and Human Capability Engineering two: together with the ten standalone languages, these are thirty-six distinct DPF descriptions. Each Suite has its own entry and Architectural Reference and identifies every full description it includes. This root Reference carries the Library-wide explanation. Each complete supplying text has an identifiable public home; this arrangement is a publication view of the richer relations described above.

For connected use, identify the contribution each source must supply. A practitioner can use a method they already command under the agreed conditions. An accessible explanation becomes necessary when they need to recover how to proceed, check a source-dependent premise or supply that explanation to the recipient. An authorized result from a specialist can instead supply a premise when its grounds cover the objects, conditions and decision at hand. Check any shared conditions before combining several results. A commission to inspect or deliver a particular edition still calls for that edition. A Suite directory can draw on Core, specialized Suites and outside suppliers. Follow a link when it supplies an explanation or evidence the recipient needs, using [Using FPF Library](USING-FPF.md) to locate it. If a required contribution remains unavailable, identify the particular conclusion or delivery it leaves open.

The README provides a short entry. The whole-language account explains the framework's subject, construction, relations and alternatives. That account can be embedded in the same publication as its patterns or carried separately; its explanatory responsibility is the same. Each publication's table of contents locates its whole-language account. Full pattern bodies supply separately selectable Methods. A reader may enter at any of these levels when the needed conditions remain recoverable.

Current use and a reproducible comparison also have different needs. For ordinary application, establish that the method available to the performer fits the conditions of use. Obtain the current source when the work depends on what that edition prescribes. A comparison between editions needs the identified sources whose changes could affect the conclusion. Publication date, a stable filename and a successful retrieval are not substitutes for those content conditions.

When a framework boundary changes, preserve the complete useful constructions and return to their supplying bodies. Distinguish a corrected description of the field from a change to the field itself, and both from a layout or access change. [FPF E.4.PFIP](FPF-Spec.md#e4pfip---principle-framework-publication-integration-and-preservation) governs preservation across reconstructed publications; [FPF E.4.PFR](FPF-Spec.md#e4pfr---pattern-framework-relation-and-edition-discipline) handles relied-on edition relations. A reference to a successor title is insufficient if the operation, conditions or receiving use have disappeared.

The affected answer is reopened where its premise changed. A revised mathematical assumption can change a comparison while leaving the authority arrangement intact; an unavailable provider can change realization without defeating retained knowledge. [FPF C.39.MC](FPF-Spec.md#c39mc---continue-method-development-from-changed-conditions) develops methodological continuation from changed conditions, and [FPF G.11](FPF-Spec.md#g11---decide-whether-and-how-to-refresh-sota-packs-and-related-results-telemetry-and-decay) supplies the source-currentness question when reliance requires it.

### Library.Reference:11.1 - Document identities and current carriers

The following catalogue records the DocID declared by each complete framework or standalone Architectural Reference in **FPF Library**, its full document title and its current carrier. The title and DocID identify the document in this publication set; the carrier is where this distribution makes it available. DocIDs do not name editions, embedded units or discipline membership. Short navigation and usage publications retain their full titles instead of receiving additional DocIDs.

Refer to a document or contribution in ordinary prose, adding only the context needed to distinguish it. Within FPF Library, HCD.12:4.1 and Library.Reference:9.1 already identify their declared documents as well as their sections. Keep each complete address; add a DocID or full document title when the document would otherwise be unclear. A whole-document reference names the document. A reference to an unnumbered unit includes that unit's title and enough document context. Name a selected edition separately when its exact content matters. [Using FPF Library](USING-FPF.md) explains resolution through the access available to the reader.

If a carrier changes, retain the document declaration and local addresses and update the carrier link. If a title or code becomes ambiguous, use the full document title and publication set until an explicit declaration or maintained reader return resolves the ambiguity.

## Library.Reference:12 - The Library and its wider ecosystem

FPF Library is the project's publication collection. The wider FPF ecosystem also includes other authors' domain and local frameworks, teaching materials, tools, services and the people and organizations that use and maintain them. A common conceptual dependency does not put all of these products inside the Library or give them one publication or maintenance responsibility.

This distinction lets the public Methods remain complete without making every useful learning arrangement part of the Library. A pedagogical companion can sequence practice, give feedback or adapt material for an audience. Tooling can retrieve a passage, compute a result or perform a declared check. Those contributions need their own capabilities and evidence; the fact that they cite FPF Library publications does not establish them. The Library's Reference explains its architecture and connected language, while learning programmes and operational tools retain their own purposes.

Public architectural reasons belong where they help readers understand and adapt the language: why a boundary exists, which serious alternative it excludes, what a dependency supplies and which condition would reopen the choice. Dated authoring decisions, review correspondence and release records serve other readers. [FPF E.11.PFP:4.4](FPF-Spec.md#e11pfp44---keep-support-units-and-adjacent-products-distinct) distinguishes those functions without hiding the useful rationale in internal records.

The resulting architecture offers common meanings and reusable general Methods, specialized constructive knowledge and explicit ways to connect them. It also leaves meaningful limits visible: field coverage can be incomplete; source grounds can be disputed or dated; a necessary supplier, capability or authority can be absent. A sufficient answer can be used directly. An unresolved contribution remains a work question.

Original content is published under the terms stated in [LICENSE](LICENSE) and [Licensing FPF Library](LICENSING.md). For a specific Method or architectural claim, cite the supplying publication, its edition and PatternID or section address. Cite this account as *FPF Library Architectural Reference*, using the date at its entry and a `Library.Reference` section when needed.

## Library.Reference:13 - What grounds the architecture and its methods

The division explained in sections 3–6 is an architectural synthesis of this Library. It preserves general constructions in Core, the different subjects and warrants of domain Methods, and the connections that make a Suite useful. The alternatives are consequential: one large framework would still need those distinctions; an unstructured collection would leave readers to reconstruct its shared conditions. These reasons support the selected publication and language architecture. They are not a finding that one universal disciplinary taxonomy has been empirically proved best.

The source question changes with the claim. A professional association can help identify a field without showing that its usual method is the strongest available answer. An experiment can support a method under particular conditions without establishing a whole Suite’s effectiveness. A new tool can change the practical division of work without changing the discipline of the result it supplies. The Library keeps these claims separate so that readers can examine the premise that matters to their use.

| Architectural question | Where the grounds and alternatives are explained |
| --- | --- |
| Why connect these engineering contributions while keeping organizational and human specializations distinct? | [Engineering.Reference:0](DPFs/Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/ENGINEERING-DPF-SUITE-REFERENCE.md#engineeringreference0---engineering-specialization-and-maintenance) explains the subjects and connections; the supplying DPFs give the technical method comparisons. |
| How do changes in human and AI work affect management without making source prestige the test of a method? | [Management.Reference:8](DPFs/Management%20DPF%20Suite/MANAGEMENT-DPF-SUITE-REFERENCE.md#managementreference8---field-basis-and-further-reading) separates field recognition from comparisons of arrangements and their observed consequences. |
| Why distinguish mathematical, physical, modeling, computational and notational contributions? | [Foundational.Reference:6–Foundational.Reference:7](DPFs/Foundational%20Thinking%20DPF%20Suite/FOUNDATIONAL-THINKING-DPF-SUITE-REFERENCE.md#foundationalreference6---architectural-rationale) compares alternative wholes and explains the adopted source contributions, including their limits. |
| Why distinguish changing a person’s capability from obtaining an answer through assistance? | [HCE.Reference:8](DPFs/Human%20Capability%20Engineering%20DPF%20Suite/HUMAN-CAPABILITY-ENGINEERING-DPF-SUITE-REFERENCE.md#hcereference8---what-grounds-the-subject-and-the-name) connects that boundary to contrasting learning evidence and the full HCD and SOM source accounts. |

For example, [Vaccaro, Almaatouq and Malone’s 2024 meta-analysis](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41562-024-02024-1) distinguishes improvement over a human alone from improvement over the better of human and AI alone. The distinction changes the arrangement comparison in OCE.8: adding a performer is not presumed beneficial. It does not establish the best organization for every activity. The relevant whole still has to obtain its intended result under its costs and conditions.

An affected Method’s source comparison states the working question, competing answers, useful difference, limits and conditions for reconsideration. The account at the next assembly level explains what that difference changes in their combination. This is the role of SoTA here: a defensible answer to a particular question, open to a stronger rival or counterexample. It is not a single rank attached to every publication in the Library. A missing comparison remains a limitation on the affected claim even when the rest of the language is usable.

## Library.Reference:End
